Therefore, there is no point in worshipping these deities (Sri Murtis) or Lord Visnu.” They cannot comprehend that these atheists or hypocrites cannot even see the true substance (satya-vastu), let alone touch it. The illusory energy of the Lord, Maya, takes on the guise of substantial reality and deludes them.

A magician may create the illusion of making a gold coin from dust in the presence of his audience. Although ordinary people may examine the gold coin to verify it, because they themselves are caught in the trap of illusion, they cannot understand that the coin is part of their illusion, so their ‘verifications’ are invalid. Similarly, the omnipotent Lord, whose Maya bewilders the whole world, bewilders the atheistic hypocrites by His Maya and keeps His own spiritual form hidden. It is no wonder that atheists find ample proof to support their misconceptions while the Lord remains beyond their grasp.

## Chapter 12 Maharaja Pariksit and Kali

One day, Maharaja Pariksit saw a bull that was limping along with only one intact leg, and a cow that was crying loudly. The bull asked the cow the reason for her lamentation: “Mother! Are you lamenting due to seeing me moving on only one leg? Are you worried thinking that low class (sudra) persons posing as kings will enjoy you? Are you distressed to see that the sacrifices for the demigods are no longer being performed these days and, as a result, they are not receiving their share of the offerings? Are you grief-stricken thinking that since King Indra, the lord of the demigods, is not receiving a share of the sacrificial offerings, he is no longer providing rainfall and thereby, the people will suffer from drought?

“Are you grieving because husbands no longer strive for the welfare of their wives and fathers no longer strive for the welfare of their children? Instead, they mistreat them like heartless demons. And the brahmanas are devoid of ethical conduct. People have already become servants and slaves to those who are inimical to the brahmanas. Are you grieving for this?

“Are you lamenting that in the future the degenerate administrative class, caught up in the attractions of Kali, will ruin the nation states? Are you grieving over the fact that people, disregarding the commands of the scriptures, are eating, drinking, taking up residence, bathing and engaging in misconduct in accordance with their independent whims?

О    Earth! Bhagavan Sri Krsna appeared in this world and performed pastimes that bestow a happiness beyond the happiness of liberation. Are you overwhelmed with sorrow remembering that now Lord Hari has disappeared from this world?”

Here, the bull with one intact leg is religion personified (Dharma) and the lamenting cow is mother Vasundhara (Earth). Hearing these words of Dharma the bull, Mother Earth, in the form of a cow, replied, “O Dharma! I am lamenting for the human beings overpowered by the sinful glance of Kali. I grieve seeing your condition, my condition and the condition of the demigods, the sages, the varnas (occupational castes) headed by the brahmanas, and the asramas beginning with the brahmacarts (celibate students).”

As Mother Earth and Dharma were speaking to each other in this way, Maharaja Pariksit arrived nearby on the bank of the Sarasvati River in Kuruksetra. There, he witnessed a distressing scene. A low class man (sudra), dressed as a king, was torturing a bull and a cow. The bull, who was missing three legs, was urinating out of fear and the cow was crying like an orphan. Seeing this cruelty inflicted on two helpless animals in a secluded place, King Pariksit prepared to slay the sudra. King Pariksit assured the cow and the bull of protection and asked the bull why three of his legs had been destroyed. Dharma, in the form of the bull, replied, “I am religion (dharma) personified in the form of a bull. In Satya-yuga, I had four legs, namely, austerity (tapasya), cleanliness (saucam), compassion (daya) and truth (satya). Before Kali-yuga my three legs: austerity, cleanliness and compassion were destroyed. Now I stand on only one leg - truthfulness. But this fearsome Kali is trying to break that also.”

After comforting Dharma and Mother Earth, Maharaja Pariksit prepared to kill Kali. Kali, seeing no other remedy, abandoned his royal disguise and fell at the feet of Maharaja Pariksit, begging for his life.

Thinking that it is improper to kill a surrendered person, Maharaja Pariksit assured Kali, “Your life is no longer in danger. But you cannot stay anywhere within the boundaries of the kingdom governed by me. All irreligiosity such as greed, falsehood, theft, extortion, wickedness, neglect of duty, poverty, deception, quarrel, arrogance and pride resides with you. Therefore, you cannot stay where righteousness and truth prevail and where the devotees reside.”

Then Kali told Maharaja Pariksit, “Your Majesty! It appears to me that there is no place which is not under your jurisdiction. Kindly give me a place to reside.”

Then King Pariksit told Kali, “You may stay in these four places: wherever there is gambling on games such as dice and chess, wherever intoxicants are consumed, wherever there is adultery or attachment to illicit sex, and wherever there is violence against living beings. I am giving you these four places for your residence.” After receiving these four places, Kali pleaded for one more place. Then Maharaja Pariksit gave Kali one more place, “You can reside in gold. Wherever there is gold (wealth), then falsehood, egotism, lust, violence, and enmity are all present together.” From that day, Kali began to reside in these five places.

Therefore, persons who desire auspiciousness, especially the religious kings, the leaders of the public and gurus, should not indulge in these five things. To do so is improper in every way. Maharaja Pariksit restored the three legs (austerity, cleanliness and mercy) of Dharma the bull, reassured Mother Earth and made her prosperous. Those who aspire for true service should always stay away from these places of Kali and engage in the loving service of Hari (hari-bhajana). Devotees should not engage in illicit activities such as adultery, righteous or unrighteous attachment to women, attachment to wealth and intoxicants like alcohol. They should not engage in killing living entities, falsehood, violence, crime, pride or any type of activity in ignorance (tamas) and passion (rajas). Rather, devotees should always remain established in service to Lord Hari, which is beyond the three modes of material nature (nirguna hari-bhakti). By preaching hari-katha and practising accordingly, they will eternally benefit themselves and others.

## Chapter 13 Sati and Daksa

Daksa, the father of Sati, initiated a grand sacrifice (yajna). The sages among the brahmanas (brahmarsis), the sages among demigods (devarsis), the demigods and their wives all arrived. On hearing about her father’s grand sacrifice and seeing everyone leaving for the event, Sati, the daughter of Daksa, was especially enthusiastic to attend. When Sati requested permission from her husband, Lord Siva, to attend the yajna ceremony in her paternal home, Lord Siva cautioned her, “Previously, your father Daksa insulted me in the presence of the Prajapatis. It seems to me that your going there will not be appropriate. Especially, Daksa will never properly respect you. Your father is extremely egotistical. Upon seeing the pious fame of humble persons who are free from egotism, his heart burns with jealousy. These kinds of people are just like the demons. They hate Bhagavan and His devotees. I did not offer my obeisances unto him; therefore, he spoke ill of me. Certainly, knowledgeable individuals do not greet and offer obeisance to those who are attached to the body and averse to Bhagavan (bhagavad-vimukha). Instead, they mentally offer their obeisances unto the Supreme Being, Lord Vasudeva, who dwells within their hearts. О Sati, although Daksa is the father who gave you birth in this body, it is not appropriate for you to even see him. Moreover, it is not proper for you to see those people who support him.”

Sati was so excited to meet with her family members that she disregarded her husband’s good advice and went anyway to Daksa’s house. Due to fear of Daksa, no one at the gathering paid any attention to her except for her mother and sisters. Her father did not show her any respect. Not paying heed to the words of her sisters, Sati noticed that Daksa had organized the yajna for the purpose of insulting Lord Siva. Sati could no longer tolerate the insult to Lord Siva, the best among the vaisnavas, and angrily confronted her father, “A great personality (maha-purusa), who finds no one agreeable or disagreeable, and who cannot feel enmity towards anyone, sees only virtues even in the faults of others. Those who consider both a person’s faults and attributes are in the intermediate stage of spiritual development (madhyama). And one who praises even trivial qualities (tuccha-guna) as significant (mahat) is uttama, the best of all. You have even projected fault upon the greatest personality of all, Lord Siva.

“If some audacious person criticizes the protectors of religion, then, if one is powerless, one should cover both ears and leave that place at once. If one is powerful, one should respond by cutting out the blasphemer’s tongue, and then one should sacrifice his own life. Therefore, if someone mistakenly consumes something despicable (nindita-vastu) from one who is inimical to a saint, he should purify himself by vomiting it out.” Sati continued, “This body, which has been born from your body, is not fit to be maintained. I am ashamed to be related to you. Hence, I will now discard this disgusting body which came from you.” After saying this, Sati gave up her body through the fire of yoga.

We learn from the example of Sati that authentic devotees do not tolerate disrespect to Sri Guru and the vaisnavas. When there is criticism of a transcendental guru or a pure devotee, it is proper to refute the allegations, if one has the capacity to do so. And if that is not possible, one should immediately leave that place. If one cannot purify the tongue of the blasphemer, then it is appropriate to give up one’s life.

Some say that it is our duty to show social courtesy (samajika-vyavahara) or good manners (sistacara) even towards those who criticize. Sri Guru and the vaisnavas. This is the opinion of deceitful persons who have no love (anuraga) for Bhagavan and His devotees, and who consider Bhagavan and His devotees to be like an ordinary soul (Jiva). The desire for worldly social popularity is the origin of behavioural etiquette. But whenever there is criticism of a great vaisnava who is dearer to us than our life, then social etiquette is not observed. One can see that the policy of ‘non-cooperation’ (asahayoga) with bad association is completely established by the ideal example of Sati. Prahlada rejected the company of his father who hated Lord Visnu. Sati also left the company of her father who was envious of a vaisnava. Moreover, by incinerating her body, which was born from Daksa in the fire of yoga, she has given an even more elevated example of non-cooperation with offenders and instructed us on the nature of a vaisnava (vaisnavata).

“Being a common sense-enjoying woman, Sati could not tolerate an insult to her husband because he is the best at satiating her senses.” Those who harbour such thoughts commit an offence at her lotus feet. The standard set by Sati was both exalted and beyond human capacity (atimartya). She considered her husband as the best among pure vaisnavas. This is clearly manifested by every word spoken by Sati in Srimad-Bhagavatam. Sati never had the bodily conception of life (dehatma-buddhi). If she had such bodily identification, then how could she give up her body? Ordinary chaste (pativrata) women have bodily consciousness or a powerful tendency to take revenge. Among all the worldly women who give up their bodies by self-immolation (jauhara-vrata) to protect their chastity, the ideal of Sati is millions of times greater. Her example is filled with the vision of extraordinary love (atimartya-priti) for Lord Visnu and the vaisnavas. Therefore, she is the best of all.

## Chapter 14 Dhruva

Svayambhuva Manu’s son, King Uttanapada, had two wives, Suniti and Suruci. Of the two, Suruci was exceedingly dear to the king. Dhruva was born from the womb of Suniti. Once, King Uttanapada was affectionately holding Suruci’s son, Uttama, on his lap. Seeing this, Suniti’s son, Dhruva, was also eager to sit on his father’s lap. What to speak of taking Dhruva onto his lap, the king did not even look at him. Then Suruci, with great arrogance, told Dhruva, “Although you are a prince, you are not worthy of sitting on the royal throne. If you desire to sit on the throne, first, satisfy Bhagavan through austerities, obtain His blessings and then take birth from my womb.”

Upon hearing such heartless words, Dhruva was deeply saddened and ran to his mother. Suniti was also distressed when she heard from others in the royal court how Suruci had spoken to Dhruva. She said, “Son, it is because of being born from an unfortunate woman like me that you are in this state. If you have the ambition to sit on the royal throne, then you must satisfy Bhagavan, the Supreme Lord. Other than the mercy of bhakta-vatsala, Bhagavan Sri Krsna, who is affectionate to His devotees, there is no other solution to your sorrow. Surrender to Him and worship Him.” Hearing such essential advice from his mother, Dhruva became steadfast in his resolve to worship Sri Hari and set off to the forest.

The great sage Sri Narada understood Dhruva’s intention. Sri Narada appeared before him and said, "Vatsa! О Child! Fate is the cause of happiness and sorrow. One should remain content in all circumstances. It is extraordinarily difficult for you to please Bhagavan by following the path suggested by your mother. Even after thousands of years of austerities, sages have not obtained Bhagavan’s mercy.”

Hearing the advice of Devarsi Narada, Dhruva asked, “O Master, what path should be adopted to attain the highest position, which has never been attained by anyone until now? Kindly instruct me about this.”

Devarsi Narada said, “O Child, all perfection lies only in serving Bhagavan. Therefore, go to the sacred bank of the Yamuna River in Madhuvana and worship Bhagavan Sri Hari by your body, mind and words.” In this way, Sri Narada instructed Dhruva and initiated him with the supremely confidential twelve-syllable mantra.

Being satisfied with Dhruva’s extended period of worship, Lord Sri Hari, who is kind to His devotees, appeared before him. When He blessed Dhruva with transcendental knowledge, Dhruva began to glorify the Lord with prayers, “O Bhagavan! Whatever one desires, You grant him accordingly. Those who desire anything other than Your service, certainly remain deprived by the illusory energy, Maya, because they continue to crave for temporary things. The enjoyment of such worldly pleasures is easily available even in hell. But the joy derived from meditating upon Your lotus feet and listening to Your glories from the mouths of Your devotees is not even attainable in the bliss of brahman (brahmananda). Therefore, even attaining the position of a demigod is also insignificant. О Ananta! Unlimited Lord! May I attain the company of all those great pure-hearted souls who are incessantly devoted to You. In their association, I will listen to songs of Your glories and effortlessly cross this terrible ocean of worldly existence.”

Pleased with Dhruva’s prayers, the affectionate Lord said, “O virtuous one who strictly observes his vows! All auspiciousness to you. I know your desire. I am bestowing upon you that splendid position no one has attained until today. Dharma, Agni, Kasyapa, Indra, the forest-dwelling sages and the Sapta Rsis continuously orbit this place. Vatsa!

O    Child! Your father, entrusting the burden of ruling the world to you, will retire to the forest. Taking shelter of religious principles, you will rule for thirty-six thousand years without anxiety. Your co-mother, Suruci, harbours animosity towards you. Although you do not hold any grudge against her, I cannot tolerate any envy towards My devotees. As a result of her hostility towards you, her son Uttama will be destroyed when he goes hunting. Distraught from not seeing her son, Suruci will search for him in the forest and perish in a forest fire. Throughout your reign, I will always remain in your memory as a result of your worship. And afterwards you will come to My abode.” Saying this, the Lord disappeared. Although every desire of Dhruva was now fulfilled, his heart was not satisfied.

He returned to his home, but his mind was not peaceful. Even after attaining the direct vision of the Lord, he could not pray for the eternal service of His lotus feet. This fact troubled him. Feeling repentant, Dhruva admonished himself, “Oh, how unfortunate I am! Although I was present before the lotus feet of Sri Hari, the destroyer of material existence, I desired only the perishable material things. Pleasing Bhagavan, who removes the cycle of birth and death, is extremely difficult through penance. Yet after pleasing Him, I desired only temporary mundane things. Exactly as a destitute person requests grains of rice from the emperor of the world, I am so unfortunate that

I    desired insignificant things from Sri Hari. He wanted to grant me the bliss of His service (sevananda) but, impelled by foolishness, I rashly desired the objects of pride.”

When King Uttanapada heard news of his son’s return, he joyfully welcomed him. By associating with his saintly son, good intelligence awakened within him. After some time, he arranged the coronation of his son Dhruva as the crown prince and left to the forest to engage in hari-bhajana.

Both Dhruva and Prahlada have set the ideal of devotion to Lord Hari since childhood. However, there is one difference between their devotion. From the very beginning, Prahlada never worshipped Lord Hari to gain a kingdom or worldly prosperity. In contrast, Dhruva worshipped Bhagavan in the beginning with the desire to attain his father’s kingdom. Because he was deeply hurt by his co-mother’s harsh words, he also wanted to win his father’s favour.

When Nrsimhadeva invited Prahlada to ask for a boon, Prahlada said, “One who desires something in return for serving the Lord is not a servant but a merchant.”

Dhruva worshipped the Lord with the hope of gaining a kingdom, but when the lotus-eyed Lord Sri Hari asked him to request a boon, Dhruva said, “I engaged in penance with the desire to attain a kingdom, but I have attained Your darsana, which is rarely attained even by the demigods, philosophers and sages. I came here to search for pieces of broken glass, but I found a wish-fulfilling jewel (cintamani). I do not desire anything else.”

From Dhruva’s life history, we learn that making a show of serving God while internally intending to fulfil some type of worldly desire is not true service. However, even when there is some other desire in the heart, it is also destroyed by the influence of sincere service to Bhagavan. Nevertheless, it is crucial to remember that service to the Lord is not attained by those with other desires (anyabhilasa). However, by maintaining honesty (akapata) and attentiveness (unmukhata) while serving, other desires go away and pure service is attained.

## Chapter 15 Prthu the ideal emperor

Vena was born to King Anga in the dynasty of Dhruva and was known for his cruel nature from childhood. When he used to go hunting, seeing him at a distance, the inhabitants would flee, calling out, “Here comes Vena! Here comes Vena!” Since childhood, he was so cruel that he would kill his companions while playing. King Anga tried hard to correct his son; he even punished his son, but it was of no use.

Finding no other solution, one day King Anga abandoned his wife and son and went to the forest. Although everyone made a great effort to bring him back, no one could even find him. Giving up all hope of his return, the sages tried to counsel Vena, urging him to abandon his wicked ways and embrace the worship of Lord Hari. However, Vena claimed, “I am God. There is no other God but me.” The sages could not tolerate Vena’s criticism of Lord Visnu, so they destroyed him.

Vena’s mother preserved the body of Vena through a mantra. Anarchy began to spread throughout the state in the absence of a king. Seeing this, the sages considered, “The dynasty of King Dhruva should not be destroyed. Great maha-bhagavata devotee kings were born in this lineage. Therefore, this dynasty should be protected.”

Thinking thus, they began to churn both of Vena’s arms. From this emerged a man and a woman. Both were partial expansions (amia) of Bhagavan Sri Visnu. The man was named Prthu and the woman was named Arci. In time, Prthu Maharaja became the king. Under his rule and by his mercy, the earth began to provide various gifts to its inhabitants. When King Prthu commenced the ceremony of the aivamedha-yajna, Indra attempted to steal the sacrificial horse. Fearing the mighty strength of Prthu’s son, Indra disguised himself in religious attire. He abandoned the horse and then disappeared.

The various types of religious dress Indra adopted for the purpose of stealing the horse were all signs of sin. The Digambara Jains, the Buddhists, who wear red cloth, and the kapalikas, who carry a skull, all continue to wear garments of the heretics.

Understanding Indra’s deceitfulness, Maharaja Prthu prepared to kill him. But Lord Brahma intervened and protected him. Finally, Sri Hari, the Lord of the sacrifices, appeared at the sacrificial place, along with Indra, and told Prthu Maharaja, “Indra has obstructed your performance of one hundred asvamedha-yajnas. He is now seeking forgiveness from you. Kindly forgive him.” Abiding by the Lord’s order, Prthu Maharaja forgave Indra. Indra also grasped Maharaja Prthu s feet and begged for forgiveness.

When Lord Visnu wished to grant boons to Prthu Maharaja, he said, “O Lord, in the matter of giving boons You are the controlling Lord of all gods. What wise person would ask for a boon that is desired by those who are in the bodily conception of life? All these enjoyable things are available even in hell. If I do not get the opportunity to hear about Your glories from the lotus mouths of the vaisnavas, then I will not even pray for salvation. My only prayer unto You, О Lord, is that You grant me ten thousand ears to hear the glorification of Your virtues. I do not want anything else. Whoever has even once heard Your glories from the mouths of great personalities, if he is even slightly sara-grahi, inclined to accept the essence, and not an animal, he will never give up that association and deprive himself of hearing Your glories.”

The vaisnava emperor, Maharaja Prthu, resided in the supremely pure land between the Ganges River and the Yamuna River. Without worldly attachment, he ruled the state for the purpose of serving Lord Hari and the vaisnavas. He was the undisputed emperor (ekachatra-samrata) of the entire earth comprised of seven continents (saptadvipa). His command was unchallenged everywhere. Yet he did not exercise authority over the brahmanas, who are realized in brahman, or the vaisnavas, who are the masters of all.

He was an ideal monarch who protected his subjects and genuinely desired their welfare. Therefore, he used to propagate spiritual discourses and the congregational chanting of the glories of Sri Hari, and all his subjects held a deep attachment for the service of the Lord. He never considered subjects to be instrumental sources of luxurious pleasure for the king. He offered no patronage to atheism. His nectarean instructions to his subjects are described in Chapter 21 of the Fourth Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam.

Maharaja Prthu addressed his subjects: “In accordance with your capacity and with firm faith, all of you should sincerely worship only Lord Visnu with your body, mind, words and prescribed duties. On this earth, those subjects who worship Lord Hari, the guru of the universe, with firm determination are bestowing grace upon me. The prowess of the greatly powerful royal family may never have influence over the dynasty of the brahmanas, who are the knowers of the soul, or the dynasty of the vaisnavas. I always want to carry the foot dust of such self-realized personalities upon my crown.”

By the order of Sri Bhagavan, great sages such as Sanat Kumara and others came to the royal assembly of Maharaja Prthu. The detached personalities (niskincana) never meet with materialists or kings, but they were present in the royal court of Maharaja Prthu, the vaisnava emperor, to bestow their grace upon him. Prthu Maharaja established the ideal of vaisnava-seva by serving their holy feet with his own hands.

Prthu Maharaja addressed those great saints (maha-bhagavatas), “Only they are true householders (grhasthas) in whose homestead there are items such as water, grass, land, a head of household and attendants, suitable for serving most worshipful holy persons (sadhus) like you. Even if they are poor, they are fortunate. A house that has not been anointed with the dust of the feet of great devotees, even if it is filled with great wealth, is just like a tree in which snakes live, as it brings only the fear of death. О my masters! We consider the pleasurable objects of the senses to be our ultimate goal. This world is a storehouse of various kinds of suffering. We have fallen into this world because of the faults of our actions (karma-dosa). What possibility of auspiciousness is there for us? I firmly believe that you are the only well-wishing friends of the distressed in this world. I am inquiring from you all, how can there be auspiciousness (mangala) for this world?”

Sanat Kumara said, “O King! The deep absorption of your mind in glorifying the virtues of the lotus feet of Lord Hari, the enemy of Madhu, is unwavering and very rarely attained. By such absorption, the filth of impressions of sense objects is cleansed from the consciousness. Divine love (rati) arises by cultivating devotional service (bhagavata-dharma) with firm faith, enquiring about the Ultimate Truth (tattva-jijnasa), serving the great saints, and hearing and chanting narrations of Sri Bhagavan’s pastimes. By being disinterested in the company of those dishonest persons who, being attached to wealth and beauty, are intoxicated with sense pleasures; by renouncing their desired material goals; and by having taste for living in seclusion (nirjana), one attains the happiness of the soul.

“However, do not desire to live in seclusion (nirjana) in such a place where there is no opportunity to hear divine narrations (hari-katha) from the holy mouths of saints. Although such a place may satisfy the senses, it does not satisfy Sri Krsna. Bhakti is enhanced by nonviolence (ahithsa) and peacefulness (santi), observing good conduct in accordance with the instructions of Sri Guru, profound contemplation on Lord Mukunda’s character, restraint of the senses, renunciation of sensual desires, observance of vows for the sake of Lord Hari, not criticizing others, being indifferent to the attainment and protection of one’s own enjoyment, being tolerant of heat and cold, and continuous glorification of the qualities of Sri Hari, which are the ear ornaments of the pure devotees. By this, rati for the transcendental Supreme Absolute Truth (parabrahma) naturally arises. It is only due to taking refuge in the soul that the body and other objects are dear to us. If the soul were to be destroyed, what more harm could befall the living being? Thinking about wealth and enjoyable objects is the root cause of the destruction of all the living being’s goals of life (purusarthas).

“By constantly remembering with devotion the effulgence of the toes of the Lord’s lotus feet, the devotees cut the knot of material desires arising in the heart from past impressions. On the other hand, the impersonalist yogis, who are devoid of bhakti, are incapable of severing the knot in the heart even by controlling their senses (indriya-damana). Therefore, giving up the effort to control the senses, one should engage in the service of Lord Vasudeva. Those who desire to cross over the ocean of birth and death suffer immense distress by not taking refuge in service to the lotus feet of the Lord, which is the only boat for crossing the ocean of material existence. Therefore, О King, make the worshipful lotus feet of the Lord your boat and cross this miserable, insurmountable ocean of material existence ”

Prthu Maharaja said, “O fortunate one, Sri Hari, who is compassionate to the destitute, previously bestowed His grace upon me. Your arrival here is the fulfilment of that blessing of the Lord. This body and kingdom of mine are simply the remnants (ucchista) of sadhus like you. Just as a servant serves tambula (betel leaf) and other items to the king, in the same way, I offered my life, my sons, my family, along with this kingdom, army, the earth and everything to you. Therefore, what can I give you as a daksina?”

Maharaja Prthu had renounced attachment to the fruits of action and had offered them to the Supreme Lord. Recognizing the transcendental Lord as the sole agent of all actions, he had abandoned the pride of being the doer (kartrtvabhimana). He resided in the kingdom with his empress. Just like the sun, he travelled everywhere, yet he never became attached to material pleasures. In parental affection, he was like Manu. In authority, he was like Lord Brahma. In deliberation on the Absolute Truth (brahma-tattva), he was directly akin to Brhaspati. And just like the Supreme Lord, he was victorious over the senses.

After entrusting his kingdom to his son, Maharaja Prthu, only accompanied by his wife, retired to the forest for austerities. He sometimes ate roots and fruits, sometimes dry leaves, and sometimes only drank water. Eventually, he passed his days surviving only on air. He performed all these severe austerities only for the sake of worshipping Bhagavan Sri Krsna. In this way, always remaining diligent in the service of Sri Bhagavan, in a very short time, one-pointed exclusive devotion (aikantiki-bhakti) to Sri Bhagavan arose within his heart.

## Chapter 16 King Prachinabarhi

Maharaja Pracinabarhi was born in the dynasty of the vaisnava emperor Prthu. He was a karmavira, a courageous hero in the field of worldly duties. He performed so many yajnas at one place after another that the entire earth was covered with kusa grass with the tips pointing towards the eastern direction. Henceforth, he became famous by the name Pracinabarhi \[from pracina (eastern) + barhi (sacrificial kusa grass)\].

Upon the advice of Lord Brahma, King Pracinabarhi married the daughter of the ocean, Satadruti. From the womb of Satadruti, Pracinabarhi begot ten sons called the Pracetas. He sent his sons away from the kingdom to perform penance.

Pracinabarhi’s consciousness was deeply attached to the performance of worldly karmas (prescribed duties). Observing this, the best of vaisnavas, Sri Narada Muni, approached him with compassion and imparted teachings.

Pracinabarhi asked Sri Narada, “A person attached to household life mistakenly considers his wife, children and wealth to be the highest purpose of life (paramartha). Thus, while wandering through the world performing his worldly duties (karma), he can never attain the real goal of life. Mercifully tell me how such a person can attain auspiciousness.”

Sri Narada gave Pracinabarhi a vision of the thousands and thousands of animals sacrificed in his yajnas and said, “O King, all these animals, who were sacrificed in your merciless yajnas, remember their pain and are burning with anger. They are waiting for your death. They will tear you apart with their iron-like horns. At this time, it will be auspicious for you to hear the story of Puranjana.

“There was once a famous king named Puranjana. He had a friend whose name and activities were unknown to anyone. Puranjana, driven by the ambition for sensual pleasures, searched all the cities (bodies) on earth but none of them could fulfil his desires. In the end, one day, while travelling in the southern part of the Himalayas in Bharatavarsa, he saw a city of nine gates (the human body). It was adorned with all kinds of beautiful features, terraces, gardens, pergolas, palaces, and full of magnificent buildings made of gold, silver and iron.

“The floors of the palaces were made of sapphires, crystals, pearls, beryl, emeralds and rubies. The wild animals in the forest there also observed the vow of non-violence appropriate for sages, so no one was harmed by them. While King Puranjana was wandering around in that wonderful forest, he saw a beautiful woman (visaya vivekavati buddhi: intelligence characterized by the power of discrimination with respect to material sense objects) who happened to pass by. She was accompanied by ten servants (ten senses), each of whom was the husband of one hundred heroines. A five-hooded serpent (prana: the vital life air) was her gatekeeper. The beautiful maiden (ignorance that bewilders the jiva) was simple and innocent, and she was in search of a husband who could provide her with the very best pleasures.

“When her eyebrows quivered with love, the hero Puranjana (the soul)was wounded by the arrow of her loving sidelong glance. When Puranjana began to address her with sweet words, her beauty was further enhanced by her bashful smile.

“He said, ‘This place is certainly suitable for my residence. It is my good fortune that you have come here. It seems to me that you also desire sensual pleasures like me. You may enjoy whatever material pleasures I am offering to you. You may live in this city of nine gates for a hundred years’

“In this way, Puranjana, becoming the pet dancing monkey of that beautiful woman, enjoyed a variety of material pleasures for one hundred years and completely forgot his own intrinsic nature (svarupa).

“One day, King Puranjana took in his hand his mighty bow (kartrtva-bhoktrtvadi abhinivesa: absorption in the sense of being the doer and the enjoyer), his golden armour (the covering of rajo-guna), and his inexhaustible quiver of arrows (the ego consisting of unlimited impressions of material enjoyment). He sat upon his chariot (svapna-deha: dream body) and entered the forest named Pancaprastha (the five sense objects: sound, tactility, form, flavour and aroma). King Puraiijana’s military commander (the mind who is the leader of the senses) also followed him. Puranjana abandoned his wife (vivekavati buddhi; intelligence characterized by the power of discrimination) out of an intense desire for hunting (visaya-bhoga: sense enjoyment) and he wandered in the forest killing many innocent wild animals.

“Tormented by hunger and thirst (the regret of wrongdoing), he returned home (to the path of dharma). At first, he did not see his wife. Later, he saw her lying on the ground without bedding. The king touched her feet, asked the reason for her sorrow, and begged for her forgiveness.

“King Puranjana, whose heart was filled with lust, continually enjoyed with his wife and thus begot eleven hundred son (discrimination, ascertainment, doubt, etc.). Each of these sons, in turn, had hundreds of sons. He also begot one hundred and ten daughters (shyness, eagerness, anxiety, etc.). In this way, Puranjana became attached to his family and could not devote his mind to the service of the Supreme Lord, who is the well-wisher of the soul.

“Jara (old age), who is undesirable for lusty persons, came and captured him. Many diseases, personified by the Yavana army, along with Jara, the daughter of time (kala-kanya), besieged the city of Puranjana (his body). In this attack, all of Puraiijana’s prosperity (sri) was plundered by the Gandharvas and the Yavanas. Thus, having become thoroughly degraded, he experienced the absence of his wife in the form of intelligence and the unfavourable behaviour of the presiding deities of the mind, his sons (discrimination, etc.) and grandsons (gravity, composure, etc.).

“Puranjana could not see any way to counteract all these troubles. Despite not having the desire to be parted from the city and his family, he was forced to leave. At the time of death, he did not remember his previous friend and only well-wisher, Sri Bhagavan. When all those animals he had sacrificed saw him in the abode of death, Yamapuri, they began to exact their revenge.

“Puranjana had left his body remembering his wife, and therefore, he was born as a woman in his next life. Whatever merits (punya) he had accumulated, he enjoyed in heaven. Afterwards he took birth in the earthly realm as the daughter of the king of Vidarbha. She was offered in marriage to a devotee of Krsna named Malayadhvaja. From his wife, the daughter of the king of Vidarbha, King Malayadvaja begot a daughter who was devoted to krsna-seva, and seven sons beginning with sravana (hearing), klrtana (chanting) and so on. Rajarsi Malayadhvaja, the king among sages (the pure devotee in the form of guru), distributed his kingdom amongst his sons (made arrangements for the various angas of bhakti) and departed to Kulacala (a secluded place that bestows bhakti) to engage in the worship of Sri Krsna. Vidarbha Nandini, his wife, also renounced everything and followed her husband. Devoted to her husband (guru-seva nistha-sisya: a disciple fixed in service to the spiritual master), Vidarbha Nandini, taking shelter of the appropriate detachment that arises from bhakti, began serving him (guru) with utmost devotion. When Malayadhvaja (her gurudeva) departed from this world, Vidarbha Nandini also resolved to follow her master.

“Remembering the virtues of her master (after giving samadhi to Sri Gurudeva), she was burning in the fire of separation. She relinquished all attachment to her life and became extremely anxious to attain the service of Sri Gurudeva in the eternal abode (nitya dhama). At that time, her previous friend (Bhagavan), in the dress of a brahmana, came to Vidarbha Nandini and revealed to her (gurugata-prana sisya: the disciple who cannot live without the spiritual master) her own spiritual form (jiva-svariipa), the svarupa of Bhagavan, the svarupa of ignorance arising from Maya (avidya), and the way to be free from the clutches of Maya and attain the service of Bhagavan.”

When King Pracinabarhi inquired about the interpretation of this narrative, Sri Narada explained the essence of each incident:

“The living entities take various births, high and low, according to the fruits of their actions. The filth of the threefold miseries is never mitigated by karma (pious rituals). No eternal and supreme welfare (parama mangala) is achieved by any means other than supreme devotion (parama bhakti) to Sri Vasudeva. Only by partaking in the nectar of Sri Hari’s glories from the lotus mouths of sadhus does one develop rati (love) for Sri Vasudeva. Incidentally, the experience of various types of insufficiency such as hunger, thirst, lamentation, infatuation and fear are also eliminated.

“Karma-kanda (fruitive ritual) is not the true purport of the Vedas. Sri Visnu is the root personality of the Vedas. Knowledge that engenders devotion to Sri Hari is the true knowledge. Abandoning attachment to one’s body and household and fixing one’s citta on Sri Hari is the only duty of the living entities. Those who are guru in name only are not aware of the truth of the soul (atma-tattva). Only the bona fide spiritual master (sad-guru) can remove the doubts of the living entities.”

After hearing all these teachings from Sri Narada Muni, King Pracinabarhi renounced all worldly association. He went to Kapilasrama, where he worshipped Sri Bhagavan in seclusion and attained His lotus feet.

## Chapter 17 The Ten Praceta brothers

King Pracinabarhi had ten sons who each became renowned by the name ‘Praceta’. They were all dedicated to righteousness and were of good conduct. One day, in obedience to their father’s orders, they travelled to the west to perform penance (tapa). Along the way, they met Lord Siva. Being pleased, Lord Siva gave them various teachings from which we can derive valuable lessons.

Lord Siva said, “Those who take exclusive shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Sri Vasudeva are dear to me. A man who faithfully adheres to varnasrama-dharma, duties related to one’s occupation and social order, attains the position of Lord Brahma after a hundred births. With even greater merit (punya), he attains me. However, a person who is devoted exclusively to the Supreme Lord attains, after death, the supreme abode of Lord Visnu which is beyond the material world. It is true that time (kala) destroys the universe. But time never has the courage to control those who are devoted to the lotus feet of Lord Vasudeva. Even if one attains the company of a vaisnava who is Bhagavan’s associate, even for a moment, the pleasures of being the ruler of the earth, the pleasures of the heavenly abodes, or even the pleasures of liberation (moksa) become insignificant. Only the attainment of the association of Sri Hari’s devotees makes one realize the grace of God. If one’s heart is attracted to the devotees of the Lord through bhakti-yoga, then one can easily realize the truth of Sri Bhagavan.”

Following the teachings of Lord Siva, the Pracetas worshipped Lord Vasudeva by performing kirtana for ten thousand years. They satisfied Sri Visnu by reciting a song of praise called Rudra Gita. The jewels of Lord Siva’s humble requests filled with pure bhakti unto Lord Visnu shine forth from this Rudra Gita.

After ten thousand years, Lord Visnu appeared before the Pracetas. Bhagavan Sri Visnu showed His immense joy to see that each of the Pracetas had unwavering faith in the process of devotion (bhakti-dharma) and unpretentious, unbreakable affection for each other. He asked them to ask for a boon and also instructed them to enter householder life (grhastha asrama) and practise hari-bhajana there.

Sri Vasudeva said, “When one knows that only I am the enjoyer of the fruits of all actions, they dedicate the fruits of all their actions to Me. And when they make all their activities favourable to My service and they spend all their time in discussing My pastimes, then, although they may remain in householder life (grhastha asrama), their homes do not bind them. I always manifest My beautiful form in ever newer ways in the hearts of those who constantly hear the narrations of My virtues.”

The ten Praceta brothers all praised Sri Bhagavan together, “O Bhagavan! Supreme Lord who is the guide on the path of bhakti and the only ultimate goal of the living entities, when You become pleased with someone then what boon will he desire other than Your mercy? If a bee obtains a Parijata flower with effort, will he go to any other tree? Now that we have come to the shelter of Your lotus feet, what more can we ask for?

“We simply request that as long as we are enchanted by Your Maya and continue our journey in this world in accordance with our destiny, may we continue to have the association of Your loving devotees in every birth. For us, in the company of Your devotees, neither heaven nor liberation hold any significance, what to speak of the meagre pleasures of humans. In the assembly of devotees, there are always sweet discussions about Bhagavan. Just by hearing them, thirst in the form of the desire for worldly enjoyment is pacified without effort. In such an atmosphere, there is no enmity or conflict among the living beings. In that association, Narayana, the Supreme Lord, the goal of the sannyasis, is repeatedly praised by persons without material contamination through pure discussions.

“Your devotees are wandering on foot to the holy places (tirthas) on this earth just to purify them. How could their association not be relishable to those who are fearful of this material existence? О Bhagavan, just by meeting with Your beloved friend, Lord Siva, for a moment, today we have received Your direct audience. You are the most qualified doctor to treat the otherwise incurable disease of birth and death. Therefore, we have taken shelter of You.

“We studied the Vedas with full concentration. We continuously served under the guidance of our Sri Gurudeva. We pleased the brahmanas and the respected elders. Abandoning the fault-finding mentality, we honoured all relatives, brothers, and all living entities. We stood in the water for a long time, practising penance (tapasya), giving up food and water, etc. May You be satisfied with our proper conduct (sadacara). This is the only boon we request.”

The welfare of all living beings will be achieved by following the ideal of pure devotion set by the Pracetas. In Srimad Bhagavad-gita, Bhagavan Sri Krsna tells Arjuna, “The actions of those who become devotees of the demigods and worship them with faith are unauthorized (avidhi purvaka), i.e. it is not suddha bhakti (pure devotion). Lord Visnu is the root of all demigods. He is their life-force (prana) and the Supreme Controller (Isvara) of all controllers. Mahadeva, Lord Siva, shows the ideal of visnu-bhakti by keeping the Ganges, the water that has bathed Lord Visnu’s lotus feet, upon his head.

Mahadeva always wears the serpent Lord Anantadeva as his head ad neck ornaments. He and Parvati continuously sing the names of Lord Sankarsana Rama in Ilavrta-varsa and become maddened by love. The Pracetas have accepted this very Lord Siva who is most dear to Sri Krsna as their guru, and following his instructions, have rendered service to Sri Visnu. The Pracetas never accepted unauthorized, non-Vedic (avaida) and ungodly (adaiva) theories that Lord Siva is an independent form of Sri Bhagavan, that he is directly Sri Visnu Himself, just with a different name and form. Therefore, their devotion is the ideal of suddha-bhakti (pure devotion).

Following the instructions of Lord Visnu and Lord Brahma, the Pracetas married a virgin girl named Marisa who was offered to them by the presiding deity of the trees. Due to committing offences at the lotus feet of Lord Siva, Brahma’s son Daksa took birth in the womb of Marisa. After living in the grhastha asrama for many years, finally the Pracetas entrusted their wife in the care of their sons and renounced the home. They travelled to the eastern shore of the ocean, where the sage Jajali had achieved siddhi (spiritual perfection). On arriving there, they saw Sri Narada Muni. Meekly approaching his lotus feet, they prayed, “O Master! We have already forgotten whatever instructions Sri Gurudeva Lord Siva and Lord Hari had given us due to becoming attached to household life. Therefore, kindly enlighten us again.”

Then Sri Narada Muni mercifully instructed them, “O Pracetas! In this world, one’s human birth, actions, lifespan, strength, mind, sharp intelligence and eloquence are fruitful only when they serve the Soul of all souls, the Lord of all, Sri Hari. If one does not attain Lord Sri Hari, who bestows direct realization of one’s spiritual nature (svarupa), then what is the use of one’s aristocratic parentage? What is the use of the purificatory rite (samskara) of receiving the sacred thread (yajnopavita), diksa, righteous actions prescribed by the Vedas, and longevity equal to that of the demigods? What is the use of knowledge of the scriptures, austerities, the power to remember many topics, cleverness in speaking, sharp intelligence, physical strength, dexterity of the senses, yoga, renunciation (sannyasa), study of the Vedas, vows, detachment and all other methods to attain auspiciousness? What benefit is derived from all these things? Indeed, the ultimate limit of auspiciousness is the atma, and only Sri Hari, who bestows knowledge of the atma, is Himself the beloved atma of all living beings. lust as watering the roots of a tree nourishes its trunk, branches and leaves, and just as satisfying hunger nourishes all the senses, in the same way, worshipping Sri Bhagavan includes the worship of all. There is no need to worship any other demigod separately. Therefore, the pure devotees engage themselves only in the service of the infallible Lord Acyuta.

“Certainly, the saints (sadhus) do not have any material desires. Their souls are pure. When they call upon Sri Bhagavan, He hears their call, enters their hearts and resides there. The Supreme Lord Hari, becoming subordinate to His pure devotees, does not leave their hearts to go anywhere else. Those devotees are niskincana, that is, they consider the Supreme Lord to be their only wealth. They are also dear to Sri Hari, and He finds delight in their devotion.

“Therefore, those who are intoxicated by pride in their scholarship, religiosity, aristocratic birth and worldly achievements disrespect and censure the niskincana sadhus. Lord Sri Hari will never accept the worship of such wicked persons.”

Hearing such instructions filled with pure devotion from the lotus lips of Sri Narada Muni, the Pracetas, meditating continuously on the lotus feet of Lord Hari, finally attained the eternal spiritual planet of Visnu (Visnuloka).

## Chapter 18 Bharata and Rantideva

In ancient times there was a king named Rsabhadeva. He is worshipped as an avatara of the Supreme Lord. His wife’s name was Jayanti. Rsabhadeva had one hundred sons. In those times, one’s varna, or caste, was determined by examining one’s characteristics, not by birth, as is often the case now. Nowadays, the son of a brahmana (priestly class) is called a brahmana and the son of a siidra (labourer class) is called a siidra, but this was not the case in ancient times. In those times, if the characteristics of a brahmana were found in the son of a ksatriya (warrior class), vaisya (agricultural class) or siidra, he would be considered a brahmana.

Rsabhadeva was the son of King Nabhi who was a renowned ksatriya. Rsabhadeva had a hundred sons, among whom ten were ksatriyas, nine were paramahamsa vaisnavas, and eighty-one were brahmanas. Among these ten ksatriyas, Bharata was the most illustrious. This country, Bharata-varsa (India), was named after him. Previously, it was referred to as Ajanabha-varsa.

Rsabhadeva taught his sons about the Eternal Truth and the principles of dharma (righteousness). One can find the best example of how a father should instruct his sons in the teachings of Rsabhadeva.

Rsabhadeva advised his sons, “Humans should not be like dogs or hogs that eat stool and are greedy for sensory pleasures. The only duty of human beings is to serve Bhagavan, the Supreme Lord. The service of great personalities (maha-purusas) is the door to liberation. Those who are not attached to anything in this world and whose only engagement is to praise the virtues of Bhagavan (guna-kirtana) are truly the great personalities (mahat). О sons, relinquish your bodily identification (dehatma-buddhi) and serve those great souls. One who sees a blind man going on the wrong path, but who does not alert him, is cruel. Similarly, to not alert someone who is following the path of bodily attachments is also a form of cruelty. That guru is not a guru, that relative is not a relative, that father is not a father, that mother is not a mother, that demigod is not a worshipful deity, and that husband is not a husband who does not liberate his beloved dependants from the noose of death by imparting instructions about devotional service to the Supreme Lord. Therefore, in ancient times, the great soul Bali gave up his so-called guru Sukracarya, Vibhisana gave up his elder brother Ravana, Prahlada gave up his father Hiranyakasipu, Bharata gave up his mother Kaikeyi, King Khatvanga gave up the demigods, and the wives of the yajnika-brahmanas renounced their husbands for the sake of serving the Supreme Lord because they had made obstacles in the service of Bhagavan.”

After receiving all these teachings from his father, King Bharata ruled the kingdom for some time. Eventually, he renounced household life and went to Pulahasrama to serve Bhagavan Vasudeva.

Bharata’s hermitage was located on the banks of the Gandaki River, where an abundance of Sri Narayana-silas could be found. There, he stayed alone in the forest and served Bhagavan with offerings of flowers, leaves, tulasi, fruits and roots. When he attained deep attachment for Bhagavan in his heart, symptoms of ecstasy such as trembling, flowing tears and horripilation (pulaka) became visible in his body. One day, while Bharata was engrossed in chanting the Holy Names, a pregnant deer came to the river to quench her thirst. Not far away, a ferocious lion suddenly roared. The deer became terrified and attempted to cross the river to escape from the lion. This trauma resulted in the premature birth of the fawn and the sudden death of the mother deer. The baby deer began to float away down the river. Bharata was sitting on the bank of the river witnessing the whole incident.

Whose stone-like heart would not melt after witnessing such a scene? This was the case even with Bharata. Giving up his chanting of the Holy Name of Hari, Bharata jumped into the river to rescue the fawn.

He thought,

**“It is certain that a peaceful and compassionate soul, who is the guardian of the helpless, does not care even for his highest self-interest for the sake of protecting those who come to his shelter.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 5.8.10)**

Thinking in this way, Bharata saved the helpless fawn from the current of the river and diligently began to take care of it. Bharata thought of the deer at every moment. Even at the time of his death, he saw the fawn was sitting beside him, as if it were his son. So strong was his attachment to the baby deer that even though he had renounced the world and had become a sannyasi (ascetic), he began to meditate only on the deer. Consequently, he attained the body of a deer in his next birth.

Bharata possessed one virtuous quality. He never considered the individual souls to be God (Narayana) as the mayavadis do. He never considered an ordinary soul to be the Supreme Controller. He did not imagine that poverty-stricken people are directly Narayana. Since his practice of devotion (sadhana) was complete, the memory of his previous life was not lost. By the influence of the devotional service he had performed in previous lives, he could understand the reason why he had now taken birth in the body of a deer. Knowing this, he felt intense contrition. “Alas!” he repented, “It is a matter of great regret. I fell from the path traversed by the self-controlled and enlightened persons. I patiently gave up all types of material attachment and I sought refuge in a sacred secluded forest. Residing there, I fully applied my steady mind (citta) in constantly hearing, contemplating and singing the glories of the Supreme Lord Vasudeva. I succeeded in worshipping and remembering Him at every moment. Then that very mind of this ignorant person suddenly followed a tiny deer and fell from its own objective. What a fool I am!”

Reflecting in this way, the saintly King Bharata’s clear spiritual intelligence prevailed. He abandoned his deer mother and Kalanjara Parvata, the place of his birth in the deer body. He set out for the sacred Saligrama-tirtha, which, being the abode of Bhagavan, is dear to the peaceful sages. He arrived there once again at the asrama of the sages Pulastya and Pulaha. Living there, he waited for death. Finally, while keeping half his body submerged in the waters of the Gandaki River, he gave up his deer body.

In his next life, he was born in a brahmana family. He could recall the incidents of his previous life. Out of fear of falling due to the fault of material association, he avoided all social interactions. To protect himself from others, outwardly he behaved like a mad person while remaining immersed in the service of Bhagavan within his heart.

One night, while on watch guarding the agricultural fields, Bharata was captured by bandits. They intended to offer him as a human sacrifice to the goddess Bhadrakali. When one of the bandits raised the sacrificial sword to sacrifice Bharata in front of Kali, suddenly, the statue of the goddess burst open and Bhadrakali emerged from it. She killed all the bandits and saved the devotee.

Once, the ruler of the Sindhu and Sauvira regions, King Rahugana, was travelling to Kapilasrama upon a palanquin. On the way, the king’s servants required one more palanquin bearer, so they forcefully engaged Bharata in this task. Bharata, being devoid of pride, began to carry the palanquin without offering any argument or resistance. He walked very slowly and cautiously to ensure that no creature would be crushed beneath his feet. As a result, he could not keep pace with the other carriers, and the palanquin was moving erratically. King Rahugana told the palanquin bearers, “Hey you carriers! Carry the palanquin properly! Why are you making it go up and down?” The palanquin carriers were fearful of the king’s punishment. To save their lives, they began to explain that the cause was the newly recruited carrier. Then the king, being intoxicated with pride, berated Bharata with sarcastic words and threatened him with punishment.

Bharata responded to the king’s arrogant statements with a profound philosophical discourse on ontological truths (tattvas). Witnessing an ostensibly ignorant palanquin bearer wax eloquent with such erudition, King Rahugana was struck with wonder and his spiritual consciousness was awakened within. He begged for forgiveness from Bharata because he knew that even if someone were to be as powerful as Siva, by disrespecting great personalities (mahat), he would be ruined. Bharata’s teaching to King Rahiigana is described in the Fifth Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam in Chapters Ten to Fourteen.

Bharata instructed King Rahugana, “This material world is impassable like a dense forest. The living being (jiva) is under the control of Maya. In this forest, his six senses are dacoits. His wife, children and relatives are like man-eating foxes and wild dogs. Just as wolves sneak into a flock of sheep and snatch them away from the herd, in the same way, the jackals (wife and children) living with him also steal away his mind when he is not alert and they start to snatch his wealth at every opportunity. In this place, the living being, afflicted with hunger and thirst, loses patience and becomes angry towards his wife and sons. In this place there is only punishment and the fire of the threefold miseries. Those powerful men, who can defeat even the undefeated conquerors of the earth, under the control of pride, thinking, “The world is mine.” harbour enmity towards each other and give up their lives. Some take pleasure in the company of women. On seeing their faces, they want to continuously enjoy the women through the sense of hearing and other senses and see the faces of their future sons. Sometimes, out of fear of the wheel of time, they meet with swindlers and wicked hypocrites. Therefore, О Rahugana, give up the sense of worldly identity and immerse your mind in the service of Lord Hari.”

By the power of the association of the maha-bhagavata Bharata, King Rahugana realized the impermanence of the world and understood that loving service to Sri Hari is the only way to attain supreme auspiciousness. He renounced bodily identification (dehatma-buddhi) and completely offered himself (atma-samarpana) in Sri Hari’s service.       Even in his youth, Rajarsi Bharata, the sage among kings, had easily renounced the desires for beautiful women, sons and the pleasures of the kingdom out of his intense longing to serve the Supreme Lord. He considered the joys of heaven and even of liberation (moksa) to be insignificant. He considered serving Lord Narayana to be the essence of life. He realized that regarding oneself and others to be Lord Narayana gives rise to offences and self-destruction. Therefore, while giving up his deer body, he had said, “I offer myself unto the Supreme Lord Sri Narayana, who is the master of Maya and the indwelling witness in everyone’s heart.”

*narayanaya haraye nama ity udaram*  
*hasyan mrgatvam api yah samudajahara*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 5.14.45*

The acaryas have also analysed and compared the life history of the great soul, Mahatma Rantideva, with Bharata. Rantideva was a greatly charitable king. Unlike Bharata, he was not a renunciant, but a parama- vaisnava householder. He dedicated his wealth to serving the devotees of the Lord. While remaining hungry himself, he would always satisfy others by distributing the prasada of Lord Visnu. Once he fasted for forty-eight days without water. “May the living entities be satisfied by Bhagavan’s prasada and may devotion awaken in their hearts.” He only endeavoured for this purpose. His fervent prayer was,

*na kamaye ’ham gatim isvarat param astarddhi-yuktam apunar-bhavam va artim prapadye ’khila-deha-bhajam antah-sthito yena bhavanty aduhkhah*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 9-21.12*

**О    Bhagavan, I do not desire from You the eight mystic powers or attainment of the highest abode. Nor do I seek liberation (moksa). My only desire is this: May I become situated in the hearts of all living beings and bear their sufferings so that they will not suffer.’**

Seeing Rantideva distraught with the pain of others, Lord Brahma and the divine Maya of Lord Visnu presented numerous temptations before him to test his patience. However, Rantideva offered obeisance to those allurements from far away and firmly established his consciousness in devotion to Lord Vasudeva.

*sa vai tebhyo namaskrtya*  
*nihsango vigata-sprhah*  
*vasudeve bhagavati*  
*bhaktya cakre manah param*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 9.21.16*

Srila Jiva Gosvami, an associate of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, has compared the life stories of King Bharata and King Rantideva and provided us with a valuable teaching. He stated that King Bharata fell into a crisis by abandoning the service of the Lord to help the body of another living being. The truly auspicious path is to help the souls of the living entities. All living entities are the eternal servants of Bhagavan. It is by forgetting their servitude to the Lord that they undergo mental and physical suffering. If one wants to save them from miseries, then he will have to uproot and discard the source of all afflictions, namely, avidya, the ignorance arising from Maya. The destruction of ignorance only occurs through the chanting (kirtana) of Bhagavan’s name and the narration of His pastimes (hari-katha). Then the soul becomes aware of his intrinsic spiritual nature (svarupa-dharma). Just as the acquisition of wealth simultaneously eliminates poverty, similarly, on attaining the wealth of Bhagavan’s eternal service (nitya-seva), ignorance is immediately destroyed. King Rantideva did not just try to help the bodies of the living entities out of compassion for their suffering condition. He had established his consciousness (citta) firmly in Lord Vasudeva with devotion. The demigods presented him with so many temptations, but he was not infatuated or enchanted by them. He did not desire the happiness of liberation or his own personal enjoyment. “May all living beings be engaged in the service of the Lord.” He made intense endeavour for this purpose. Therefore, those who truly desire the welfare of living beings must strive for the welfare of the soul and make arrangements for everyone to listen to and chant the glories of the Supreme Lord. By merely serving the physical and mental well-being of others, the perpetual deficiency (abhava) in life of the living entities cannot be eradicated. It is only by becoming rich in the wealth of serving God that all deficiencies will be eradicated.

## Chapter 19 Ajamila

In the city of Kanyakubja (modern day Kanauj) there lived abrahmana named Ajamila. His character became polluted by association with a low-class serving maid and his virtuous conduct was destroyed. Sometimes he would plunder the possessions of others. Sometimes he would cheat people on the pretext of gambling. Sometimes he would divest others of their wealth by fraud. By indulging in such despicable activities, he filled the bellies of his family and inflicted pain on other living beings. In this way, occupied with raising and protecting the serving maid’s children, around eighty-eight years, the major portion of his life, passed.

In his old-age, Ajamila had ten sons. The youngest among them was named ‘Narayana’. Both the mother and father loved the child very much. The elderly Ajamila, in a state of intense bewilderment, fully gave his heart to his son Narayana. He never grew tired of listening to his child’s sweet broken words and watching him playing simple games. Ajamila had become bound up by the bonds of the child’s affection.

This fool passed his life in this way until the time of his death arrived. Now, Ajamila began to think only about his son Narayana and consider what might happen to him in the future. Just then, he saw three extremely fearsome messengers of death (Yamadutas) who had come to take him away. They had nooses in their hands, twisted faces, and the hair on their bodies stood on end. At that moment, the boy Narayana was playing nearby. Seeing the Yamadutas, Ajamila became extremely distressed and loudly called out, “Narayana! My son, Narayana!”

The divine messengers of the Lord (Visnudutas) observed that Ajamila, at the time of his death, was calling out the name of their master, Bhagavan Narayana. So with great speed, they immediately arrived there. At that moment, the messengers of Yama were pulling Ajamila’s subtle body out of his physical body. The Visnudutas forcefully stopped them. Yama’s messengers asked, “Who are you to defy the orders of Dharmaraja? Whose messengers are you? Where did you come from? Are you some demigods (devatas), sub-demigods (upadevatas) or the best of perfected beings (siddhas)? We see that your eyes are as tender as lotus petals; you are all dressed in yellow silken clothes, your heads are adorned with crowns, earrings decorate your ears and garlands of lotus flowers are swinging from your necks. Your youthful forms are resplendent. You all carry in your hands a splendid bow, a quiver, a sword, a mace, a conch shell, disc and lotus. Your bodily radiance is dispelling the darkness and naturally illuminating all the directions. We are servants of Dharmaraja. Why are you stopping us?”

When the messengers of Yama spoke thus, the obedient associates of Lord Narayana smiled and spoke in deep voices like the rumbling of storm clouds: “O messengers of Yama! If you are truly the obedient servants of Dharmaraja, then tell us, what are the characteristics of dharma and what is the principle of religion (dharma-tattva)? How is punishment allotted? Who deserves to be punished? Are all sinful humans punishable or are some exempt?”

The Yamadutas replied, “Whatever actions the Vedas have prescribed is dharma and whatever actions they have prohibited is adharma. The Vedas are directly the form of Narayana. They are His natural breathing (svabhavika ivasa-praivasa) and self-revealing knowledge (svayam prakasa-jnana) - this we have heard. In the entire material creation, all objects made of sattva, rajas and tamas, and all the living beings are situated in their supreme shelter, Bhagavan. He appropriately awards the living entities with forms (rupa), attributes (guna), names (nama), and actions (karma). Whatever actions the living entity performs by body or mind are witnessed by the sun, fire, ether, air, senses, moon, twilight, night, day, directions, water, earth, time and Dharma (Yamaraja). Adharma is identified by them and then the decision of who is to be punished is determined. All human beings who commit sinful actions are liable for punishment according to their deeds.

О    sinless beings! Those who perform actions (karma) always have a connection with the modes of material nature (gunas). Hence, everyone engages in some sin (papa) and some piety (punya). No embodied being can refrain from performing actions. In accordance with the type and the amount of dharma and adharma a person performs in this life, he experiences the corresponding results in the afterlife. Our master, the unborn Bhagavan and omniscient Yamaraja, resides within the hearts of all. Therefore, he can see everyone’s past forms. At the same time, he also deliberates on their future forms. This Ajamila was a great scholar of the scriptures. He possessed the treasure of modesty, good conduct and other virtues. He was celibate, humble, self-controlled, devoted to truth, well versed in mantras and pure. However, after falling into bad company, he fell down from his pious life. In the end, being devoid of good conduct, he became entangled in various sinful activities. He did not atone for these sinful activities. Therefore, we will take him to the jurisdiction of Yamaraja, the chastiser. There he will undergo the punishment for his sins and become purified.”

Upon hearing this, the Visnudutas replied, “O Yamadutas! It is truly astonishing and regrettable that adharma is entering the assembly of the righteous. For here, punishment is unjustly given to an innocent and blameless person. If those who protect and govern the people, those who are impartial and altruistic, begin to show partiality in their dealings, then in whom shall the people seek refuge? Just as the righteous leaders conduct themselves, the common people follow. Their own conduct proves which activities are compatible with dharma and people begin to imitate their example. О Yamadutas! He (Ajamila) has completely absolved himself of countless sins from many lifetimes because, being helpless at the time of death, he has uttered the supremely auspicious and liberating name of Bhagavan. The very moment he uttered the four syllables of ‘Narayana’, he was absolved of all his sinful deeds. Uttering the names of the Lord is the greatest atonement because by uttering the names of the Lord, one’s consciousness (buddhi) becomes engrossed in the Lord’s divine form (svarupa), qualities (guna) and pastimes (lila). Bhagavan considers, ‘This person is My own (nija-jana); and therefore, it is My duty to protect him in all ways’”

Great brahmavadi sages have prescribed numerous rigorous practices to atone for sins, such as the candrayana-vrata and other severe vows. However, the root of a person’s sinful nature is not removed by these practices as it is by the utterance of the names of Bhagavan, because the Holy Names of the pure and glorious Lord cause realization of His qualities. If even after performing an atonement ritual the mind is still inclined towards sin, then the atonement is not complete. Hence, those who want to perform an atonement, by which sinful actions and sinful desires are uprooted, should sing the glories of the Lord. Engaging in ritualistic practices (karma-kanda) is as futile as the bathing of an elephant. An elephant driver may goad an elephant to enter a river to wash its body and make it clean, but as soon as the elephant returns to the riverbank, it picks up dust with its trunk and makes its entire body dirty again. That is why one should always keep their consciousness (citta) pure by chanting the Holy Name and the qualities of Bhagavan until the desire to sin again disappears forever. When a fire breaks out in the jungle, it burns the trees and plants, but it becomes extinguished before reaching their roots. In the same way, celibacy, charity, cleanliness, austerity, etc., are not capable of completely eradicating the root of sin. However, devotees absorbed in service to Sri Vasudeva effortlessly destroy the roots of sin by unalloyed devotion. Just as fog is completely dissipated when the sun rises, similarly, when unalloyed bhakti rises in the heart, the inclination towards sin does not remain in the living entity. Providing light is the main action of the sun, and simultaneously it also dispels the cold. In the same way, when unalloyed devotion (kevala-bhakti) rises in the heart, love of God (prema) appears, and simultaneously ignorance (avidya) and the inclination to sin are dispelled. The degree of purification a sinful person attains by associating with pure devotees, serving them, and taking shelter of Sri Krsna, is certainly not attained by performing austerities. As even all the rivers put together cannot purify a jar of liquor, similarly one who is indifferent (vimukha) to serving Sri Hari cannot be purified by the most elaborate atonement rituals. In this material world, a person who has even once absorbed his mind in the lotus feet of Sri Krsna and whose heart has become even slightly attached to Sri Krsna’s qualities, has completed all atonement by the mere semblance of love (rati abhasa) for Bhagavan.

Ajamila did not just atone for sins from a single birth; by chanting the semblance of Harinama, he atoned for sins from countless millions of births. Ajamila uttered the supremely auspicious Holy Name, the embodiment of the method to attain liberation. The moment he uttered the four syllables ‘Narayana’, merely through that utterance, all his sins were absolved. One may be a thief, a drunkard, a betrayer, a killer of brahmanas, one who violates his guru’s wife, the accomplice of such sinners or one who murders a woman, a king, his own father or a cow -no matter how sinful a person may be, the chanting of the Holy Name is the best atonement for everyone. By uttering the name of the Lord, the human intellect becomes engrossed in the qualities, pastimes, and form of the Lord, and Bhagavan Himself considers that person His own (iatmiya-buddhi). However, if someone uses the Holy Name as a weapon to counteract sins with the intention of committing further sins and improper conduct in the future, that is, if one thinks, “The Holy Name can wash away all sins, so we can commit sins again and again and then utter the Holy Name and all sins will be washed away” then he makes a greater offence. This is called: ‘Committing sins on the strength of the Holy Name’. The Holy Name of the Lord never protects such persons. They are grave sinners and offenders at the lotus feet of Sri Harinama. If we offend and deceive our only protector, then no one else can protect us.

Ajamila had committed many sins, but he had not committed any offence at the lotus feet of the Lord. Therefore, the name that came from his mouth is called a semblance of the Holy Name (namabhasa). “The Supreme Lord is my only Master. He is complete consciousness (purna cetana) and I am minute consciousness (anu cetana). The soul is His eternal servant. I am neither the body nor the mind. This material world is a prison for my reformation.” This type of knowledge is called sambandha-jnana, relational knowledge. Until one receives and realizes this kind of knowledge by the grace of the guru, the recitation of the Holy Name is considered to be namabhasa, the semblance of the Holy Name.

There are four types of namabhasa:

1\)    sanketa (to indicate something else)

2\)    parihasa (in jest)

3\)    stobha

4\)    hela (with neglect)

Sanketa has two subdivisions. The first one entails chanting the name to indicate Lord Visnu while conceiving of Lord Visnu with the dull intellect (jada-buddhi). Ajamila provides an example of this sanketa-namabhasa. Although initially he took the name thinking of his son, later the name of Lord Narayana became signified. Bhagavan Visnu had already arisen on the path of his memory. After the awakening of this namabhasa, he became freed from worldly life (samsara). He abandoned all bad company and engaged in the service of Sri Hari (hari-bhajana) in solitude.

The second type of sahketa-namabhasa entails reciting the name of Lord Visnu to refer to an inert material object. For example, the non-Aryans (mleccha) utter, “Harama!” meaning ‘prohibited by scripture’. Although this word can be taken as ‘ha Rama!’ a form of address to Visnu, the speaker intends to refer to another being.

Taking Sri Krsna’s name in jest (parihasa) can be seen in the case of Jarasandha. The word stobha means ‘with disrespect’. Saintly persons say that Sisupala’s uttering of the name with disrespect is an example of this kind of namabhasa. The word stobha can also mean ‘a meaningless word (nirarthaka)’ as in using the name to fill the time between lines of a song.

The word hela is understood as negligence or indifference. This kind of namabhasa can be observed in sense gratifiers, persons inclined to vice (adharma) or laziness, provided there is no offence involved.

Parihasa is more faulty than sanketa, and stobha is more flawed than parihasa-namabhasa. Of all the ways to accrue merit (sukrti) in this world, namabhasa is the foremost. Namabhasa is more fruitful than piety, vows, yoga practices and all other auspicious acts. Namabhasa purifies the subconscious mind (citta), annihilates sins, easily liberates one from the cycle of birth and death, and is eternally auspicious.

There is another abhasa (semblance) called pratibimba-namabhasa, the reflection of the Holy Name. Included in the category of offence to the Holy Name, it arises from anarthas (impediments) due to ignorance. In fact, this is not like the types of namabhasa previously mentioned. It is actually an offence against the Holy Name, namaparadha. It nourishes the doctrine of illusion, mayavada, because it is based on the assumption that Sri Krsna’s names, forms, qualities, associates and pastimes are temporary and imaginary. Thus, such theatrical imitation of the chanting of the name is called pratibimba-namabhasa. Among the ten offences against the Holy Name, it is the first.

The great saints say that on the very day Ajamila first called his son ‘Narayana’ that is, during the baby’s naming ceremony (nama-karana-samskara) when, following the saints’ advice, his son was named Narayana, at that moment, all Ajamila’s sins were absolved. Subsequently, after uttering the name for the first time, whatever names he repeatedly chanted constituted the sadhana of bhakti (devotional practice). However, it is observed that despite initially uttering namabhasa, he still did not desist from sinful activities. Being attached to a low-class maidservant, he continued to indulge in various sinful activities.

To reconcile this, it is said that just as there is an interval between the first appearance of the bud and the appearance of the fruit on a tree, similarly, although namabhasa appeared the very first time Ajamila uttered the name ‘Narayana’, that namabhasa manifested its full fruit only at the time of his death. Embracing this conclusion in a distorted way, some people imagine that the mere utterance of Sri Hari's name is the Holy Name. Imagining this to be namabhasa, they engage in sins and other misconduct after chanting the Holy Name. Considering the analogy of the interval between the seed of a tree sprouting and the appearance of fruit, they increase their sinful inclinations on the strength of the Holy Name. But everyone cannot be compared to Ajamila. If someone justifies his misconduct by proposing that in the eyes of God the immoral actions performed by a person with impediments (anartha-yukta) are equivalent to the immoral actions performed by Ajamila, there will be a delay in the appearance of the pure Holy Name (suddha-nama).

Essentially, if the actions of persons such as Ajamila and Bilvamangala are used to justify one’s misconduct, then it becomes the offence of committing sins on the strength of the Holy Name.
