Furthermore, although liberated persons are not to be blamed for any type of transgression, such occurrences cannot be held as ideal examples for those who are not liberated. Therefore, some great personalities have concluded that when Ajamila uttered the name ‘Narayana’ at the time of giving up his body, it was namabhasa. By presenting this conclusion, the common insignificant living entities will not have any opportunity to run towards inauspiciousness. In principle, there is no difference between the first conclusion, that namabhasa occurred at the name-giving ceremony, and the second conclusion, that namabhasa occurred at the time of death. However, in the second conclusion (siddhanta), more vigilance has been exercised for the sake of the practising devotee who is still covered by material attachments (anartha-yukta sadhaka).

All powers exist in the Holy Name. When a person, while falling, slipping, breaking a limb, being bitten by a snake, engulfed in fire or wounded, helplessly utters, “Hari Hari!” taking the name of Bhagavan, they are no longer a candidate for the torments of Yamaraja in hell. Sages have knowingly prescribed major atonements for major sins and minor atonements for minor sins. There is no doubt that through penance, charity, reciting mantras, and other atonements, the reactions of sins are destroyed. But the heart contaminated by those sins does not become pure. Only by serving the lotus feet of the Lord does the heart become pure. Knowingly or unknowingly chanting the names of the Lord turns all our sins to ashes. Just as one who drinks nectar becomes immortal even if he does not know the potency of nectar, similarly, uttering Bhagavan’s name bestows its fruit, whether one knows the power of the Holy Name or not.

In this way, the associates of Bhagavan delivered a complete verdict based on the divine principles of bhagavata-dharma and, releasing Ajamila from the ropes of the Yamadutas, rescued him from the jaws of death. Then the Yamadutas went to their master, Yamaraja, and recounted to him the entire incident in detail. Ajamila, freed from the nooses of the messengers of death, became fearless and tranquil. Immersed in the joy of witnessing the messengers of the Lord, he bowed before them in reverence. Bhagavan’s associates noticed that Ajamila desired to speak to them. Then they suddenly vanished before his eyes.

On this occasion, Ajamila had heard about the visuddha bhagavata-dharma (the pure religion of devotion to God) from the associates of Lord Visnu, and the saguna-dharma of the Vedas (religion influenced by material qualities) from the messengers of Yama.

Devotion quickly arose within the heart of Ajamila upon hearing the glory of Lord Hari that removes all sins. Now, Ajamila reflected upon his past misdeeds and he began to deeply repent. He condemned himself: “Oh what a servant of the senses I am. I have ruined my brahminical qualities by begetting children from the womb of a serving maid. It is a matter of great sorrow. Fie on me! Shame on me again and again! I am to be condemned by saintly persons. I am so sinful. I have tarnished the good reputation of my dynasty. Bodily identification (dehatma-buddhi) is the root of desires for physical enjoyment. I vow to control my mind, senses and life-force so that I should not fall into the darkness of hell again. Under the control of ignorance, I considered my body to be the self. I engaged in so many activities to fulfil desires arising from the body. The result of this is only bondage. Cutting free these shackles, I will act for the benefit of all living beings and pacify all worldly desires. The illusory energy of Bhagavan, Maya, taking the form of a woman, ensnared me and made me dance like a pet monkey. Now, I shall liberate myself from the illusions of Maya. I have identified the Absolute Truth (satya-vastu) Paramatma. Therefore, now I shall relinquish the bodily conceptions of 'I' and ‘mine’ and shall purify my mind by chanting the Holy Names of Bhagavan and absorbing my mind in Him.”

Ajamila’s brief association with the messengers of Lord Visnu awakened detachment (vairagya) and devotion (bhakti). Renouncing all worldly connections and infatuation, he embarked on a journey to Haridvara where he dedicated himself to the service of the Lord. There, he obtained the vision of those same four Visnudutas. Offering obeisance to them, he gave up his body and at that very moment, he attained a spiritual form just like those associates of Bhagavan.

When someone hears this narration of Ajamila’s life, they may think that it is merely a fictional story to exaggerate the glories of chanting Sri Hari s name. Some might argue that many people chant Sri Hari’s name frequently, yet their worldly desires have not gone and they are not free from sins or misconduct. So how can one understand that there is so much power in the Holy Name and that Ajamila’s example is true? If it cannot be proven, then it must be assumed that vaisnavas have fabricated this tale just to exaggerate the glories of chanting Sri Hari’s name.     There are no greater offenders than those who consider other spiritual practices to be equal to chanting the Divine Names, or view chanting as just one among many practices. They will never develop any affection (rati) for the Holy Names. Therefore, the famous Srimad-Bhagavatam commentator, Srшla Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura, has stated,

*namabhasa-balenajamilo duracaro‘pi vaikuntham prapitas tathaiva smartadayah sadacarah sastrajna*  
*api bahuso nama-grahino’py artha-vada-kalpanadi-namaparadha-balena ghora-samsaram eva prapyanta*  
*ity ato nama-mahatmya-drstya sarva-mukti-prasango’pi*  
*nasankyah*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 6.2.10, Sarartha Darsini*

Due to the influence of the semblance of the Holy Name (namabhasa), even a sinner like Ajamila attained Vaikuntha. However, although those who are learned in scripture and observant of good conduct (smarta-sadacara) repeatedly chant the Holy Name, due to the offences, such as considering the glories of chanting the Holy Name to be exaggerated or imaginary, they continue to suffer the terrible afflictions of worldly existence. Therefore, there is no doubt that upon witnessing the greatness of the Holy Names, everyone will become liberated.

Bhagavan Sri Caitanya Deva has stated,

*namnam akari bahudha nija-sarva-saktis*  
*tatrarpita niyamitah smarane na kalah*  
*etadrsi tava krpa bhagavan mamapi*  
*durdaivam idrsam ihajani nanuragah*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Antya-lila 20.16*

**The Holy Name is supremely powerful, even more so than the nami, the avatara form of Bhagavan Himself. There is no doubt about this. However, we have made so many offences that we lack faith in and eagerness (anuraga) for the lotus feet of Sri Harinama Prabhu.**

One who doubts the omnipotence of Bhagavan is an atheist. Yet, those who see their own disqualifications and strive wholeheartedly to overcome them are genuine pilgrims on the path of devotion. We should follow the path of pure devotees rather than becoming atheists.

## Chapter 20 Citraketu

There was once a kingdom, Surasena, ruled by the emperor Citraketu. In his kingdom, the earth itself used to provide food according to the people’s wishes. He had ten million queens and they were capable of bearing children, but none of them were blessed with offspring. All his wives were in constant anxiety about their lack of progeny.

One day, while roaming freely throughout various realms, the sage Angira, who is competent to grant boons or pronounce curses, arrived at the palace of King Citraketu. The king rose from his throne and respectfully worshipped him in accordance with prescribed customs. When the sage sat comfortably on the throne, the king also sat, on the ground beside him, with a peaceful demeanour. Observing the king’s unhappy and anxious state, the sage inquired about the cause of his worry and dissatisfaction. Citraketu desired offspring, so, when the sage inquired, he humbly bowed his head and revealed his heart, “I have rulership over the empire of the earth, with opulence and wealth, which is hankered for even by the demigod guardians of the world (lokapalas). However, due to having no offspring, I am unable to find the slightest peace in these pleasures, just like a person inflicted with hunger and thirst cannot find satisfaction in anything other than food and water.”

In response to the king’s plea, the all-capable and immensely compassionate son of Lord Brahma, the great sage Angira, prepared sweet rice suitable for the demigod Tvasta and offered it in a sacrifice. Citraketu’s first wife, Maharani Krtadyuti, was the most qualified among all the queens. Angira Rsi gave her the remnant from the sacrifice and announced, “O King, from the womb of your wife, a son will be born who will give you both joy (harsa) and sorrow (soka).” Having spoken thus, Angira departed from there.

After some time, a beautiful son was born from the womb of MaharanI Krtadyuti. The bond of affection of King Citraketu and Mother Krtadyuti with this son, who was attained with so much difficulty, grew stronger with each passing day. The king’s love for Krtadyuti surpassed his love for the other queens. Because of this, their hearts were filled with jealousy towards the son and the mother. Their intelligence was completely lost in hostility and cruelty prevailed. They could not tolerate their husband’s affection for his son. Out of jealousy they administered poison to the little prince.

When the king and the queen discovered the lifeless child, they became overwhelmed with lamentation. When Queen Krtadyuti saw that her only child had died and that Citraketu, who was as dear to her as her life, was tormented by intense grief, she began to loudly express her sorrow in various ways, “O Creator! You are indeed foolish to act against Your own creation. It is astonishing that the elderly people live while the infant child dies. If such contradictions are actually natural for You, then You are the eternal enemy of everyone. If there is no sequence to life and death in this world, and beings merely take birth and die in accordance with their fructified karmic reactions, then what is the need of You? You have made the bonds of affection among relatives to increase Your creation, yet You are killing children on Your own initiative and rubbing Your hands together.” After speaking in this way, she began loudly calling out to her departed son.

Maharsi Angira and Devarsi Narada observed that King Citraketu was becoming unconscious due to grief. Moreover, there was no one present to make him understand the truth. So they approached him and began to enlighten him with beautiful reasoning. They said, “O King, for whom are you grieving so much? Who was this child to you in this life and his previous life? Did this relation with your son exist before and what relation will you have with him in the next life? Just as small particles of sand on the bank of a river come together and sometimes separate due to the force of the water, similarly, living beings also meet and part ways by the force of time. О King, just as some seeds are born from other seeds and perish, similarly, prompted by the illusory energy of the Supreme Lord, living entities are born from other living entities and also pass away. О King, your body, our bodies and the bodies of all other moving and non-moving beings in this world who exist in the present were not present before our birth and will not remain after our death. It is established that even now their existence (astitva) is not factual, because the truly abiding substance (satya-vastu) remains constant throughout time. Bhagavan alone is the Master of all beings. He does not undergo transformations of birth and death. He has no desires nor expectations. He independently creates, sustains and annihilates all dependent life forms. Therefore, one should not grieve for such relationships. Why are you grieving for this inert body which is not eternal as if it were conscious? That which is inert (jada) can never be eternal like that which is conscious (cetana).”

When Maharsi Angira and Devarsi Narada informed King Citraketu in this way, he composed himself somewhat and inquired about their identity, “You both appear to be enlightened and the greatest of great personalities. You have come here while concealing yourselves in the dress of ascetics. Mercifully reveal who you are. I know that many self-realized devotees dear to Bhagavan wear the dress of mad ascetics (avadhutas) and wander throughout the world to impart teachings to people like me who are attached to material sense objects. О Masters! I am ensnared in tasting sense gratification. My intelligence is bewildered. I am like a village animal, immersed in the deep darkness of ignorance. Please light the lamp of my knowledge.”

Maharsi Angira said, “O King, when you ardently desired a son, it was I who granted your wish. I am Angira, and standing before you is the all-capable Devarsi Narada, the son of Lord Brahma himself. When we saw you deeply immersed in the darkness of ignorance due to lamentation for your son, we thought that since you are a devotee of Bhagavan, it is not appropriate for you to lament. Thus, we have both come here just to show mercy to you. О King, the truth is that a devotee of Bhagavan and the brahmanas should not grieve in any circumstance. When I first came to your home, I intended to impart supreme knowledge to you. However, I noticed that at that time, your heart was longing for a son. Therefore, I withheld knowledge for the moment and granted you a son. Now, you are personally experiencing the agony that parents feel when they lose a son. The same principle applies to one’s wife, home, wealth, various luxuries, properties; the sense pleasures such as sound, form and taste; a kingdom, armies, treasury, servants, relatives and dear friends. Since they are impermanent, they are all the causes of sorrow, attachment, fear and distress. They are the toys of the mind, imaginary and false, because although they do not exist, they are visible. The reason is that although they are seen for a moment, the next moment they disappear. They are as unreal as a mirage in the desert, a city in the sky, dreams, magic or objects of fantasy. People driven by desires arising from the psychological impressions of their previous actions constantly contemplate worldly matters, then their minds initiate various activities. The body of the living entity, composed of the five elements (panca-bhuta), the knowledge-acquiring senses (jnanendriya) and the working senses (karmendriya), is the cause of various pains and sufferings for the soul. Therefore, you should restrain your mind from wandering into worldly matters; remain tranquil and content; and deliberate upon your true nature. Abandon the illusory notion that the forms you see are independent permanent entities, and become situated in awareness of the form of ultimate peace, Paramatma.”

Devarsi Narada said, “O King, with one-pointed attention, accept this mantra from me. By meditating on this mantra, you will have the divine vision of Lord Sankarsana within seven nights. In ancient times, Lord Siva and others took shelter of the lotus feet of Sri Sankarsana Deva. By this, they gave up their mistaken experience of duality (dvaita-bhrama) and attained unsurpassed magnificence. Soon, you too shall attain that supreme abode of Bhagavan.”

Then Devarsi Narada brought forth the soul of the deceased prince in front of his grieving relatives and said, “Soul, all auspiciousness unto you. Your father and mother and your relatives are overwhelmed with sorrow due to separation from you. Return to your body and complete the rest of your life by staying alongside your dear ones. Enjoy the pleasures bestowed upon you by your father and take your place on the royal throne.”

The soul responded, “Devarsi, in accordance with my actions, I have wandered through various births among gods, humans, animals and other life forms for countless lifetimes. Among all these lives, in which life were these people my parents? In all these different births, there were relatives, siblings, friends, enemies, allies, and neutral parties. Just as commodities like gold pass from one merchant to another in trade, similarly, souls are born in different life forms. If one reflects upon this, it becomes evident that the relationship of human beings with commodities like gold, which exists for more days than human beings, is not steady; rather, it is momentary. And as long as that relationship remains, then the human has a sense of possessiveness for the gold. The soul is eternal and devoid of material ego. Upon entering the womb and residing in a body, the soul considers that body as its own. However, the soul is eternal, indestructible, and subtle (without birth). It is the intermediate instrumental cause of the body and self-illuminating. Therefore, it has neither birth nor death. No one is dear to the soul or hated by the soul. No one belongs to the soul and no one is a stranger. This soul is the witness of the body and senses and it is dependent. Therefore, the soul does not identify with the faults and attributes of the body. The soul, not accepting the fruits of the actions of the body, always remains situated in a state of neutrality.”

After speaking in this way, the soul departed, leaving his relatives utterly astonished. Their emotional ties were severed and their sorrow over the child’s death faded away. The queens who had administered poison had become devoid of beauty due to the crime of murdering a child. Overwhelmed with shame, they could not even lift their eyes. Remembering the instructions of Angira Rsi, they became free from envy and, following the order of the brahmanas, performed atonement for the act of infanticide on the bank of the Yamuna River. King Citraketu’s power of discrimination had been awakened by the advice of Angira and Narada Rsi. He emerged from the blind well of household life just as an elephant emerges from a swamp. Narada Rsi saw that Citraketu had become victorious over his senses, devoted and surrendered to Bhagavan. With great satisfaction, Narada Rsi imparted further instructions to the king, then, accompanied by Angira Rsi, departed for the realm of Brahma.

King Citraketu applied himself with great concentration to the practice given by Devarsi Narada, consuming only water for seven days. As a result, he gained rulership over the Vidyadharas within seven nights. Within a few days, he experienced a vision of Bhagavan Sankarsana. Praising Lord Sankarsana, he exclaimed, “O Unconquerable One! You are conquered by those saints who have conquered their senses (jitendriya) and who have equal vision (samadarsi). You have also brought them under Your control by Your qualities such as beauty, sweetness and compassion. To those who worship You, desiring nothing in return (niskama-bhava), You give Yourself. You are the cause of all causes. You are unlimited. Those humans who desire only worldly pleasures are actually animals. Neglecting devotion to You, they worship the demigods such as Indra who are just minute aspects of Your material powers (vibhuti). When a royal dynasty comes to an end, so does the livelihood of its attendant courtiers. Similarly, when the demigods are destroyed, the benedictions given by them also perish. In bhagavata-dharma, the eternal religion of service to God, there is no room for any kind of ulterior motive (anyabhilasa). Therefore, even actions performed with selfish motivation (sakama-bhava), if they are directed towards You, do not result in repeated birth and death like ordinary actions, just as parched seeds do not sprout. О Bhagavan! Merely by seeing You, all the sins of humans are destroyed. This is not impossible because hearing Your Holy Name just once liberates even the lowest untouchable beings (candala) from this material world.”

Bhagavan Sahkarsana instructed Citraketu extensively and Citraketu, being detached from the sense objects, took those teachings into his heart with faith. Then Lord Sankarsana said to Citraketu, “By the knowledge about Me imparted by Devarsi Narada and Maharsi Angira, by mantra and by personally seeing Me, you have already attained the perfection of your practice. I am the Soul of all beings and their sustainer. When the living entities forget Me and consider themselves separate from Me, they become entangled in the cycle of the worldly existence, leading to repeated births and deaths. If you carefully follow this teaching with faith, being enriched with knowledge and realization, you will swiftly attain the perfection of My direct association.”

Now a great yogi, Citraketu sported for millions of years in the valleys of Mount Sumeru, where one can become perfect in all types of vows. He inspired the Vidyadhara women to continually sing about the virtues and pastimes of the omnipotent Bhagavan. One day, Citraketu boarded a radiant celestial airplane bestowed by the Lord and began to fly somewhere. At that moment, he saw a grand assembly of great sages. Lord Sankara was seated in the midst of a host of accomplished beings (siddha purusas). Parvati Devi was affectionately seated upon his lap and Sankara was embracing her with one hand. Witnessing this, Citraketu approached them on his celestial vehicle and, laughing so loudly that Parvati could hear, exclaimed, “Aho! Here is the religious instructor and spiritual master of the whole world. He is the greatest among all beings. His condition is such that he is sitting and clinging to his wife’s body in a grand assembly. Despite being the head of the assembly (sabhapati) of ascetics with matted locks and transcendentalists, he sits like an ordinary person with his wife on his lap without any sense of embarrassment or shame. Even common men mainly sit with women only in private. But this person, despite being such an observer of great vows (vratadhari), is sitting with his wife publicly in a grand assembly. The intellect of Lord Sankara is unfathomable.”

Hearing Citraketu’s insinuation, Sankara began to laugh but did not say anything. His followers seated in the assembly also remained silent. Why Citraketu spoke in this way about Mahadeva is beyond the understanding of common men. The great saints explain that Citraketu’s intention was to express that Lord Siva is a powerful controller (isvara) and a capable personality (samartha purusa). From the external perspective, even though it seems to be that Lord Siva is engaged in misconduct (suduracara), he is not harmed by this in any way. However, if a foolish person of feeble intelligence dares to criticize Lord Siva, he becomes an offender and brings about his own destruction, just as Daksa did by criticizing him. Considering such points, Citraketu spoke in this way to Lord Siva.

Lord Mahadeva considered that even though Citraketu’s language was somewhat harsh, he was a devotee of Lord Visnu. That is why Mahadeva did not become angry with him and even the followers of Siva remained silent. The purport is that if Citraketu had intended to criticize Lord Siva, the followers of Lord Siva would have covered their ears and left that place, but they did not.

For the sake of educating the public, Parvati Devi then enacted one drama. Inspired by Sankarsana Deva, she expressed anger towards Citraketu. She said, “His lotus feet are meditated upon even by great demigods like Lord Brahma. Will such a Lord who is to be worshipped by the whole world be disciplined by you? For this offence, you must be born into a sinful dynasty of demons so that you will never commit offences against saints again.”

Upon hearing Parvati Devi’s curse, Citraketu humbly descended from the celestial vehicle and said, “With great joy, I fold my hands and accept your curse.”

Citraketu was a devotee of the Lord. He was never subordinate to the reactions of karma. Devotees in whose hearts love (prema) has appeared are never bound by karma. They see curses, blessings, heaven, liberation and hell with equal vision. Lord Sankarsana was feeling the hunger of love caused by separation from Citraketu. Therefore, Lord Sankarsana in the heart of Parvati Devi inspired her to curse Citraketu as a pretext to end Citraketu’s long reign over the Vidyadharas and bring him quickly to Vaikuntha, so that He could bestow upon him the sweetness of service to His lotus feet.

Upon hearing the curse, Citraketu Maharaja was not even slightly fearful. Observing this, Lord Mahadeva said to Parvati Devi, “O beautiful one! Now you have witnessed with your own eyes the divine glories of the dispassionate and gracious servants of the servants of Bhagavan who enjoys divine pastimes. Those who have surrendered to the Lord never fear anyone because in heaven, in salvation, or in hell, they behold only one entity, Bhagavan, with the same love. I, Lord Brahma, the four Kumaras, Narada Rsi, Brahma’s son Bhrgu and the great demigods cannot comprehend the mysteries of Sri Bhagavan’s divine play. Under such circumstances, how can those who are but infinitesimal parts of the Lord and who consider themselves to be separate controllers even know about His divine form? This supremely fortunate Citraketu, Bhagavan’s dear servant, is peaceful and possessed of equal vision. I, too, am Bhagavan Sri Hari’s dear servant. Therefore, you should not be surprised in any way about the dear servants of the Lord who are peaceful great souls with equal vision.”

Bhagavan’s supremely loving devotee (parama-premi bhakta), Citraketu, could have indeed counter-cursed Parvati Devi in return. However, he chose not to retaliate. He respectfully accepted her curse upon his head. This exemplifies the conduct of a true saint. To teach the world the consequences of criticizing vaisnavas and to fulfil the words of Parvati Devi, Citraketu took birth as a demon from the fire sacrifice performed by Tvasta. His name was Vrtrasura in that life and he remained immersed in devotion and complete in knowledge of the spiritual form of Sri Bhagavan. Indra eventually killed Vrtrasura. After giving up his body, Vrtrasura became an eternal associate of Bhagavan Sankarsana Deva.

## Chapter 21 King Suyajna

In the country of Usinara, there was once a great and famous king named Suyajna. After he was killed by his enemies in battle, his brothers and relatives surrounded his body. They saw that his armour was shattered; his ornaments and garlands were scattered. His entire body was smeared with blood, his heart pierced by the arrows of the enemy. His hair was dishevelled and his eyes had become lustreless. At the last moment, he had bitten his lip in anger and his teeth remained in that position. His beautiful lotus-like face was now covered with dust, his arms were cut and his weapons were broken.

Upon seeing their husband in this condition, the queens were deeply saddened. They began to cry, “O lord, now that you have been killed, we also have been killed.” Pounding their breasts again and again, they fell at the feet of their master. They wept so much that their tears, reddened by mixing with the kunkuma on their breasts, fell upon the lotus feet of their beloved husband. With their hair and jewellery in disarray, they mourned piteously, evoking sympathy from the hearts of everyone.

They cried, “Alas! О Creator, you are so cruel! О my master, this cruel providence has removed you from our sight. Previously, you gave life to the residents of our nation. But today, providence has made you the cause of our increasing lamentation. О Husband, you loved us so much. You appreciated even the smallest service from us, considering it to be great. Alas! How can we live without you now? We remain at your feet. О best of heroes! Wherever you are going, give us permission to go there too.” They continued to grieve in this way, holding on to their husband’s body.

In the meantime, the sun set in the west. Hearing the lamentation of the king’s relatives, Yamaraja, the lord of death, appeared there in the disguise of a young boy. He approached them and said, “How surprising it is! These people are older than me and they have seen so many people living and dying, yet they are so foolish. The living beings come from an unknown place and return to that same unknown place. These people must also go to that same place one day or another. Why do they uselessly lament like this?”

He continued, “We are thousands of times better than you. We are fortunate because our parents abandoned us. Even though our bodies lack sufficient strength, we have no worries. Wolves and other ferocious animals cannot even touch our hair. The One who protected us in the womb continues to protect us in life. О respected ladies! By the will of the indestructible Supreme Lord, the entire universe is created, maintained and annihilated. It is His toy. He alone is competent to punish or reward the moving and non-moving creatures of the world. When fate is favourable, even objects fallen on the road remain untouched. However, when fate is unfavourable, even items kept securely in the safe at home are lost. By the merciful glance of God, one remains alive for many days without being harmed even in the jungle. Whereas, without the protection of God, some die although they are protected at home.

“O queens! Every living entity dies and is then reborn in accordance with the impressions of actions performed in his previous birth. However, the soul is entirely distinct from the body, so it remains untouched by the cycle of birth and death even though living within the body. Just as a person considers his house to be separate from himself and to be made of earth, similarly, this body also is separate from the soul (atma) and made of earth. Being under the control of bewilderment, the soul identifies with the body.

“Foam is formed by the transformation of water. A pitcher is formed from a transformation of clay. And jewellery is made by transforming gold with fire. In time, they gradually change their forms and then their forms are dissolved. Similarly, this body, formed from a transformation of the three elements, water, earth and fire, also appears, changes and disintegrates over time.

“Fire resides inside wood and pervades it, but fire is distinct from wood. Air exists within the body, but has no direct relationship with the body. Although space (akasa) exists uniformly everywhere, it is not affected by anyone’s faults or virtues. In the same way, the soul resides within and is the shelter of the body and senses, but it remains separate from them.

“O fools! The one for whom all of you are weeping, that body named Suyajna, is right in front of you. You used to see this body only. The one who used to listen and speak through it was never seen by anyone. Again today, that entity is not visible. So why lament? You believe that the life-force (prana) was the one speaking and listening and now that prana has departed. This idea is completely foolish because during deep sleep (susupti) the life-force remains, yet it neither speaks nor hears. Even though the great prana element is the chief cause of all the activities of the senses in the body, it is not the speaker or the listener because it is insentient (jada). The seer of all objects through utilization of the body and senses is the soul (atma). The soul is distinct from both the body and the life-force.

“In a jungle, there lived a hunter who would set traps wherever he lived and lure birds with bait to capture them. One day he saw a pair of kulinga birds eating the bait. The hunter quickly captured the female bird in his trap. Witnessing the plight of his partner, the male bird felt immense sorrow. He was unable to free her from the net. Helplessly, he began to lament out of affection for the female bird. He said, ‘The Creator can do everything, yet He is so merciless. Firstly, my companion is female. And secondly, she is fluttering about in great humility lamenting for wretched me. After taking her away from here, what will He do with her? She may die or He may take me. Without her, what will this incomplete widower do with his life, which has become lowly and filled with misery? How will I raise these children without a mother?’

“While he was thus deeply engrossed in sorrow, time (kala) inspired the hunter, who was hiding nearby, to shoot an arrow that immediately killed the male bird. О foolish queens, your condition is exactly the same. You do not foresee your own death and you keep crying over your husband’s death. Even if you continue beating your chest like this for a hundred years, you will not get him back.”

After narrating this tale, Yamaraja disappeared. The brothers and relatives also calmed their grief and performed the final rites for King Suyajna. Therefore, an intelligent person, without mourning for the inevitably perishable body, will seek out the eternal devotional service of Sri Krsna.

## Chapter 22 Prahlada

The demigods were unsettled by the tyranny of Hiranyakasipu. The demon had conquered the three worlds and enslaved all beings. He established his authority over the realm of Devaraja Indra and began to enjoy there. Except for Lord Brahma, Lord Visnu, and Lord Siva, all the other guardians of the worlds worshipped him and pleased him by offering gifts.

Previously, Hiranyakasipu had set out to Mandaracala mountain to execute severe austerities. In his absence, Indra made huge arrangements for an invasion. When the demons found out, they began to flee here and there. Then, the demigods destroyed Hiranyakasipu’s palace and captured his pregnant queen, Kayadhu.

As Indra was leaving, he met Devarsi Narada. Narada stopped Indra and said, “It is not appropriate to abduct the innocent queen because she is another man’s wife and she is a virtuous lady.”

Indra replied, “Until the demon child in Kayadhu’s womb is born, I will keep her with me and protect her. After she gives birth, I will kill the demon child and let Kayadhu go.”

Hearing this, Narada said, “The child growing in Kayadhu’s womb does not possess the nature of a demon like Hiranyakasipu. He is a sinless, great saint (maha-bhagavata) and an associate of the allpowerful Lord Visnu. No one can kill him.” Hearing these words from the mouth of Devarsi Narada, Indra released Kayadhu.

At that time, Hiranyakasipu was engaged in severe austerities in the valley of the Mandaracala mountain. Therefore, Devarsi Narada told Kayadhu to stay at his hermitage.

When Kayadhu took up residence at the hermitage of the best of vaisnavas, Narada, he began to instruct the pregnant mother on the principle truth of Bhagavan with the aim of teaching the child in her womb. Prahlada, while still in the womb, began to hear transcendental discourses about the Absolute Truth (tattva-katha) just like Sukadeva Gosvami.

Meanwhile, Hiranyakasipu, the demon king, underwent severe penance and finally obtained a boon from Lord Brahma that he could not be killed by any being created by Brahma, whether human, animal or non-animal, demigod or demon, or even by weaponry, in the day or night, indoors or outside, on land or in the sky. No one could confront him in battle. He would become the sovereign ruler of all beings, surpassing even the guardians of the worlds. His glory would be like Brahma’s own glory. He would become the master of all mystic powers, like the ascetics and yogis. Hiranyakasipu, having received the boons, took Kayadhu from Naradajfs hermitage and returned to his abode. It was there that Prahlada was born, and he grew day by day like the moon.

Right from childhood, Prahlada would forego playing games for the sake of immersing himself in meditation on Bhagavan. By the grace of Bhagavan Sri Krsna, his heart was so captivated that he remained absorbed, unaware of the happenings of the external world. He experienced the touch of Bhagavan taking him in His lap and embracing him. He gave no attention to eating, drinking, walking, sitting, or conversing. Sometimes, he would cry loudly; sometimes, feeling joy in the core of his heart, he would begin to laugh. Sometimes, he would begin to sing loudly, and at times, impelled by eagerness, he would shout. Sometimes, disregarding worldly opinion, Prahlada would dance without inhibition. By the influence of the association of pure devotees of Bhagavan, he remained immersed in the joy of love, serving the lotus feet of Lord Sri Krsna. Even those wretched souls who were fallen due to bad association attained loyalty and love for Bhagavan in Prahlada’s company.

Sukracarya was the priest of the demons. He was occupied elsewhere in his priestly duties. He had two sons, Sanda and Amarka. In their father’s absence, they were tasked with educating the demon children at his residence near the royal palace. They taught Prahlada and other deserving demon children subjects such as politics (rajaniti) and economics (arthaniti). Prahlada used to listen to their teachings and recite the teachings back to them word-for-word. However, he never found the teaching appealing because it was based on the dualistic mentality of thinking, “This person is my friend and this person is my enemy.”

One day, Hiranyakasipu, with great affection, took his son Prahlada in his lap and asked, “Son, tell me honestly, what is the best subject you have learned?”

Prahlada replied, “O best of the asuras (demons), the living entities in this world are full of anxieties due to always being caught in the false conception of T and ‘mine’. Their homes are just like a blind well covered with grass. I understand that such living beings should leave their homes, which are the root cause of their fallen state, go to the forest and take shelter of Lord Sri Hari.”

Upon hearing words of praise for the enemy’s side from his son, Hiranyakasipu laughed. He said, “The boy’s mind has been corrupted by someone else’s influence. I know that some supporters of Visnu reside at the guru’s place disguised as brahmanas. The child should be carefully supervised so that his intelligence is not destroyed.”

When the demons took Prahlada back to the guru’s house, the priests showed affection. Cuddling him and coaxing him, they inquired with sweet words, “Son Prahlada, tell us truthfully; don’t lie. How did your mind change like this? The other children’s minds are not like this. О  joy of your dynasty! Prahlada! Tell us dear son. We, your teachers, want to know whether your mind changed this way by itself or whether someone has really misled you.”

Prahlada replied, “Those humans whose intelligence is bewildered by the Lord’s illusory energy, Maya, falsely perceive that this is ‘my friend’ and this is ‘my enemy’. I offer my obeisances to that Lord, who is the master of Maya.”

The priests were subordinate servants of the king. They rebuked Prahlada and said to their disciples, “Someone bring my stick. His disobedience is tarnishing our reputation. To rectify this foolishness, the fourth method, punishment (danda), will be appropriate. In the sandalwood forest of the Daitya dynasty, from where did this thorn tree take birth? One can make the handle of an axe from a thorn tree. Visnu is like an axe and this simple child is acting like the handle. He is helping Visnu to cut the roots of the sandalwood forest of the Daitya dynasty.”

In this way, the teachers scolded and threatened Prahlada. Again, they instructed him on various aspects of economics, morality, and sense enjoyment. After some time, when the teachers saw that Prahlada had become well-versed in the political tactics of reconciliation, bribery, sowing division, and punishment, they took him to Hiranyakasipu. Prahlada bowed down before his father. Being pleased, Hiranyakasipu lifted Prahlada into his lap and caressed him affectionately for a long time. Finally, Hiranyakasipu asked Prahlada, “Tell me, of all the teachings you received from the guru during this time, what pleased you the most?”

Prahlada said, “There are nine forms of devotional service to Lord Visnu. Hearing (fravanam), chanting (kirtanam) and remembering (smaranam) the names, forms, qualities and pastimes of the Lord, serving His lotus feet (pada-sevanam), worshipping (arcanam), offering prayers (vandanam), servitude (dasyam), friendship (sakhyam) and offering one’s self completely to Him (atma-nivedanam). In my opinion, these nine forms of devotion, if performed in the mood of complete surrender, constitute the supreme study.”

On hearing Prahlada’s words, Hiranyakasipu became infuriated and his lips began to tremble with anger. He said to his guru’s sons, “You lowly brahmanas! What kind of mischief is this? О wicked ones! Not heeding my orders, what a wonderful education you have given this boy. It is certain that you have taken shelter of our enemies. You are enemies in the guise of friends.”

The guru's sons replied, “Whatever your son is saying is not influenced by us or by anyone else. Your Majesty, it is his natural inclination from birth.”

Hiranyakasipu then asked his son, “O destroyer of our dynasty! If you did not acquire this harmful and wicked intelligence from the mouth of a guru, then tell me, from where did you get it?”

Prahlada said, “All the persons who have made the vow to live and die at home are heading towards the dreadful darkness of hell because their senses are uncontrolled. Like ruminant animals, they are chewing that which has already been chewed, that is, they repeatedly chew the same happiness and distress that was tasted by their materially bound predecessors. The consciousness of such attached householders is never absorbed in Bhagavan Sri Krsna, either by their own efforts or by the association of others who are like them.

“Persons who consider the external objects perceived by the senses to be supremely desirable are blind. They are just like blind men who, out of foolishness, follow other blind men and fall into a ditch. They are bound by the ropes of the rituals of self-interest (kamya-karma) described in the Vedas. Thus, they are unaware that our ultimate self-interest and the goal of life lies in Lord Visnu. By attaining Him, one attains the fulfilment of all human endeavours. For those whose intellect is touched by the lotus feet of Bhagavan, the unwanted impediment of the cycle of birth and death comes to an end. Even for those who are fully engaged in Vedic kamya-karma, unless they bathe in the foot dust of the great souls who love the Lord and who are free from material possessiveness (niskincana), they cannot attain the touch of the lotus feet of the Lord because the foot dust of the great devotees is the birthplace of devotion to Sri Krsna.”

Hiranyakasipu, blinded by rage, lifted Prahlada out of his lap and threw him to the ground. He could not tolerate Prahlada’s words. His eyes turned red with anger and he ordered, “Demons! Take him out of here and kill him immediately! He deserves to be killed. Giving up the love of his own kin, he worships the feet of Visnu like a lowly servant. It is quite possible that Visnu, who killed my brother, has come in the form of this boy. Now he is not trustworthy. At the age of five, he has forgotten the affection of his mother and father. How can such an ungrateful person even do any good for Visnu?”

When commanded by Hiranyakasipu, the demons, with their sharp teeth, terrifying faces, red hair and beards and holding tridents in their hands, shouted loudly and screamed, “Kill him! Cut him!” Prahlada sat silently while the demons attacked all his vital organs with their tridents. At that moment, Prahlada’s heart (citta) was fixed on the Supreme Lord, who is beyond the mind and speech, who is the Soul of all and the omnipotent Supreme Brahman. Therefore, all their assaults had no effect, just as the vast industrial enterprises of an unfortunate person go in vain.

When the assault with tridents had no effect on Prahlada’s body, Hiranyakasipu grew deeply suspicious. He stubbornly began to employ various means to eliminate Prahlada. Hiranyakasipu tried to have Prahlada trampled by intoxicated elephants, bitten by poisonous snakes and killed by a man-eating demoness (krtya-raksasi) invoked by his priests. He was thrown from the peaks of high mountain cliffs. Hiranyakasipu employed Sambarasura to use many types of illusions. Prahlada was locked in dark dungeons, deprived of food and made to drink poison. Yet Hiranyakasipu could not cause the slightest harm to Prahlada because Prahlada always kept only Sri Hari sitting in his heart.

When Hiranyakasipu discovered that he could not harm Prahlada by any means, he became extremely worried. Sukracarya’s sons, Sanda and Amarka, saw Hiranyakasipu hanging his head in anxiety. They approached him in private and said, “Master! You have single-handedly conquered the three realms. When you merely raise your eyebrow, the demigods protecting the universe tremble with fear. In our opinion, you have no reason to worry. Until our father Sukracarya returns, ensure he does not escape. Bind him with Varuna’s ropes. As a general rule, the intellect improves as one grows up and serves one’s seniors.”

Following Hiranyakasipu’s orders, the guru’s sons continued to instruct Prahlada about the three human objectives: religiosity (dharma), economic prosperity (artha), and enjoyment (kama). However, Prahlada did not like this instruction from the guru’s sons because this teaching is only for those who take pleasure in the dualities of attachment (raga) and aversion (dvesa) and in sensual enjoyment.

One day, the teachers were away on some duties. All the children called for Prahlada to play because it was their school vacation. Prahlada was extremely wise. Seeing the love of the other children, with sweet words he invited them to join him. The destination of their birth and death was not hidden from him. Being merciful to them, he began to laugh and give them some advice. They were all children, so their minds had not become polluted by the words and actions of hedonistic men absorbed in attachment and aversion. Out of respect for Prahlada, they abandoned their playthings and approached him. They sat around him on all four sides and attentively listened to his teachings.

Prahlada said, “Friends! Human birth in this world is very rare. In this human birth one can attain the imperishable Supreme Lord. However, we don’t know when its end may come. Therefore, a wise person does not assume he will live to a ripe old age or trust in his youthfulness. Right from childhood one should engage in the practices by which one can attain Bhagavan. In this human birth, taking shelter of the lotus feet of Sri Bhagavan is the only success in human life because He is the master, friend, beloved and soul of all beings. The pleasures enjoyed by the senses are automatically available in every species of life as the reactions to past activities (prarabdha-karma), just as one receives suffering automatically without any effort, although one tries to avoid it. Therefore, there is no need to strive for worldly pleasures because exerting effort for something that we receive automatically is a waste of life and energy. Those entangled in such efforts do not attain the ultimate welfare in the form of service to the Supreme Lord. We are beleaguered by many types of fear. Therefore, as long as this body, which is sufficient for attaining the Lord, is not entering the mouth of death in a state of disease and grief, an intelligent person should strive for his own ultimate benefit.

“A human’s full lifespan is one hundred years. But half of it is spent immersed in profound ignorance while sleeping at night. During childhood, humans have no awareness of what is beneficial or harmful. As they grow older, they become engrossed in playing, so much so that the first twenty years seem to pass unnoticed. In the final twenty years when old age surrounds them, they do not even have the energy to accomplish anything. Just a small portion of time is left between youth and old age. And during that time also, the soul has so many strong desires that are never fulfilled, that is, he is in the grip of infatuation and attachment to his family and home. The soul becomes so entangled that he does not know what to do and what not to do. In this way, even this remaining portion of life also slips from his hand. What person, who has not controlled his senses, who is attached to household life, and who is ensnared in the strong noose of illusory possessiveness, can have the courage to free himself?

“Thieves, servants and businessmen gather possessions by risking their own lives. Therefore, they consider possessions to be even more desirable than their lives. So, who among them can renounce the thirst for wealth?

“One who has already surrendered himself to the affectionate words and sweet advice of his dear-most wife in the secluded bedchamber; who is already bound by the bonds of affection of his brother, relatives and friends; who is already charmed by the broken words of his small children - how can he possibly give them up?

“How could one abandon those beloved daughters who have gone to their in-laws’ homes, or his sons, siblings, and parents who are helpless in old age? He is continuously immersed in thoughts about his home, beautifully adorned with valuable possessions, inherited properties and businesses, domestic animals and servants. How could he possibly abandon them?

“He considers the pleasures of the tongue and the genitals to be everything. His desires to enjoy are never satisfied. Just as a silkworm binds himself in his own cocoon, while performing actions upon actions driven by greed, he becomes more deeply entangled in material bondage and his bewilderment knows no limits. How can he become detached and give up all these desires?

“He thinks, ‘This is my family,’ and becomes so absorbed in that emotion that he loses his whole precious lifespan just in sustaining and nurturing them. He fails to realize that the true aim of his life is being ruined and his life is going in all other directions. There, his heart burns with the heat of the threefold miseries - miseries arising from the body and mind, miseries arising from other living entities and miseries arising from the natural environment. Yet still detachment does not arise. How ironic it is! Being caught up in possessiveness for family, he becomes so careless. Despite being aware of the consequences of stealing in this life, punishment by the king and in the next life, punishment by Yama, his mind is so preoccupied with thoughts of wealth that he cannot control his desires. Out of greed for sense enjoyment, he succumbs to temptation and just continues to steal the wealth of others.

“Brothers! Those who are busy just in feeding the bellies of their family members can never engage in devotional service. Even if they are learned scholars, they cannot attain the Supreme Soul, Paramatma, due to discriminating between friends and enemies. Those who have become playthings in the hands of women, whose feet are firmly held by the shackles of their offspring, no matter how poor they may be, regardless of their status or situation, cannot liberate themselves in any way. Therefore, brothers, abandon all desires to associate with the materially attached demons and take shelter of the lotus feet of the primordial Lord Narayana, because for those great souls who have renounced attachment to the world, He is their most beloved and ultimate goal.

“Friends, to please the Lord, one does not need to undergo excessive toil or effort because He is the Soul of all living beings and present everywhere as the self-existent reality and the very existence of all. Therefore, relinquish your demoniac tendencies and possessions and show compassion to all living beings. This itself will please the Supreme Lord. When Lord Narayana, the eternal Lord, is pleased, what is there that cannot be achieved? When we partake in the nectar of the Lord’s lotus feet (caranamrta) and sing His names and glories, what is the need for liberation? This pure knowledge I have imparted to you is indeed very rare. Previously, Bhagavan Narayana imparted this teaching to Sri Narada, and I have heard all these teachings from Sri Narada’s lotus mouth. This knowledge can reach all those who have bathed their bodies with the dust of the lotus feet of the material possessionless (akincana) and loving (premi) devotees of Bhagavan. This knowledge, along with its realization, is the pure bhagavata-dharma.”

Upon hearing Prahlada’s sweet and heart-touching discourse, being offenceless, the boys immediately grasped his words. They no longer paid attention to the corrupt instructions of their teachers. Seeing that all the students’ minds were focused solely on Bhagavan, the teachers became very anxious and immediately went to appeal to Hiranyakasipu. When Hiranyakasipu heard about the intolerable and unpleasant misconduct of his son, Prahlada, he began to tremble with rage. Finally, he decided that he should kill Prahlada with his own hands. Prahlada, whose mind and senses were controlled, humbly folded his hands and stood silently in front of Hiranyakasipu.

Hiranyakasipu glared at him with sinful eyes and scolded him with harsh words, “Fool! You have become too audacious. You are personally dishonourable and now you are trying to mislead the other children of our lineage. Today, I will send you to the abode of Yama and make you taste the fruit of your actions. The three worlds and their guardian demigods tremble at the sight of my slight displeasure. Imbecile! You act as if fearless. On whose strength do you defy me?”

Prahlada said, “O King of demons. From Lord Brahma down to the smallest blade of grass, all big and small, moving and non-moving beings are under the control of Bhagavan, the Supreme Lord. Not only mine and yours, but the strength of all the powerful beings in the world comes only from that mighty omnipotent Lord. He is time itself and the strength of the senses, mind, body, and patience of all living entities. That same Supreme Lord creates, preserves, and destroys this universe through His potencies. He is the Master of the three modes of material nature. Give up this demonic nature of yours. Equilibrate your mind towards everyone. There is no enemy in this world besides one’s own uncontrolled mind.”

Hiranyakasipu said, “O slow-witted one! Your proselytizing has now reached the limit of its effectiveness. You claim that someone other than me is the Lord of the world. So, show me! Where is that Lord of the universe of which you speak? If He is omnipresent, why is He not visible in this stone pillar? I will now separate your head from your body. You have so much trust in your Hari. How will He save you now?” In this way, the extremely powerful demon king was constantly threatening Prahlada, Bhagavan’s loving devotee.

When Hiranyakasipu could no longer restrain his anger, he grabbed a sword, descended from the throne and forcefully struck the stone pillar. At that moment, a terrifying sound emanated from the pillar, as if the universe itself was being torn apart. When that sound reached the realms of the guardians of the world, the demigods, headed by Lord Brahma, felt as though the time for universal annihilation had arrived. Hiranyakasipu, upon hearing the terrifying and unprecedented sound, became somewhat nervous about killing Prahlada. He began to look around wondering, “Who is making this sound?”

At that very moment, to uphold the words of His servant Prahlada and of Lord Brahma, and to demonstrate that He pervades all things, assuming a wonderful form, Lord Nrsimhadeva manifested from the stone pillar in the assembly. This form was not fully that of a lion, a human or any other animal. As Hiranyakasipu was in a state of confusion, wondering, “What extraordinary living being is this Nrsimha (man-lion)?”, at that very moment, Lord Nrsimha appeared standing right in front of him. His form was extremely fearsome. His eyes were shining like molten gold. His teeth were terrifying. Hiranyakasipu began to think, “Definitely, Visnu, who is beyond the great illusion (Mahamaya), has created a game to kill me.”

Thinking in this way, the king of the Daityas roared like a lion and charged at Lord Nrsimha wielding a mace in his hand. However, just as a moth falls into fire and disappears from view, he entered the divine effulgence of Bhagavan and vanished. Such an event is not at all amazing for Bhagavan, who is the shelter of all divine powers and effulgence, because at the beginning of creation, the Lord dispelled by His effulgence the dense darkness of tamo-guna, which had been the cause of the annihilation (pralaya). In a fit of rage, Hiranyakasipu swung his mace to inflict a forceful blow upon Lord Nrsimha. But just as Garuda swiftly captures a snake, the Lord seized Hiranyakasipu along with his mace. Bhagavan took him to the doorway and dropped him across His thighs. As if playing in a game, Lord Nrsimha tore him apart with His sharp nails, just as Garuda rips apart a venomous snake. With His sharp nails, Lord Nrsimha tore apart Hiranyakasipu’s chest, ripped out his heart and slammed him onto the ground. At that moment, thousands of heavily armed demons began to attack the Lord. However, the Lord with His own army of kicks, sharp nails and other weapons in His many hands struck out in all four directions at once, killing them all.

Although the demons had been destroyed and no one could see anyone confronting Lord Nrsirhha, His rage was still escalating. He sat on the high throne in Hiranyakasipu’s royal court. Seeing His blazing rage and terrifying face, no one had the courage to approach and serve Him. The sky became crowded with the celestial vehicles of the demigods who had come to see the Lord. The demigods began to beat their drums and the Gandharvas began to sing. Brahma, Indra, Siva, and various demigods, sages, forefathers, Siddhas, Vidyadharas, Gandharvas, Kinnaras, and all the attendants of the Lord such as Sunanda and Kumuda arrived there. They all offered prayers glorifying Lord Nrsimhadeva but they could not pacify His anger. When Goddess LaksmI Devi witnessed this astonishing form of Lord Nrsirhha, She was afraid to approach Him. At that moment, Brahma instructed Prahlada, “Child Prahlada, it is for your sake that the Lord has appeared in this form. Go to Him and pacify Him.” Prahlada, the best devotee of the Lord, approached Him with folded hands and prostrated on the ground before Him. When Bhagavan saw the little child prostrating at His feet, His heart filled with compassion. He lifted Prahlada and placed His lotus hand upon his head. Instantly, Prahlada fully realized the paramatma-tattva.

Prahlada said, “I understand that wealth, birth in an aristocratic family, beauty, austerities, erudition, sensory power, splendour, influence, fame, strength, prowess, intelligence and yoga are not capable of satisfying the Supreme Lord. But through devotion, even Gajendra pleased the Lord. According to my understanding, if a brahmana endowed with these twelve qualities is indifferent to the lotus feet of the Lord, then a person born in a family of dog-eaters (candala), who has dedicated his mind, speech, actions, wealth and life to Bhagavan’s lotus feet, is superior. The candala purifies his lineage, whereas the proud brahmana fails to purify even himself.

“The demon with whom You were angry is already dead. Now, please pacify Your anger. Everyone is waiting to see Your peaceful form. О Lord Nrsimhadeva! The devotees will remember this form of Yours to become free from fear. О Dina-bandhu, friend of the destitute! I am only afraid of the intolerable and ferocious grinding wheel of material existence. I am trapped among these dreadful creatures by the reactions of my own deeds (karma). My Lord, when will You graciously call me to those lotus feet that are the sole refuge and form of liberation for all beings? Master, please show me a means by which I can attain devotional service to You. You are dear to us, and our unconditional friend and well-wisher. Truly, You are the ultimate object of worship for all. I will easily cross beyond the hardships of this world and become free from the bindings of material qualities such as attachment by singing the glories of Your divine pastimes. This will be possible because I will constantly receive the company of the great swan-like liberated saints (paramahamsas) who reside at Your lotus feet.

“O Bhagavan Nrsimha! Whatever is considered a remedy to alleviate the distress of the suffering living entities in this world provides relief only for a moment if You are neglected. Moreover, parents cannot protect their children. Medicine cannot cure an ailment and a boat cannot save a man from drowning in the ocean. О Lord, no one can protect a person neglected by You. Only those upon whom Your grace falls can be safeguarded.

“O Lord of Vaikuntha! My mind is in a dire predicament. Being polluted by sinful desires, it is instinctively wicked. My mind mainly remains afflicted by various desires, and thus it does not derive taste (rasa) from the discussion of Your pastimes. Therefore, I remain spiritually impoverished. How can I contemplate Your form with such a mind?

“О Acyuta, infallible one! The tongue constantly pulls me towards delicious tastes, the genitals towards attractive women, the skin towards soft touch, the belly towards palatable food, the ears towards sweet music, the nose towards sweet fragrances, and the eyes towards beauty. My condition is like that of a man with many wives, each trying to drag him in different directions to their respective bedrooms.

“In this way, the soul, bound by the reactions of his own actions, has fallen into the Vaitarani River of birth and death. My Lord, great sages and ascetics mainly go to a secluded forest and observe the vow of silence for the sake of attaining their own liberation. They do not make any particular effort for the welfare of others. But I do not want to be liberated alone, forsaking these forgotten and helpless poor souls. Besides You, I see no other recourse for these wandering living beings.

“For those entangled in their homes, the pleasure derived from indulging in sexual acts is insignificant and leads to sorrow. It is like scratching an itch with both hands, which initially gives slight relief, but later, leads to nothing but the pain of further inflammation. Despite suffering so much, ignorant people forget this and continue to repeat the same activities. In contrast, the sober person tolerates the itching sensation. Only by tolerating is the condition eradicated.

“O Purusottama, Supreme Person! There are ten renowned means to attain liberation: practising silence, celibacy, hearing the scriptures, asceticism, self-study, observing one’s duties, explaining scriptural reason, seclusion, repetition of mantra (japa) and trance (samadhi). However, these are only a means of livelihood for one who is not in control of his senses. As long as such an arrogant and ostentatious person’s secrets are not exposed, these practices remain his means of sustenance, and once exposed they are not even that.

“О Arhattama! О Most Revered One! There are six limbs of devotional service to You: offering obeisances, offering prayers, offering all of one’s actions, worship, contemplation of Your lotus feet and listening to narrations of Your divine pastimes. How can one attain devotion to Your lotus feet without these six types of service? How will one attain You without devotion? О Lord, You are the all-in-all of Your dear devotees.”

Nrsimha Bhagavan was pleased by Prahlada’s prayers and His anger was pacified. He told Prahlada to ask for any boon he desired. However, the exemplary devotee, Prahlada, knew well that sometimes the Lord cheats the living entities by granting boons such as liberation, whereas He keeps faith and unconditional devotion to Himself extremely hidden and protected. Thus, requesting benedictions from the Lord is an obstacle to pure love (prema-bhakti). Considering this, Prahlada said, “Lord, I have been attached to worldly pleasures since birth. Now, kindly do not entice me by these boons offered by You. Out of fear of those pleasures and having experienced the intense pain they cause, I have taken shelter of You with the hope of being rid of them. Bhagavan! By instigating me to request a boon, You are testing to see whether or not I have the qualities of a devotee. Poisonous material enjoyments tighten the knot within the heart and cast one perpetually into the cycle of birth and death. О Nrsimhadeva, You are supremely compassionate. How can You grant a boon that entangles Your devotee in worldly enjoyment?

“One who wants to fulfil his own desires by serving his master is not a real servant. He is a merchant. One who becomes a master to receive service in return for fulfilling the desires of the servant is not a real master. I am Your desireless servant (niskama-sevaka), and You are my absolutely independent master (nirapeksa-svami). О best of all benefactors! If You wish to grant me a benediction, then bestow upon me the boon that no seed of material desire will ever sprout in my heart.”

Nrsimhadeva was exceedingly pleased by Prahladas words. Just as a lion shows ferocious power to others but gentle affection to his lion cubs, similarly, Nrsimha Bhagavan shows ferocious power to the demons such as Hiranyakasipu but gentle affection to His dear devotees like Prahlada.        Everyone, from young children to elderly persons, can receive valuable instructions from the life history and character of Prahlada Maharaja. Prahlada is the ideal example of a pure devotee. He never desired anything else but unconditional loving service to the Lord. The pure devotees do not even desire peace or liberation or anything else. Maintaining such desires is the mentality of merchants. Prahlada Maharaja has taught us what we should request from Bhagavan.

*natha yoni-sahasresu yesu yesu vrajamy aham*  
*tesu tesv acyuta bhaktir acyute’stu sada tvayi*  
*ya pritir avivekanam visayesv anapayini*  
*tvam anusmaratah sa me hrdayan mapasarpatu*

*Visnu Purana 1.20.18-19*

**О    Acyuta! О infallible Lord! As I wander through thousands upon thousands of births, in whichever species of life I may be born, may I always have unwavering devotion to You. Just as ignorant persons devoid of discrimination are attached to the sense objects with one-pointed attention, may there be such irrevocable love (priti) in my heart which constantly remembers You.**

The primary lesson from the life of Prahlada Maharaja is complete self-surrender (atma-nivedana). Hiranyakasipu was the king of the demon clan. He had no lack of wealth and manpower. Moreover, he even had the power derived from penance (tapobala). No demigod, human, yaksa, raksasa, or even any being created by Lord Brahma could kill him. He had received such boons that the three worlds were subordinate to him. He lacked no powers or prosperity. However, Prahlada was just a boy. He had nothing but surrender (saranagati) to the Lord. Pride and power could not save Hiranyakasipu. Prahlada’s surrender was victorious. Bhagavan protects those who are surrendered to Him. His devotees cannot be destroyed. Prahlada’s life is a vivid example of this truth. Prahlada Maharaja has stated:

*manye tad etad akhilam nigamasya satyam*  
*svatmarpanam sva-suhrdah paramasya pumsah*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 7-6.26*

Surrender to the Supreme Lord, Sri Visnu, is the ultimate truth. Everything else is transient and false.

## Chapter 23 Maharaja Bali

In the house of Prajapati Kasyapa, on the day of Sravana-dvadasi, the lotus-eyed Lord Sri Hari appeared in the lap of Sri Aditi Devi. He was holding a conch, disc, mace and lotus, and was dressed in yellow garments. Then, in the presence of Kasyapa and Aditi, the Lord assumed the form of Vamana, a dwarf brahmana. This twelfth lunar day is renowned as Vijaya-dvadasi. The great sages performed His birthday ceremony (jata-karma sathskara). When His sacred thread ceremony (upanayana) commenced, Suryadeva, the sun-god and presiding deity of the Gayatri mantra, personally imparted knowledge of Gayatri to Him. The guru of the demigods Brhaspati provided the sacred thread. Kasyapa offered the straw belt and Mother Earth gave Him a deerskin. The lord of the forest Candra (Moon) presented a staff (danda), while mother Aditi offered a cloth for underwear (kaupina). The deity presiding over the heavenly kingdom offered Him an umbrella. Lord Brahma offered a water pot, the seven sages offered Him kusa grass, Goddess Sarasvati gave Him a rosary of Rudraksa beads, and the revered Goddess Uma, the crest-jewel of chaste ladies and the mother of the universe, offered alms.

King Bali, the illustrious grandson of Prahlada, was performing an asvamedha yajna (sacrifice) on the northern bank of the Narmada River at a place named Bhrgukaccha. Brahmanas of the Bhrgu lineage were officiating this grand ceremony. Lord Vamana came to the arena of that very sacrifice. The brahmanas and King Bali welcomed Lord Vamana with great reverence. King Bali offered Him a seat, washed His lotus feet and worshipped Him by offering various paraphernalia. The water that cleanses the lotus feet of the Lord is supremely auspicious. It washes away all the sins and sufferings of the living beings. The best of the demigods, Lord Sankara, whose crest is decorated by the moon, always bears this water (Ganges) upon his head with the utmost devotion. Today, that sacred water was attained by King Bali, who knew its nectarean essence. With great love, he kept it upon his head. Glorifying Vamanadeva, King Bali said, “Your arrival is most auspicious. I bow down to You. Please command me. How may I serve You? Your presence in my abode has satisfied my forefathers. My dynasty has become sanctified today. You hold a begging bowl in Your hand, so it seems that You desire something. You can ask me for whatever You desire.”

Upon hearing Bali’s words, Lord Vamana replied, “O King, your words befit the traditions of your lineage. Enhancing your fame, these words are sweet and filled with the spirit of righteous conduct. And why not? After all, you hold Sukracarya, the son of Bhrgu, in high esteem as the supreme authority in matters of religious principle (dharma) which bestow benefit in the next world. At the same time, you also respect the orders of the esteemed head of your family, your grandfather, the supremely serene Prahlada Maharaja. In your lineage, there has never been an impatient or miserly person. Never has there been anyone who has not given alms to a brahmana or who has reneged on a promise to give. О King, your father Virocana, the son of Prahlada, was very devoted to the brahmanas. He was so dedicated that, despite knowing about the deceit of his rivals, the demigods, he even offered the alms of his lifespan upon their request. You have taken birth in that very dynasty. Among charitable persons, you are the greatest. Therefore, I request from you only three steps of land measured by My own paces. I understand that you are the ruler of the whole world and that you are very generous. Still, I don’t want more than this. Learned men should only accept alms in proportion to their needs. By this, they are saved from the sin of over-accepting charity.”

Hearing this request for only three paces of land, Bali Maharaja repeatedly petitioned Vamana Bhagavan to ask for more land and resources. However, Lord Vamanadeva replied, “O King! All the desirable sense objects in the whole world cannot satisfy the desires of even one person if he is not content to control his senses. If a person is not content with just three steps of land, then he cannot be satisfied with one whole island (dvipa) consisting of nine regions (varsas) because the desire to possess all the seven islands will continue to linger in his mind. I  have heard that kings such as Prthu and Gaya achieved sovereignty over the seven islands. However, even upon obtaining such wealth and material pleasures, they could not find the end of their thirst for more. A person who remains content with whatever he receives according to his destiny (prarabdha) leads a life of contentment. But one who cannot control his senses remains unhappy, even after gaining control over the three worlds, because the flames of dissatisfaction continue to blaze within his heart. Discontent with one’s wealth and pleasures causes the soul to continue in the cycle of birth and death.”

Bali Maharaja responded to Lord Vamana, “That is all well and good. Take only as much as You desire.” And with a smile, he picked up a water pot to finalize the vow (sankalpa) to give three steps of land. Sukracarya knew everything. This divine pastime of Bhagavan was not hidden from him either.

Seeing King Bali ready to give away the earth, Sukracarya cautioned him, “Son of Virocana! This is none other than the eternal Lord Visnu Himself. He has come here to accomplish the mission of the demigods. Without realizing it, you have pledged to give alms to that very person who is here to take everything away from you. The Supreme Lord Himself, by His internal potency, has appeared as a brahmana boy and is seated here. Your kingdom, opulence, fortune, influence and fame - everything - will be snatched away from you and given to Indra. He is the universal form. He will encompass the three worlds within His three steps. Fool! When you give away everything to Lord Visnu, how will you sustain your livelihood? Wise men do not praise the charity after which nothing is left to maintain one’s life. In this world, charity, sacrifice, and austerity are for one whose life maintenance is properly arranged. A dutiful householder who is fixed in his responsibilities cannot bear to see his family suffering from hunger or wandering about with a begging bowl. If you are worried about breaking your promise, let me share the purport of the statements from the Rg Veda on this subject. Please listen. The scriptures state that honouring a promise to give in charity is truth, and not honouring a promise is untruth. This body is like a tree, and truth is its fruit and flowers. But if the tree itself does not remain, how can its fruit and flowers exist? In this world, truth cannot even exist without a little falsehood. Life cannot be maintained if one uproots and casts away falsehood entirely. Therefore, when falsehood is eradicated, this body, like a tree, also withers away. The guidebooks on ethics (niti-iastra) mention that in flattering a woman, in joking, in praising the bride and others during a marriage ceremony, in protecting one’s livelihood, in a life-threatening situation, for the welfare of cows and brahmanas, or in saving someone from death, speaking untruth is not so much condemned.”

When the revered family guru, the great moralist Sukracarya, was trying to persuade him not to serve Sri Visnu, Maharaja Bali remained silent for a moment and then, with great humility and caution, said to Sukracarya, “Lord, your words are true and righteous for those living in household life. In that way of life, there should be no hindrance to acquiring wealth, sense gratification, fame and sustenance. But Gurudeva, I am the grandson of Prahlada Maharaja and I have already committed to giving alms. Now, how can I deceitfully refuse this brahmana out of a greed for wealth and say, ‘I will not give you alms’ Although one’s life is most dear and difficult to give up, great personalities such as Dadhlci and Sibi have sacrificed their lives for the welfare of others. Even if it means losing everything, fulfilling the desires of great liberated personalities who have realized the Absolute Truth bestows auspiciousness. Therefore, I will surely fulfil the wish of this brahmacdrt.”

When Sukracarya saw that his disciple was disloyal and was disobeying his command, prompted by divine inspiration, he cursed King Bali, although he did not deserve to be cursed for being magnanimous and true to his promise. Sukracarya said, “Fool! You are ignorant, yet you consider yourself to be highly learned. By disregarding me, you have shown pride. You have transgressed my order. Therefore, you will soon lose your wealth and prosperity.” King Bali was a great soul. Even on being cursed by his guru, he did not deviate from truth. He worshipped Vamana Bhagavan in the prescribed manner and King Bali pledged to offer three steps of land, holding water in his hand as per the ritual. As soon as he made the vow, the demigods began to shower flowers from the sky upon King Bali. At that moment, the unlimited Supreme Lord, in whom all material things (trigunatmaka) exist, began to expand the size of His Vamana form. In one step, He encompassed all of Bali’s earthly kingdom. His body covered the sky and His arms covered all directions. Then He occupied the heavens with His second step. Extending beyond the realms of Mahaloka and Janaloka, this second step arrived in Satyaloka. Not even the slightest space remained for placing the third step.

The demons observed that Lord Vamana, on the pretext of asking for three steps of land, had snatched away the entire earth. They thought, “Our Master Bali is now bound by his commitment, so he will not say anything. Under the circumstances, it is our duty to kill this enemy. This is service to our Master Bali.” Although it was not in accordance with the desire of King Bali, the demons took up their tridents and other weapons. In extreme rage, they all rushed to attack Lord Vamana and kill Him. When Lord Visnu’s attendants saw that the commanders of the demons were attacking, they immediately entered the fray and annihilated the demons. Garuda, the king of birds, could understand the Lord’s intention. Garuda bound Bali with the ropes of Varuna. Although Bali was bound and had lost all his possessions, his resolve remained unwavering. A true devotee of Lord Visnu is recognized by such fortitude.

Lord Vamanadeva told Bali, “You promised Me three steps of land. With two steps I have occupied the three worlds. Now, fulfil your promise for My third step. Everything you owned is already Mine. Nevertheless, since your promise is unfulfilled, you will have to reside in hell. In this regard, your guru is in agreement. Go now! Enter the hellish planets. For one who first promises to give charity and then, deceiving the petitioner, goes back on his word, all his desires become futile. In this regard you were excessively egotistical, thinking, ‘I am an immensely wealthy man.’ You promised Me, ‘I will give,’ and then deceived Me. Now you will suffer the consequences of this lie in the hellish planets for some years.”

From an ordinary perspective, it might seem that Vamana Bhagavan was cruel to King Bali. However, King Bali’s heart was unwavering (avicalita-citta). He was determined to please Lord Visnu by his body, mind and words. King Bali said to Vamanadeva, “Do You consider my words to be untrue? That is not the case. I will prove them true. You will not remain deceived. Kindly place Your third step upon my head. I have no fear of going to hell or losing the kingdom. The punishment or discipline enforced by revered guardians (guru-janas) is extremely desirable for all beings because such discipline cannot be given by mothers, fathers, brothers or friends due to their attachment arising from material bewilderment. You certainly teach the best lessons to us demons in a hidden way. Therefore, You are our highest guru. When we become blind, being intoxicated by our wealth, aristocratic birth, strength and so on, You take all those things away and give us eyes.
