О Lord, my grandfather Sri Prahlada is famous throughout the whole universe. He is the best among Your devotees. Sri Prahlada’s father, Hiranyakasipu, who was always antagonistic to You, gave him considerable distress. However, Sri Prahlada always remained devoted to You. He dedicated his whole life to You. What is the use of this material body, which must be abandoned one day or another? And what benefit is derived from all of these bandits who have taken the form of family members to steal one’s wealth? What is the use of a wife, when she puts us into the cycle of repeated birth and death? And what is the purpose of a home where the valuable energy of one’s lifetime is merely wasted?

“In the eyes of the world, You are my adversary. Nevertheless, providence has forcibly separated me from my material opulence and fortune and brought me to Your lotus feet. Material wealth makes the soul’s intellect become dull. Then he cannot realize, ‘My life is in the claws of death. It is impermanent.’”

As King Bali was speaking in this way, the recipient of Bhagavan’s love, Sri Prahlada, arrived there. Bali was confined by the ropes of Varuna, so he could not worship him as he was accustomed to do before. He simply bowed his head with tear-filled eyes. Sri Prahlada bowed his head upon the ground in front of Bhagavan Vamanadeva and said, “Lord, it was You who bestowed wealth upon Bali, and today, You have taken it away. I understand that You have shown him immense mercy by separating him from the captivating worldly riches that bewilder the soul. Even learned persons become ensnared by the allure of wealth. In such circumstances, who can properly understand their real spiritual nature (svarupa)?”

Upon seeing King Bali bound by the ropes of Varuna, his saintly wife, Vindhyavali, became fearful. She bowed down at the lotus feet of Bhagavan. With folded hands and lowered head, she addressed the Lord, “O Prabhu! You have created this entire universe for Your play. Those who possess wicked intelligence (kubuddhi) consider themselves to be the masters of this world.”

Lord Brahma said, “O Master! Giving up all miserliness, those who sincerely worship Your lotus feet attain the highest destination. This Bali joyfully offered the three worlds to You with patience and fortitude. Then how can he be put into distress?”

Lord Vamanadeva replied, “Brahmaji, when I bestow mercy upon someone, I take away their wealth. For when a person becomes intoxicated with wealth, they begin to disrespect Me and show contempt for other people. The soul wanders through many births under the control of his karma. Then sometimes, by My great compassion, the soul attains a human body. If, after being born as a human, he does not become proud due to his high birth, achievements, status, beauty, education, wealth, and other factors, then it should be understood as My great mercy. To prove this, I gave royal opulence to devotees such as Dhruva. Attributes such as aristocratic birth give rise to the vanity and intellectual inertia by which a human being becomes bereft of all means of auspiciousness. However, those who seek shelter in Me are not bewildered by them. This Bali is pre-eminent among both the Danava and Daitya dynasties and he is increasing their fame. He has achieved victory over Maya, the illusory material energy, which is extremely difficult to conquer. His wealth was taken away. He was deposed from the royal throne. He was subjected to various accusations, bound by enemies, and abandoned by friends and relatives. He had to endure many hardships. He was even cursed by his guru. However, he was determined not to break his commitment. I gave instruction on religious principles (dharma) with pretension, yet this honest person did not forsake his religious principles.”

The main teaching in Bali’s character and conduct is unconditional surrender (saranagati). Without hesitation, one must surrender oneself fully at the lotus feet of the Lord (atma-nivedana). Bali shows us that even if the Lord were to become deceitful and cheat us, we should still offer ourselves selflessly at His lotus feet. If, from an ordinary perspective, Bhagavan puts us through various hardships, if He takes away all our material wealth and honour, binds us, or even if He crosses the last limit of cruelty by taking away everything, still, without being even slightly unsettled, we should completely offer ourselves in His loving service.

A conventional family guru who is indifferent to Sri Krsna may be expert in the performance of Vedic rituals and learned in worldly ethics. He may make obstacles to completely offering everything to the lotus feet of Lord Visnu. Moreover, he may pronounce a curse, "May you lose all worldly possessions.” Even then, pure devotees will remain unwavering and offer their souls exclusively at the lotus feet of Sri Visnu. If a guru does not offer all of his disciples into the service of Sri Visnu, who is the only enjoyer, then he does not deserve to be called a guru. Even if one has accepted such a family guru, one should reject his ill-advice and serve a genuine guru who teaches complete surrender and dedication to the service of Sri Visnu. The ultimate auspiciousness is only attained when Sri Visnu assimilates the surrendered soul into His divine realm. It is the intrinsic nature of pure consciousness to be a sacrificial offering (bali) at the lotus feet of Sri Bhagavan.

## Chapter 24 Maharaja Ambarisa

Maharaja Ambarisa was extremely fortunate. He had attained sovereignty over the seven continents, undisputed wealth and incomparable opulence. Although these are all rarely attained by human beings, still, Maharaja Ambarisa considered them to be as substantial as a dream. He knew that a person who becomes captured by greed for wealth goes to a dark region of hell. Material abundance is ephemeral, like the glow of a lamp that flickers brightest before it is extinguished. Maharaja Ambarisa had great love (pretna) for Sri Krsna and for those who love Him. Upon attaining prema, all the wealth in the universe seems as insignificant as a speck of dust. He had dedicated his mind to the lotus feet of Sri Krsna. His voice was dedicated to describing Sri Krsna’s divine qualities, his hands to serving and cleaning the temple of Lord Hari, his ears to hearing the auspicious pastimes of the Lord. His eyes were engaged in witnessing the divine form of Lord Mukunda and His temples, his sense of touch in massaging the devotees of the Lord, his nose in smelling the divine fragrance of holy tulasi leaves offered to the lotus feet of the Lord, and his tongue in honouring the remnants of foodstuffs offered to the Lord. King Ambarisa’s feet were engaged in making pilgrimages to sacred places and his head was engaged in bowing with reverence at the lotus feet of Sri Krsna. King Ambarisa offered garlands, sandalwood and other offerings, not out of a desire to attain Bhagavan, but to attain the love of Bhagavan that constitutes the pure glory of Bhagavan’s intimate associates. In this way, he offered all his activities to the Supreme Lord who is the enjoyer of all sacrifices (Yajna-purusa) and who is beyond the range of sense perception (indriyatita). He understood Bhagavan to be the Soul of all souls (sarvatma) and the all-pervading form of all forms (sarva-svarupa). King Ambarlsa used to rule the earth in accordance with the orders and guidance of brahmana devotees of the Lord. In this way, He pleased Bhagavan by devotional service imbued with austerity and by executing his duty of protecting the citizenry. Gradually, he relinquished all attachments. He firmly understood that his home, wife, sons, brothers, military battalions of elephants, chariots, horses and infantry, inexhaustible treasures, ornaments and weapons were all transient effects. Being pleased by King Ambarisa’s exclusive love, Bhagavan deputed the Sudarsana Cakra (disc weapon) to protect him. Sudarsana Cakra instils fear in adversaries and safeguards the devotees.

King Ambarisa’s wife was equally righteous, detached from worldly affairs and absorbed in bhakti. Once, they both took a vow to observe the Ekadasi fast and Dvadasi for one year to worship Lord Sri Krsna. At the conclusion of the fast during the Kartika month, they observed a three-night fast and bathed in the holy Yamuna River. Then King Ambarisa worshipped Sri Krsna in the district of Madhuvana by conducting a grand bathing ceremony (abhiseka) of the deity and offering fine garments, garlands and precious ornaments. He arranged a sumptuous meal for the priesthood (brahmanas) and gave them cows in charity. Thereafter, Ambarisa sought their permission to break his fast. At that moment, the sage Durvasa Muni, who is capable of blessings and curses, arrived as an unexpected guest at his palace. Ambarisa greeted him by offering obeisances at his feet and invited him for the meal. Durvasa accepted the request but first he had to go to the river Yamuna to complete his obligatory daily rituals. When the return of Durvasa Muni was delayed, Ambarisa faced a dilemma. Only twenty-four minutes of the Dvadasi day were left on which to break the fast. He asked the brahmanas for guidance, “It is a fault to eat before feeding the brahmanas and it is also a fault to not break the fast at the prescribed time. Both situations are undesirable. What should I do? Please advise me.” After consulting with the priesthood, he decided to break the fast with water, as it is mentioned in the scriptures that consuming water can be considered eating and also not eating. After breaking his fast with water, Maharaja Ambarisa was waiting for the return of Durvasa Muni. After completing his necessary duties, Durvasa Muni returned from the Yamuna. When the king approached to greet him, Durvasa could understand by his mystic power that the king had completed the fast without his permission. Durvasa began to tremble in anger. He scolded Ambarisa who was humbly standing before him with folded hands.

“Look how cruel he is! He is becoming intoxicated by wealth. He has not even been slightly touched by devotion to Bhagavan, yet he considers himself very capable. Today, he has committed a great injustice by transgressing dharma. Despite inviting me to be honoured as a guest, he has eaten without me. Now, I will make you taste the fruit of this injustice.” As he spoke, he became more infuriated. He plucked a matted lock of hair from his head from which he manifested a demoness (Krtya) to kill Maharaja Ambarisa. The demoness was blazing like fire. With a sword in her hand, she rushed to attack Maharaja Ambarisa. However, Maharaja Ambarisa was not even slightly unsettled. The Sudarsana Cakra, the protector of devotees, immediately appeared and reduced Durvasa’s demoness to a pile of ashes. After burning the demoness, the Cakra sped towards Durvasa. Durvasa fled here and there to seek refuge in fear for his life. He fled through the sky and across the earth. He tried to hide in caves, in the oceans and in the lower planets such as Atala and Vitala, in the planets of the guardian demigods of the worlds, in the heavenly planets and in other planets throughout the three worlds. Wherever he went, he saw the intolerable heat of the Sudarsana Cakra was chasing him. When he found no refuge anywhere, Durvasa approached Lord Brahma and implored him, “O Brahma, you are self-born. Please protect me from this fiery Sudarsana Cakra.”

Brahma replied, “At the end of my lifespan, when the creation pastimes of the Lord come to an end, the Lord in the form of time (kala) vanquishes the entire universe, including my abode, simply by a movement of His eyebrow. Personalities such as myself, Lord Siva, the prajapatis headed by Daksa and Bhrgu, the rulers of humans and of demigods, are bound by the laws He has created. We respectfully hold His orders upon our heads to benefit the world. So we are not able to protect the offender of His devotee.”

In this way, Brahma disappointed Durvasa. To escape the heat of Bhagavan’s Cakra, Durvasa then sought refuge in Lord Siva, who resides in Kailasa. Lord Siva said, “The Cakra of Lord Sri Hari is unbearable for us. We are always subordinate to Him. We cannot fathom the Maya of the Lord because we are covered by that illusory energy of the Lord. Therefore, other than Lord Visnu no one can protect you. Go to Him. He alone will bestow good fortune upon you.”

Again disappointed, Durvasa went from there to the supreme abode of the Lord, Vaikuntha. Scorched by the heat of Lord Visnus Cakra, Durvasa fell at the feet of the Lord, saying, “O Acyuta, infallible one! О Ananta, unlimited one! You are the sole desirable shelter of the saints. Lord! I am an offender. Please protect me. Because I did not understand Your influence, I have committed an offence to Your beloved devotee. О Lord! Save me from this mistake. Simply by uttering Your name, even the souls in hell attain liberation.”

Sri Bhagavan said, “Durvasa, I am completely under the control of My devotees. I have not the slightest independence. My simple-hearted devotees have taken My heart in their hands. They love Me and I love them. I am the only shelter of My devotees. I do not desire My very self or My inseparable consort, Laksmi, who is half of My body, without My saintly devotees. How could I ever abandon those devotees who have left aside their wife, children, home, relatives, life, wealth and the happiness of this world and the next to come to My shelter? Just as a chaste wife brings her virtuous husband under control by her loyalty, similarly, My equipoised devotees keep Me in their hearts and, binding Me with the bonds of their love, bring Me under their control. My unalloyed loving devotees consider themselves fulfilled just by serving Me. However, when My devotees are offered any type of liberation, such as salokya (residing in the same planet as the Lord) or sarupya (having a spiritual body similar to that of the Lord), they do not accept it. What to speak of accepting any temporary worldly happiness. Respected Durvasa! What more can I say to you? My loving devotees are My heart, and I am their heart. They know nothing beyond Me, and I know nothing beyond them. You should go to the one you opposed and thereby fell into this disaster. By trying to harm the innocent saints, one simply invites inauspiciousness upon himself. Go to Maharaja Ambarisa and beg for his forgiveness. Only if he pardons you, will you find good fortune.”

After receiving this command from Bhagavan, Durvasa set out again, still being scorched by the heat of the Sudarsana Cakra. He returned to King Ambarisa and, wracked with remorse, humbly grasped the feet of the king. Witnessing this gesture and feeling ashamed, Raja Ambarisa began to offer prayers to the Lord’s Cakra. The Cakra became pacified by the prayers of Bhagavan’s devotee. When Durvasa was freed from the blazing heat of the Cakra’s fire, he regained composure and began to praise Maharaja Ambarisa and shower him with numerous blessings.

“Today, I have witnessed the greatness of the Lord’s loving devotees. О King! I gave you suffering, yet you only wish me well. What is difficult to achieve for those saints who with firm love tightly hold the lotus feet of Bhagavan Sri Hari, who is most affectionate to His devotees? And what is difficult to give up for those great souls whose hearts are so generous? What remains to be accomplished by the servants of Bhagavan? He is so great that the places touched by His lotus feet are places of pilgrimage and hearing His auspicious name makes the soul immaculate. Maharaja Ambarisa, your heart is filled with compassion. You have bestowed great mercy upon me by forgiving my offences and protecting my life.”

Maharaja Ambarisa had refrained from eating since the departure of Durvasa. He had been waiting for Durvasa’s return. Now he caught the feet of Durvasa Muni, respectfully attended to him and fed him a meal in accordance with the rules of hospitality. Maharaja Ambarisa continually nurtured his devotion to the Supreme Lord through all his actions. By the influence of his devotion, he considered all worldly pleasures, even up to the highest planet, Brahmaloka, to be the same as hell.

Maharaja Ambarisa’s character serves as a perfect example for pure devotees. Although a pure devotee may be the undisputed ruler of all the riches on earth, he is not enchanted by them. He devotes all his wealth in the unconditional service of Sri Krsna and His devotees. Pure devotees never have the self-conception of being the ‘enjoyer’ (bhokta). Only the realization of being a servant has authority over their purified minds (citta). Their bodies, minds, words and all senses remain constantly engaged in the service of Lord Hari beyond all ignorance. They consider hearing and chanting the narrations of Sri Hari’s pastimes, while serving Hari in the company of pure devotees, to be superior to liberation. They consider the various types of salvation such as salokya to be insignificant. They eagerly aspire only for the eternal service of the Lord under the guidance of His pure devotees. Whether they reside in forests or reign as emperors, such devotees conquer the unconquerable Lord. If someone intoxicated by his own beauty or scholarship disrespects the pure devotee vaisnavas in any way, then he cannot attain the grace of the Lord or the grace of the Holy Name. Pure devotees can deliver one from offences against the lotus feet of Sri Hari or the Holy Name. Offences at the lotus feet of the Lord can be absolved by the mercy of the divine namavatara, the Holy Name. But offenders at the feet of a pure devotee are not protected even by the Lord or His Holy Name. If a pure devotee forgives an offensive person, only then can his life become auspicious. The Supreme Lord Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu taught this principle to the world through the example of Sri Sacimata.

Maharaja Ambarisa’s life history teaches us about the gravity of offending a devotee (vaisnavaparadha) and how one can be saved from such offences. Among all terrible sins such as killing one’s mother, father, siblings, wife, a cow, an unborn child, or any heinous act - offences at the lotus feet of the Goddess of Devotion (Bhakti Devi) are the most severe. The reason is that sins act on the body and mind, but offences act on the soul and cover the function of pure consciousness. And among offences, vaisnavaparadha is the most dangerous. When we offend the lotus feet of Bhakti Devi, Sri Bhagavan, or His Holy Name, the devotees can absolve us from that offence. But if we offend such a devotee, then there is no shelter or redemption for us anywhere. Therefore, we must be extremely careful not to commit even the slightest offence to any great personality.

## Chapter 25 Saubhari Rsi

Saubhari Rsi was a great ascetic, yogi and brahmana. Once, he was meditating while immersed in the waters of the Yamuna River. In the water, he noticed a large fish enjoying the pleasure of mating with his female partners. Seeing the happiness of this fish, the subconscious impressions of worldly life in the form of the desire to marry arose in the conscious mind of this elderly worn-out ascetic. Therefore, he approached King Mandhata and requested one of his fifty daughters for marriage.

King Mandhata replied, “O Brahmana! If any of my daughters choose you at the bridegroom selection ceremony (svayamvara), then you can marry them.”

Saubhari Rsi understood the intention of the king. “The king has responded tactfully because I have aged. My body is wrinkled, my hair has turned grey and my head is trembling. Now, no young woman can hanker for such a person. All right, I will make myself so beautiful that not only the princesses, but even demigoddesses will become excited” On the strength of his austerities, Saubhari Rsi then transformed his body to become the pinnacle of handsomeness.

The guards of the inner chambers escorted Saubhari Muni to the elegantly decorated palatial hall of the virgin princesses. All fifty princesses chose the sage Saubhari as their husband. The hearts of the princesses became so attached to Saubhari Rsi that they immediately abandoned their love for each other and began quarrelling among themselves saying, "He is not suitable for you! He is suitable for me!” Saubhari Rsi accepted the hands of all fifty princesses in marriage. By the immense power of his austerities, Saubhari Rsi created priceless palaces surrounded by gardens of fragrant flowers and pristine lakes. He manifested sitting places, clothing, ornaments, ointments such as sandalwood paste, delicious food and flower garlands, and began to enjoy life with his numerous wives.

King Mandhata, the ruler of the seven continents of Mother Earth, was astounded by the happiness of Saubhari’s household life. Thus, he gave up all pride in his position as emperor of the world. In this way, Saubhari Rsi enjoyed the happiness of household life and experienced many sense objects with his senses free from disease. Nevertheless, just as a fire is never pacified by pouring drops of ghee, he could not attain even one drop of satisfaction and peace.

One day, Saubhari Rsi was sitting peacefully. In contemplation he realized, “By seeing that fish mating for just a moment, I have lost my austerity and even lost my very self. I was a great ascetic. I used to diligently observe my vows. Now, look at my downfall! I had preserved my brahminical energy (brahma-tejas) for a long time. Yet, just by association with that mating fish, my brahminical energy was destroyed. How terrible is the power of material association (asat-sanga)\\ Bhagavan has given the priceless jewel of independence (svadhinata-ratna). The misuse of independence leads to hell. I undertook vows appropriate for a sadhu in the water of the Yamuna. By the mercy of Yamuna, the soul can attain devotion to Sri Krsna, but instead, animalistic tendencies arose in me! Instead of attaining the auspicious mercy of Bhagavan’s dear devotee, Yamuna Devi, I attained the material association (asat-sanga) of the fish. I thought that by performing austerities my consciousness would gradually become purified (citta-suddhi) and bhakti for Lord Hari would awaken. Instead, my consciousness became distorted (citta-vikrti) and animalistic inclinations (pasu-vrtti) awakened! Therefore, one who desires liberation should always avoid the company of living entities possessed of the animalistic nature (pasu-svabhava) to engage in sense enjoyment. One should always associate with the saints, who are absorbed in the service of Bhagavan, and never turn his senses outward even for a moment. At first, I was performing austerities alone, but due to the association of a fish in the water, I ended up marrying fifty women. By begetting children, my family has now increased to five thousand members. I maintained the conception that the sense objects were substantial reality and so the modes of material nature stole away my intelligence. Now my mind is filled with so many desires in relation to this world that I cannot surmount them. Alas! Alas! How powerful is the influence of bad association!”

After deliberating in this way, he remained in his home for a while. Then, he became detached, took up renunciation and went to the forest. Considering their husband to be their all-in-all, his devoted wives followed him. There, Saubhari Rsi, whose mind was now fully concentrated, engaged in severe austerities until his body became extremely emaciated. In this way, he gave up his body along with the household fires and ultimately entered into the service of the Supreme Lord in the spiritual world. On witnessing their husband attain his spiritual destination, the chaste wives of Saubhari Rsi also attained the same spiritual state by the influence of his association, just as the flames of a fire cease when the fire is extinguished.

The life of Saubhari Rsi teaches us that the strength of yoga, the strength of austerities, and the strength of service in solitude (nirjana-bhajana) cannot protect a soul who has come under the control of lust (kama). Except for the complete shelter and guidance (anugatya) of the pure devotees of Sri Krsna, the transcendental cupid (aprakrta Kamadeva), there is no way to protect oneself. Great ascetics (tapasvis), impersonal Salvationist philosophers (jnanis), and even persons who are decrepit with old age, are all besmirched with material enjoyment. One should not associate with them.

On considering the example of Saubhari Rsi, some persons imagine that just as he went on enjoying and then finally became detached from worldly pleasures, we will also finally give up material enjoyment and take to the path of auspiciousness for the soul. However, those saints who are realized in devotional service suggest, on the basis of their direct experience, that the effort to enter the path of renunciation by repeatedly engaging in worldly pleasures leads most people in the direction of impersonalism. Those who try to imitate examples such as Bilvamangala Thakura and Ajamila, and who try to search for the path of renunciation within worldly pleasures, commit offences at the feet of pure devotees. They fall into the mire of offences and deprive themselves of auspiciousness for a long time. They cannot be delivered from this condition. Therefore, giving up the impersonal conception, serving the pure devotees and associating with them is the path to one’s ultimate good.

## Chapter 26 Rajarsi Khatvanga

Khatvanga, the son of King Visvasaha, was extremely dear to the demigods. He was a worshipper and devotee of the 330 million demigods and no one could defeat him in battle. When the demigods requested his assistance in a battle against the demons, he agreed and led the demigods to victory. Then the demigods, being very pleased, wanted to give him a benediction. First, he wanted to know how much time he had left in this life. Only after knowing how long he had left to live would he pray for a boon. When he discovered that only a muhurta (48 minutes) of his life remained, he wished for liberation. The demigods said, “Only Lord Govinda can grant liberation, not us.” Understanding this and not wanting to waste any more time, he boarded a celestial aircraft given by the demigods and returned to his capital city. He gave up service to the demigods and completely absorbed his mind in the worship of Bhagavan Sri Visnu.

He considered, “Will I attain ultimate auspiciousness by having my desires fulfilled by the demigods? They can fulfil aspirations for piety (dharma), wealth (artha), or material enjoyment (kama). But how will pure devotion arise from this? The senses and intellect of the demigods are distracted (viksipta) and, therefore, they cannot perceive the indwelling Lord (antaryami) Sri Hari present in their hearts. The objects of the senses created by Bhagavaris illusory energy are like a mirage or imaginary city in the sky (gandharva-pura). The attachment of the conditioned souls towards these sense objects is natural (svabhavika). By meditating on Bhagavan Sri Visnu, I am relinquishing all material attachment and completely surrendering unto Him through bhakti-yoga. In anything other than servitude to Sri Vasudeva, there is no way for the soul to attain eternal auspiciousness. Abandoning all identification with the material body and surrendering to Sri Vasudeva is the only desirable thing for the soul. All other desires are the cause of repeated birth and death in the material world.”

Reflecting in this way, a moment before his death, the saintly king Rajarsi Khatvahga relinquished the worship of demigods, the pursuit of wealth, religious duties, worldly enjoyment, greed for benedictions, and bodily identification. In just a moment, he had become completely surrendered to Bhagavan Sri Vasudeva and he attained His eternal servitude.

From the life story of Rajarsi Khatvanga, we receive two important lessons:

Life is impermanent, and no one knows how much time they have left. Therefore, rather than striving for worldly things, the only duty of the soul is to immediately begin devotional service to Lord Vasudeva. One should not make excuses thinking, “Most of my lifetime has been wasted, so now it is too late. How will devotion to the Lord be possible?” or “Now I am just in my childhood.” or “Now I am still a youth. I have so much time left.” It is the duty of everyone to engage themselves in devotional service to Lord Hari beginning from this very moment because one can attain perfection in devotional service in just one moment if there is one-pointed, single-minded attention (aikantikata). That moment spent in activities other than the service of Sri Hari (hari-bhajana) is futile. An intelligent person never procrastinates by neglecting devotion to Lord Vasudeva today while promising to serve tomorrow. Efforts for material objectives may be postponed for the future, but wasting time by postponing the service of Sri Hari for the future is improper. One may think, “Much time of my life has already been wasted.” Spending time being disappointed about it will not bring it back. The time one has at hand should be utilized wisely. From this very moment, one should begin hari-bhajana, loving service to Lord Hari.

The second lesson is that one cannot attain liberation by independently worshipping other demigods. If even liberation cannot be attained, then what is the question of attaining devotion. One will be very far away from transcendental service (bhakti). Direct service to Sri Vasudeva is attained only by love for Him (prema-bhakti). The lotus feet of Sri Vasudeva are the only worthy refuge. The devotees of the Lord disregard religiosity (dharma), wealth (artha), worldly enjoyment (kama), and even all types of liberation such as sdlokya-mukti, residing on the same planet as the Lord. They pray only for love of God. Those demigods who cannot protect the living entities from imminent death are not worthy of being called worshipful gods (devatas). Only Sri Vasudeva can save the living entities from death. By bestowing piety, wealth and enjoyment, the demigods show fraudulent mercy (kapata-krpa) towards the living entities. Only when they guide the living entities towards Sri Vasudeva will their mercy be called non-fraudulent mercy (akapata krpa).

## Chapter 27 Bhrigu

Bhrgu, the son of Brahma, was a great vaisnava. Once, Bhrgu kicked the chest of Lord Visnu, who was most worshipful for him. Bhrgu always meditates upon the Lord within his heart. Many wonder how Bhrgu could still be regarded as an eminent vaisnava saint, even after kicking the chest of the Lord. Some mistakenly believe that Visnu, or Sri Krsna, carries the mark made by Bhrgu’s foot on His chest to show respect to the brahmanas. However, from the history of Durvasa Muni and Ambarlsa Maharaja, it is evident that when Durvasa, a brahmana, begged forgiveness from the Lord for his offence to Ambarlsa Maharaja, the Lord replied that even He, the Lord, does not possess the power to forgive those who commit offences against His devotees. Why? Vaisnavas are the Lord’s heart, and the Lord is their heart. The Lord is under the control of His devotees. The worship of a devotee is superior to the worship of the Lord. Offending a devotee is more devastating than offending the Lord directly. While the chanting of the Lord’s Holy Name can protect one who offends the Lord, one who offends His devotee cannot be protected, even by the Holy Name or by Sri Gurudeva. Therefore, vaisnavas are superior to the brahmanas. To show this superiority of a vaisnava, Lord Visnu carries the mark of Bhrgu’s foot on His chest.

Bhrgu kicked the chest of Bhagavan to reveal the qualities and supremacy of the Lord to the whole world. This was an act of service to the Lord. Bhrgu did not do this to satisfy his own senses or to propagate the greatness of his dynasty out of pride. Bhrgu performed this pastime just to teach the world that Bhagavan Visnu is the greatest. He is infinitely forgiving and the embodiment of affection for His devotees. Bhrgu’s act was a means to illustrate and spread this divine message. Furthermore, it was Bhagavan who personally entered into Bhrgu’s body and inspired him to illuminate the glories of the devotees by manifesting such a divine pastime.

In ancient times, great sages initiated a grand yajrta (sacrifice) on the banks of the Sarasvati River. During this ritual, they were absorbed in reciting and hearing the Puranas (scriptures). They discussed among themselves the significance of various perspectives presented in the scriptures. Some scriptures extolled the glories of Lord Siva more, while others hailed Visnu as supreme. This led to a difference of opinion between the sages. Some sages proposed that all opinions are ultimately the same, thereby creating another new opinion. They said, “What is gained by conflicting opinions amongst us? One should simply accept that whatever one’s own guru teaches, that is best for him. From this perspective, everyone is equal.” This is the theory of formlessness (niravasesa-vada). The so-called ‘syncretism’ (samanvaya-vada) that has been popularized in recent times is nothing but the echo of the ancient niravasesa-vada. According to this theory, all categories collapse and everything is the same. The demigods whose power is dependent upon Bhagavan, the living entities, and Bhagavan Himself are all considered one.

The wise sages deputed the responsibility to resolve this doubt to Lord Brahma’s mind-born son Bhrgu. First, Bhrgu went to Lord Brahma’s assembly. When Bhrgu entered the assembly, he neither greeted Lord Brahma as a father nor showed any signs of reverence or praise. He just arrogantly stood there. Seeing Bhrgu’s audacity, misconduct and disrespectful behaviour, Lord Brahma became angry. It seemed as if he were about to burn Bhrgu in the flames of his wrath. Seeing this fiery form of his father, Bhrgu quickly ran away. Bhrgu understood the nature of Lord Brahma.

Next, he visited Lord Siva at Kailasa. There, Siva and Parvati stood up to honour Bhrgu. Considering Bhrgu as his younger brother, Lord Siva went to embrace him. But Bhrgu said, “Mahesa! Do not touch me. You have put on the attire of heretics. You keep company with ghosts, ghouls, witches, untouchable and ungodly beings. You are a deviant. Which scripture states that wearing ashes and bones is righteous behaviour? One must take bath to purify himself after touching you. Stay away from me.”

Bhrgu said all these things jokingly, because a vaisnava like Bhrgu could never criticize Lord Siva. On hearing Bhrgu’s words, Lord Siva, also known as Rudradeva, became furious. He took his trident in his hand ready to destroy Bhrgu. But Parvati seized Rudra’s hand, fell at his feet, and persuaded him to stop.

Bhrgu immediately departed from Kailasa and set off towards Vaikuntha. Upon arriving in Vaikuntha, Bhrgu witnessed Lord Visnu reclining on a jewel-encrusted bed while Goddess Laksmi Devi served His divine feet. At that moment, Bhrgu came and kicked the chest of Lord Visnu.

Upon seeing Bhrgu, Lord Visnu immediately stood up to greet him. Visnu bowed down to Bhrgu and, along with Goddess Laksmi, joyfully began the ceremonial greeting of washing his feet. Lord Visnu seated Bhrgu on an elevated seat, personally applied sandalwood paste on his limbs and prayed for forgiveness for any offence. Bhagavan Visnu spoke sweedy to Bhrgu, “The water that has washed a vaisnava’s feet purifies even the pilgrimage places when they have become contaminated by sinful pilgrims. All the universes within My body, along with all the demigods residing therein, have become purified by the water that has cleansed the feet of a devotee.” To eternally remember the characteristics of His pure devotee, Lord Visnu always wears the imprint of Bhrgu’s foot upon His chest.  
Bhrgu was amazed by this behaviour of Lord Visnu and, immersed in bhakti-rasa, began to dance in His presence. Then Bhrgu returned to the assembly of sages and told them about the behaviour of Lord Brahma, Lord Visnu and Lord Mahesa. Bhrgu made a triple oath and testified before the sages:

*sarva-srestha—sri-vaikuntha-natha narayana*  
*satya satya satya ei balila vacana*

**“Narayana, the Lord of Vaikuntha, is the greatest of all. This is the truth, the truth, the truth.**

*sabara isvara krsna—janaka sabara*  
*brahma, siva karena yanhara adhikara*

**Krsna is the controller and father of all. Even Lord Brahma and Lord Siva act under His authority.**

*karta-harta-raksita sabara narayana*  
*nihsandehe bhaja giya tanhara carana*

**Lord Narayana is the doer, creator, and protector of all. Therefore, one should worship His lotus feet without doubt.**

*dharma, jnana, punya-kirti, aisvarya, virakti*  
*atma-irestha madhyama yahara yata iakti*

*sakala krsnera, iha janiha niscaya ataeva gao bhaja krsnera vijaya*

*Caitanya Bhagavata 3.9-370-374*

Know for certain that religiosity, knowledge, pious activities, opulence and renunciation, as well as ones exalted position and influence, all originate from Krsna. Therefore, sing the glories of Krsna and worship Him.

## Chapter 28 Avadhuta and the twenty-four gurus

Once upon a time, Sri Krsna narrated to Uddhava the history of an avadhuta and King Yadu. An avadhuta is a person who is completely liberated from the material bodily conception of life and whose conduct is not limited by social conventions. King Yadu could understand the essence of righteousness (dharma). One day, he saw a young avadhuta brahmana who was cognizant of the past, present and future. The avadhuta brahmana was wandering fearlessly throughout the world. Yadu asked him, “O Brahmana! You do not seem to perform any responsible work, yet you possess such extraordinary intelligence. From where did you attain this profound wisdom? Although you are supremely learned, you are wandering through life like a child. I want to ask: how do you experience such indescribable joy within your soul? Please bestow your mercy and enlighten me.”

The brahmana replied, “O King! I have taken shelter of many gurus through my intellect. By accepting their instructions, I freely wander throughout the world in a state of liberation. Now listen to the names of those gurus and the teachings I have received from them. My gurus are: (1) the earth, (2) the wind, (3) the sky, (4) the water, (5) the fire, (6) the moon, (7) the sun, (8) the pigeon, (9) the python, (10) the ocean, (11) the moth, (12) the honeybee, (13) the elephant, (14) the honey gatherer, (15) the deer, (16) the fish, (17) the courtesan Pingala, 18)    the kurara bird, (19) the child, (20) the young maiden, (21) the arrow maker, (22) the serpent, (23) the spider and (24) the wasp. О King, I have taken shelter of these twenty-four gurus and have imbibed teachings from their conduct. I shall now relate to you everything that I have learned from them, listen carefully.

l)    The Earth

“I have learned from the earth the lesson of patience and forgiveness. No matter how many injuries and disasters are inflicted upon the earth, the earth never seeks revenge or cries out. All living entities in this world act according to the reactions of their own activities (prarabdha-karma). From time to time, either with premeditation or on the spur of the moment, they suddenly attack in different ways. In times of distress, a sober person should not lose his composure or become angry. He should continue along his own path undeterred. The entire existence of the mountains and trees, who are transformations of the earth, is always and forever for the benefit of others. A tree provides shade and sweet fruits. Even if someone throws a stone at a tree, it still gives them fruit. Even if someone cuts the tree with a sharp weapon, it still gives benefit to the perpetrator. It even benefits those who burn it in fire.

“The cows, buffalo and other animals trample the grass, yet the grass still acts for their welfare. Even after drying up and becoming straw, the grass continues to serve others.

“How many people are helped by the streams of a mountain? How many medicinal plants are born in the forested area of the mountains? Deer and other animals wander on the mountain, but it does not harm anyone. Only by being as tolerant as a tree and as immovable as a mountain is it possible to enter the state of divine service to the Supreme Lord Hari.

2\)    The Air

“The vital air within the body (prana-vayu) desires only as much as is required for its sustenance and is fully content upon receiving that much. Similarly, from vital air within the body, I have learned that a spiritual seeker should eat only as much as is necessary to maintain his life. He should avoid indulging the senses in many types of sense objects. He should limit his utilization of the external sense objects so that his intellect is not distorted (vikrta-buddhi), his mind is not fickle and his speech is not engaged in unnecessary chatter.

“The air outside the body travels to many places but does not become attached to anything. It does not adopt anyone’s faults or attributes. Similarly, from the air outside the body I have learned that a dedicated spiritual seeker may engage with external sense objects of various characteristics in accordance with necessity, but he should remain steadfast in pursuit of his goal without leaning towards anyone’s attributes or faults. But air must carry aromas. Although that is true, the element of air remains pure and has no contact with aroma. Aroma is not the intrinsic nature of air; it is the characteristic of earth. Similarly, a seeker, while enduring the afflictions of illness and suffering, hunger and thirst, views himself not as the body but as the soul, and despite being in the shelter of the body and its qualities, he remains unsmeared and detached.

3\)    The Sky (akasa)

“Fire ignites, water pours, and transformations of earth such as food grains are created and destroyed. Impelled by the wind, clouds come and go, yet the sky remains untouched. From the perspective of the sky, all these occurrences are nothing. Similarly, I have learned from the sky not to be confused by the cycle of past, present and future. The continuous cycle of creation and destruction is just like the uncoiling and coiling of a snake. However, all these things do not in any way touch the soul.

4\)    The Water

“Water inherently possesses the qualities of transparency, liquidity, sweetness and purity. By merely seeing, touching or even uttering the names of the holy rivers like the Ganges, people become purified. Similarly, a spiritual seeker should naturally be pure and gentle. He should speak sweetly and purify the world. One who imbibes these teachings from the water sanctifies others by his sight, by touch and by the utterance of his name.

5\)    The Fire

“From fire I learned that just as fire purifies all things without accepting their faults, similarly, a sadhu purifies the fallen souls without ever becoming contaminated. Just as fire can consume all sorts of substances, similarly, although it may be seen that a saint (sadhu) may eat some forbidden food, he is not affected by the faults of that food. Rather, burning ever brighter, he purifies everything.

“Just like a fire concealed by ashes, saints never preach their own glories. However, sometimes they expand their glories, like a blazing fire, for the sake of instructing the general populace. They administer to the well-being of everyone by becoming the gurus. The fire exists within wood, but ordinary people fail to comprehend it. Similarly, souls bound by the illusory material energy also cannot always perceive a saint.

6\)    The Moon

“I learned from the moon that although its phases wax and wane due to the influence of time, the moon itself neither diminishes nor grows. In other words, the moon does not undergo any change. Just as the moon’s various phases are related to time but do not affect the moon itself, similarly, all stages from birth to death belong to the body. They have no connection to the soul.

7\)    The Sun

“I have learned from the sun that just as the sun draws water from the earth with its rays and then, in due time, gives it back in the form of rain, similarly, the pure devotees consume and collect sense objects at the right time through their senses. When the appropriate time arrives, they renounce the sense objects and give them in charity. Both at the time of accepting and at the time of giving, they are never attached to any sensory object.

“To persons of gross intellect, the sun reflected in different water vessels seems to have entered into them and become different types of suns. However, the sun in essence is not many. Similarly, due to the variety of bodily and mental designations (upadhi) it may seem that the same soul is different when it accepts a different body. The fundamental truth is that the souls do not change with the change of body.

“Alternatively, one may think that the Supreme Soul, Paramatma, is different in every body. Those who harbour such notions have intellectual inertia. The Paramatma is the same in all bodies. Just as the sun is one, constitutionally, there is no difference between the forms of the Paramatma in the hearts of all living entities.

8\)    The Pigeon

“One should not develop excessive attachment or affection for anyone in this world, otherwise his intellect will lose its independence and become miserable. This is the lesson I have learned from the pigeon.

“In a jungle there lived a pigeon who had built his nest upon a tree. He lived in the nest with his female pigeon partner for several years. The pigeons’ love for each other grew continuously in their united hearts. The male pigeon had so much love for the female pigeon that it endured great hardships to fulfil her every desire. The female pigeon also fulfilled the desires of her lusty husband. In due course of time, the female pigeon had many offspring. They lovingly nurtured and cared for their children and became immersed in joy upon hearing the sweet cooing sound of their voices.

“One day, both parents went to fetch food for their young ones. By chance, a hunter, who was wandering in the area, approached their nest. He spread a net and caught the young ones. Just then, the parents returned to their nest carrying food. The female pigeon saw that her little ones were trapped in the net and they were chirping in distress. Seeing them in such a state, the female pigeon could not contain her sorrow. Crying out loudly, she flew towards her trapped children, completely forgetting about her own safety, and also became entangled in the net. When the male pigeon saw that his beloved children and his dearest life partner were caught in the net, he became extremely distressed and began to lament. The foolish pigeon was so desperate that he deliberately jumped into the net and became ensnared. The hunter had now caught all of them and he took them away.

“The family members who find happiness in material sense objects and their community, and who have lost all presence of mind in taking care of their families, can never find peace. Just like that pigeon, they experience hardship along with their families. This human body is an open gateway to liberation. Although one may have attained the elevated human birth, if he is trapped in the bonds of house and family like that pigeon, he falls down. In the language of scriptures, he is called arudha-cyuta, fallen after attaining an elevated position.

9\)    The Python

“Living entities experience suffering due to their previous activities (karma). Thus, suffering comes although they do not want it, they do no effort for it, and even in spite of making every effort to avoid it. In the same way, happiness also comes without seeking it. Therefore, a wise person who knows the mystery of happiness and suffering should neither desire nor endeavour for them. Just like a python, a wise person should sustain his life by accepting whatever comes effortlessly, without asking and without desiring, whether it is dry and stale or sweet and delicious, in greater or lesser quantity, and always remain indifferent (udasina). This was the lesson I learned from the python. Taking refuge in the Lord and not being agitated into seeking one’s own pleasure, one should serve Sri Guru and Bhagavan. Those who wander here and there, impelled by greed to satisfy the tongue or belly, can never attain the service of Sri Krsna.

10\)    The Ocean

“From the ocean, I learned that a practitioner should always remain blissful and inscrutable (gambhira). His mood should be unfathomable, boundless and infinite. He should never lament for any reason. He should remain unchanged and composed, just like a tranquil ocean without high waves, tides and currents. The ocean is not increased by the flooding rivers during the monsoon season, nor does it decrease during the summer. Similarly, a devotee immersed in service to Bhagavan should not be overjoyed by the acquisition of worldly objects nor saddened by their loss.

11\)    The Moth

“From the moth I have learned that just as a moth who is bewildered by the beauty of a fire flies into it and perishes, similarly, the soul who does not control his senses becomes entangled in ephemeral objects such as beautiful women, gold, ornaments and clothing. His consciousness becomes smeared with impressions of material pleasure which give rise to further desires. Overwhelmed by desires, he becomes devoid of knowledge, and just like a moth he falls into the mouth of death.

12\)    The Honeybee

“I have learned from the honeybee that just as a bee gathers a little nectar from various flowers, similarly, a renunciant should collect alms from different households. If a bee becomes attached to the scent of a single lotus flower, then when the lotus petals close at night, the bee is trapped and dies. In the same way, by eating at the house of only one householder due to the desire for tasty food, even a renunciant will be trapped in the illusion of household life and perish. Just as the honeybee collects nectar from flowers, big or small, a wise person should extract the essence and rasa from all scriptures, big or small.

“From honeybees and flies we receive many instructions. A bumblebee only drinks the nectar of flowers, whereas a fly tastes everything. The fly sees ripe mangoes, jackfruit, sewage and rotten garbage with equal vision. The fly finds the same satisfaction in an open wound, a leprotic ulcer, a decomposing body and bloody mucus as he does when enjoying sweet rice pudding. The fly considers the foul odour of stool and the fine fragrance of sandalwood to be the same.

“The syncretists (samanvayavadis) who equate spirit (cit) and matter (jada) are like this fly, and the pure devotees are like the honeybee. The impersonalists (mayavadis) conflate the conditioned living entities and Lord Visnu; the spiritual and material energy; the pure transcendental existence (nirguna suddha-sattva) and the mixed material existence (saguna misra-sattva); that which is free from designations (nirupadhika) and that which is covered by designations (saupadhika); transcendental loving pastimes (cid-vilasa lila) and inert material lust (Jada-vilasa kama); the supernatural (aprakrta) and the mundane (prakrta); the liberated and the bound; the perfected soul (siddha) and the practitioner (sadhaka). This is called samanvayavada.

"Experts in medical scriptures say that a fly is more dangerous than a venomous snake in terms of its potential to cause harm. A fly serves as the primary carrier of epidemics, leading to the widespread destruction of lives. The impersonalists act in the same way as flies. Therefore, it is the essential duty of everyone who desires health and well-being to discard the harmful company of the fly-like impersonalists.

13\)    The Elephant

“A she-elephant is sent to lure a male elephant into a deep pit concealed by straw. The elephant, desiring the company of the she-elephant, becomes trapped and loses his independence for a long time. A discriminating person should never pray for the company of a woman. Doing so would lead him to bondage for a long time. This was the lesson I learned from the elephant.

14\)    The Honey Gatherer

“I have learned from the honey gatherer that although worldly greedy men accumulate wealth with great difficulty, they neither give it as charity to someone else nor enjoy it themselves. Just as the honey gatherer takes away and enjoys the honey collected by the bees, similarly, the accumulated wealth of greedy men will be enjoyed by someone else. The honey collected by the bees is wiped away before they have the chance to eat it. Similarly, the householders undergo much hardship to accumulate material possessions, which they hope to enjoy later. But a person in the renounced order of life has the first right to accept this wealth for the service of Sri Hari. This was the lesson I learned from the honey gatherer.

15\)    The Deer

“I learned from the deer that a renunciant should never listen to songs or discussions related to worldly matters. They should learn this lesson by seeing how the deer who becomes enchanted by the music of the hunter is then captured. Rsyasrnga Muni, who was born of a deer, became attached to the dancing, singing, and playing of musical instruments of beautiful women and came under their control. Therefore, listening to any kind of worldly talk (gramya-varta), songs or narratives must lead to bondage as in the case of the deer.

16\)    The Fish

“A fish loses its life due to greed to taste the bait on a hook. Similarly, the mind of a foolish and greedy person, driven by the desire to taste, becomes controlled by the tongue. The tongue gives him terrible anxiety and eventually leads him to death. A person of discrimination may quickly gain mastery over the other senses, but this will not bring the sense of taste under control. It becomes even stronger by giving up food. Even if a person gains victory over all the other senses, they cannot conquer their senses until they bring the sense of taste under control. Whereas, if one has controlled the sense of taste, all the other senses have become controlled.

17\)    The Courtesan Pingala

“In ancient times, in the city of Videha, there lived a courtesan named Pingala. She adorned herself with the finest clothes and ornaments and stood near the outer gate of her home for a long time in the hope of attracting a client. Whenever she would see a man passing by, she would think, ‘Surely this man will fulfil my desires’ One after another, men passed by, but none of them fulfilled the courtesan’s expectations.

“At that moment, she felt extremely disappointed and a feeling of renunciation arose within her heart. She began to reflect on how she had sold her body in pursuit of wealth, serving effeminate, lusty men. Gradually, she realized how she had become attached to the male human body that undergoes various transformations. Repenting over this, she said, ‘There is no enjoyer of the soul of a living entity except for Sri Hari. By constitution, all are female and Bhagavan is the only male.’ Such a perspective arose in her mind. She abandoned all worldly hopes and ambitions, and began to desire only service to Sri Hari. From the courtesan Pingala, I have learned that relinquishing all worldly hopes and ambitions, accepting the teaching of surrender, and offering oneself at the lotus feet of Sri Hari is the highest act of wisdom.

18\)    The Kurara Bird (Osprey)

“Once a kurara bird was carrying a piece of flesh in its beak. Seeing this, many other powerful birds who did not have any meat surrounded the kurara bird and started to attack him to snatch the meat. When he let go of the piece of meat, the other birds left him alone and finally he found peace. The lesson learned from the kurara bird is that no one harms the living entity who has given up harming others. If one is eager to attain divine love for the Lord, no one can create hostility towards him. The purport is that even if someone holds hostility towards him, the devotee does not experience the absence of happiness in his heart.

19\)    The Child

“One whose heart holds no concern for personal honour and dishonour, home, sons and possessions can wander in perpetual happiness. Both an ignorant child and a highly learned devotee of Bhagavan can wander without a care in a state of supreme joy. The more one’s mind is absorbed in worldly matters, the more one will taste suffering. To remain detached like a child and be constantly immersed in the joy of serving Bhagavan is the path to attain true peace. This lesson I have learned from the child.

20\)    The Young Maiden

“Once upon a time, some guests came to see a girl of marriageable age at her home. At that time, she happened to be alone in the house because the rest of her family members had gone elsewhere. Therefore, she took responsibility for hosting the guests herself. She began to prepare their meal alone in the inner chamber of the house. While she was threshing rice paddy, the conch shell bangles on her wrists were colliding together and making a loud noise. She felt embarrassed because the guests would infer that her family were too poor to hire a servant. She quietly removed the bangles, one by one, leaving only two on each wrist, and resumed husking the rice paddy. The remaining bangles still made too much noise, so she removed one more from each wrist. When only one bangle remained on each hand, there was no sound at all. “I learned from the young girl that, like the bangle, one should wander alone in an uninhabited place. Where many people with different goals and intentions live together, there will certainly be altercations between them. One cannot attain success in life where there are many ambitious persons with ulterior motives. Those with similar mental inclinations (samana citta-vrtti) should reside together, under the guidance of one realized spiritual master (sad-guru), for the divine purpose of serving Sri Krsna. If they assemble to serve Sri Krsna and perform kirtana in the same mood, then there will be no loss of concentration (ekagrata). Serving Sri Krsna in the company of persons who are like-minded (samana citta-vrtti) is real solitude (nirjanata). Otherwise, even while staying in solitude, the poison of discord continues to grow in the heart.

21\)    The Arrow Maker

“Once, an arrow maker became so absorbed in crafting an arrow that he did not notice the king’s royal parade was passing before him with great pomp and pageantry. From this arrow maker, I learned that one who is surrendered to Bhagavan does not remain absorbed in external matters. He even performs his physical bodily maintenance out of habit, remaining at every moment absorbed in glorifying the Holy Names and qualities of Bhagavan and serving the saints.

22\)    The Serpent

“A snake used to roam alone freely without any specific residence. He was always alert and nobody knew of his movements. He did not make much noise. Entering into homes built by others, he passed his time in great comfort. “I have learned from the serpent that one should not rely on anyone. One should not accept anyone’s service. A renunciant should not have any specific residence like a householder. One who engages in devotion to the Lord (bhajana) will always remain alert like a snake. If one is not engaged in devotional service under the protection of God-realized saintly association (sadhu-sanga), then the illusory energy Maya can come and destroy their spiritual life at any time. Every subject, except discussions about Sri Hari, is simply trivial talk. Engaging in futile conversations is not becoming of a devotee. Becoming entangled in the trouble of building a home for this temporary physical body is a futile and distressing pursuit. Those who unnecessarily carry the burden of futile worldly responsibilities are called ‘bharavahi', burden-carriers (bhara: burden + vahi: carrier). They undergo various hardships to construct huge skyscrapers and make new scientific discoveries in fields such as technology and transport. Only the devotees who utilize such resources for the service of Sri Hari can be called 'saragrahi', the accepters of essence (sara: essence + grahi: one who accepts).

23\)    The Spider

“The spider produces a web from within himself through his mouth, wanders within the web, and then swallows it again. Similarly, the Supreme Lord creates this universe from within Himself, wanders within it in the form of the living entities, and then again withdraws it within Himself. Therefore, without becoming intoxicated and lost in the illusory world of Mahamaya, it is our duty to remain always engaged in the service of Bhagavan.

24\)    The Wasp

“I have learned from the wasp that if a living being deliberately directs his attention towards any entity with undivided focus, be it out of affection, hostility, or even fear, he will eventually attain the same form and nature as that entity. The wasp confines a small insect to its residence in a small hole in a wall. The insect, by continuous absorption in thinking of the wasp out of fear, transforms into a wasp without even leaving its former body.

“The purport is that a practitioner on the path of spontaneous devotion (raga-marga), who continuously serves the Holy Name (nama-bhajana) by hearing, chanting and remembering, and who remains absorbed in his internally realized spiritual body, can swiftly and naturally become a jivanmukta. That is, he attains the state of liberation while still living. His body attains the quality of eternal existence, consciousness and bliss (sac-cid-ananda) like Bhagavan.”        Mahaprabhu has stated:

*diksa-kale bhakta kare atma-samarpana*  
*sei-kale krsna tare kare atma-sama*  
*sei deha kare tara cid-ananda-maya*  
*aprakrta-dehe tanra carana bhajaya*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Antya-lila 4.192-193*

**At the time of initiation, when a devotee fully surrenders unto the service of the Lord, Sri Krsna accepts him to be as good as Himself. When the devotee’s body is thus transformed into spiritual existence, in that transcendental body, he renders service to the lotus feet of the Lord.**

The avadhuta brahmana acquired all these instructions by accepting the aforementioned twenty-four gurus. He also learned many lessons from his own body. Attachment to the body makes the soul forget his eternal welfare. The avadhuta brahmana considered that one day dogs and jackals will take this body and make a festival, that is, it will become a feast for them. Therefore, considering his body to be the property of others, he wandered while researching the Absolute Truth, Sri Bhagavan. The conditioned soul faces immense suffering to earn wealth for the sake of physical pleasure. By this wealth, he expands and protects his family, property, animals, servants and home. When his life span is complete, the body naturally perishes, just as a tree makes a seed which becomes the next tree and the original tree eventually perishes. In this way, all arrangements are also made for the suffering of the next generation. Just as many co-wives each try to pull their husband towards themselves, similarly, the tongue, skin, stomach, ears, nose, eyes, and other senses always trouble the person who is attached to the body by pulling him towards themselves.

The conditioned beings, who are bound by the illusory energy, Maya, attain a human birth after wandering through the cycle of birth and death in 8,400,000 species of life. Among these births, there are 900.000    aquatic species, two million plant species, 1,100,000 varieties of insects, one million types of birds, three million animal species and 400.000    types of humans. Finally, after attaining all these various life forms, one attains the association of saints and the eligibility to hear their instructions. This human body is more suitable for serving the Lord than the bodies of the celestial demigods, because celestial beings become intoxicated by the pleasures of the celestial realm and forget their eternal welfare. Therefore, an intelligent person who has attained this human body incessantly endeavours to serve the Supreme Lord. Raising the objection that one who serves is deprived of worldly pleasures is not a symptom of intelligence. Bodies and minds which are suitable for sense enjoyment and the objects for satisfying them are available in all other species of life. However, the opportunity to serve an authentic spiritual master (sad-guru) and Sri Krsna is not possible in any birth other than this valuable human birth.

## Chapter 29 The mendicant of avanti city

In the city of Avanti (district of Malava), a brahmana had earned a huge fortune in commerce and agriculture. However, he was extremely miserly, refusing to share even a penny of his accumulated wealth with the demigods, relatives, guests or anyone else. Witnessing his extremely stingy nature, which was like that of a treasure-hoarding creature (yaksa), no one liked him, including his sons, wife, daughters, friends and the demigods. Gradually, due to the wrath of the demigods, his wealth began to diminish. By the influence of time, he was assailed with various misfortunes such as fires, robberies, natural calamities and atrocities committed by the king and other people. When his wealth was completely exhausted, his relatives began to thoroughly neglect him.

Seeing the behaviour of his family, the brahmana’s heart was first seized with anguish. Then detachment arose within. Filled with regret, the brahmana said, “Alas! Even after enduring so much hardship to acquire wealth, I could not attain piety (dharma) or sense enjoyment (каmа). I have given difficulties to my own body in vain. In the seeking, attaining, increasing, protecting, spending, losing and enjoying of wealth, all men experience great labour, fear, anxiety and delusion. Robbery, violence, lies, arrogance, lust, anger, shock, pride, quarrelling, enmity, distrust, competition, illicit sex, gambling and intoxication -these fifteen impediments arise from the desire for wealth. Even loved ones such as brothers, spouse, father and friends become enemies over a little wealth. Rare is the human birth. Who is a greater fool than one who, having obtained a human birth, becomes entangled in the snare of these impediments in pursuit of wealth and abandons devotion to Bhagavan? Lord Sri Hari is most certainly pleased with me. Only by His grace did I fall into this present predicament, and only by His grace has this detachment, which is the means of deliverance from the ocean of material existence, arisen in my heart. Even Maharaja Khatvanga attained the spiritual world, Vaikuntha, through his sadhatia in one muhurta (48 minutes). Hence, by the grace of the Lord, it is not impossible for me to attain auspiciousness within a short time as well.”

After reflecting upon his situation in this way, the Avanti brahmana accepted the renounced order of life signified by the triple-staff (tridandi-sannyasa). Worldly people engage the body, mind and words in both enjoyment and renunciation of worldly pleasures. However, one must withdraw one’s body, mind and words from both enjoyment and renunciation, and engage them in the service of Bhagavan through hearing, chanting and remembering His Holy Name. This is the true meaning of tridandi-sannyasa, triple-staff-bearing renunciation. Since such renunciates engage their body, mind and words in serving God, ordinary people, considering the renunciates to be different from themselves, often subject them to various types of harassment and criticism.

Seeing this elderly mendicant from Avanti in shabby attire, members of the public began to verbally abuse and harass him in various ways. Not understanding that the triple-staff (tridanda) is a symbol of Lord Narayana, some snatched it from him, thinking it to be an ordinary branch of a tree. Others began to snatch his water pot, his rosary, clothes, bowl and other belongings. He sat down by the riverbank and offered to the Lord the grains he had collected by begging. When he began to eat, some wicked persons incited children to spit on him and to urinate and throw sand, ashes and dirt in his food. They passed air on him and attempted to disturb him in numerous ways. Despite this, the ascetic remained unperturbed and did not utter a word. Seeing this, the perpetrators intensified their torment to make him speak. Yet, without becoming even slightly agitated, the ascetic accepted everything, considering it to be the punishment of fate (karma) and the mercy of Bhagavan. Despite the attempts of degenerate people to deviate the renunciate from his duty (svadharma) by insulting and criticizing him, he adhered to his noble duty with serene patience and began to sing this song:

"Humans, demigods, the soul, home, actions (karma) and time are not the cause of my joy or sorrow. It is the mind that perpetuates the cycle of material existence. Only the mind is the cause of joy and sorrow. The mind is stronger than the strongest and is terrible even for the yogis. Therefore, the one who has mastered this mind has conquered all the senses. They are the greatest fools who uselessly quarrel with others without conquering this enemy, the mind. They have accepted the real enemy as a friend. Except for developing love in the service of Sri Krsna’s lotus feet, there is no other way to conquer the mind. Therefore, I will take refuge in the knowledge acquired from the great souls of the past and transcend the infinite and boundless ocean of ignorance by serving the lotus feet of Lord Krsna.” Deliberating in this way, the triple-staff-bearing mendicant (tridandi-bhiksu) began to wander throughout the world, vigorously singing the glories of the Lord.      One should learn from the life of the tridandi-bhiksu that those who desire to be dear to the public, that is, dear to worldly people who are indifferent to God, are nothing but materialistic sense enjoyers and mundane fruitive workers (karmis) impelled by the ambition for reputation. Srila Jagadananda Gosvami, an associate of Sri Caitanyadeva, has said:

*gaura-bhaja loka-raksa ekatre nisphala*

*Sri Prema Vivarta 4.14*

**The worship of Lord Gauranga and protecting society cannot coexist. It is futile to combine them.**

Serving the external world is not devotion to God. From the very beginning of devotional service to Bhagavan, worldly persons try to divert those engaged in devotion from the path of truth by various types of criticism and harassment. The onset of such harassment signifies the authenticity of devotion to Lord Hari. As described in the Srimad-Bhagavatam, the living entities who are indifferent to God (bahirmukha jivas) become adversaries of those engaged in His service.

The demigods feel that devotees are surpassing their status and going beyond their world to Vaikuntha. Therefore, they also create various obstacles to impede their progress. It is not only the demons who hinder those who serve Hari. To crush these obstacles underfoot and go to the spiritual realm, Vaikuntha, one must follow the path of the previous great sages.

The tridandi-bhiksu has given us the plan of this path. He has shown us the path of controlling the mind without causing distress to others. By instructing his mind, he has taught the world that the auspicious duty of the soul is to focus only on the selfless service of Sri Mukunda, disregarding harassment, torture and hundreds of complaints and allegations by worldly deluded individuals. The torment inflicted by godless persons is not antagonistic to devotion. It is in reality favourable for devotion.

## Chapter 30 The devotee hunter

One day, while travelling through a forest for pilgrimage, Narada Muni saw a deer struggling after being wounded by an arrow and falling to the ground. Further along the path, he witnessed a pig in a similar half-dead suffering state injured by an arrow. Moving on, he saw a rabbit also flailing in distress. Narada Muni felt deeply troubled upon seeing these animals afflicted by pain. While pondering who could be so cruel, Narada Muni saw a hunter standing in the shade of a tree. The hunter was waiting there to ambush and kill more animals. With a fearsome appearance, dark complexion, red eyes, and holding a bow and arrow in his hand, he seemed like a messenger of the lord of death, Yamaraja, the chastiser.

Seeing the hunter, Narada Muni changed his path and walked towards him. All the animals ran away on seeing Narada Muni. The hunter’s anger knew no limits, yet, by Narada Muni’s astonishing influence, the hunter did not rebuke him. Though he did not directly insult Sri Narada, his anger burned within. The hunter said, “Gosvami! О    holy man! Why did you come here leaving your own path? Seeing you, all the animals I was aiming at ran away!”

Narada Muni said, “I have come to you because I have a doubt. Why have I seen so many half-killed animals on the way? It seems that this is all your doing. If you want to kill those animals, why leave them in a half-dead suffering state? It would be better to kill them straight away.”

The hunter replied, “О respectable Gosvami. My name is Mrgari (enemy of the animals). I have learned to do this from my father. When animals suffer in pain and struggle, it gives me more pleasure. By killing them outright, I will not experience that kind of happiness.”

Narada Muni said, “O Hunter, I request from you some alms.”

The hunter replied, “All right. If you want animals, I will give you some animals. And if you want deerskin, come with me to my house. I will give you deerskin or tiger skin. Whatever you desire I will give you.”

Narada Muni said, “I don’t want any of that from you. I only request that if you are going to kill an animal, ensure you kill it in one go. Don’t leave the animal in a crippled state.”

The hunter asked, “What benefit will you gain from such alms? What difference does it make if I leave an animal in a crippled state?”

Narada Muni explained, “By this the animals suffer pain. As you are causing pain to others, you will also have to suffer in the same way. Your killing of others is a grave sin. But half-killing them in this way is even more sinful. In your next lives, each of those animals will torment you in the same way you have tormented them. Whatever you do to others will come back to you.”

The hunter became fearful after hearing these words from Narada Muni. “How many animals have I tortured every day? What immense suffering must I endure in the future?” His mind became perturbed at the thought of it. The hunter humbly asked Narada Muni for guidance. “Respected Gosvami, I have been doing this since childhood. How can I now be free from this sin? I seek refuge at your feet. Please save me.”

Narada Muni said, “If you act according to my instructions, I can relieve you from your sins.”

The hunter replied, “I will do whatever you say.”

Narada Muni said, “First of all, break your bow and throw it away. After that, I will advise you.”

The hunter asked, “How will I sustain my life after breaking my

bow?”

Narada Muni said, “I will arrange food for you.”

Being assured in this way, the hunter immediately broke his bow and fell at Narada Muni’s feet. Sri Narada lifted up the hunter and advised him, “O Hunter, go back to your home and give away to the brahmanas all the wealth earned from your sinful deeds. Wearing one simple cloth, you and your wife should give up your home and build a hut by the riverside. There, create a platform for Tulasi (Holy Basil). Daily serve and circumambulate Tulasi and continuously chant Sri Krsna’s name at every moment. I will send sufficient food for both of you. Only eat as much as you need.”

Then Narada Muni miraculously healed the wounded deer, pig, rabbit, and other animals simply by his touch. Witnessing this supernatural event, the hunter was struck with wonder and gradually devotion to the lotus feet of Sri Gurudeva began to flow, infusing his heart. When Narada Muni departed, the hunter returned home and implemented all his instructions. The news spread throughout the village that the ruthless hunter had transformed into a saintly person by the grace of his guru. Consequently, everyone in the village came to see the hunter and they began to bring food for him. Every day, ten to twenty people would bring various kinds of food and drink, but the hunter only took what was necessary for himself and his wife. He did not take more than what he needed.

After a few days, Narada Muni, accompanied by Parvata Muni, came to visit the hunter. Seeing his gurudeva from a distance, the hunter prostrated himself and then began to walk towards him. When he saw some ants on his pathway, he gently swept the path with his cloth and then again offered obeisances on the ground. Observing the mood of non-violence in the hunter, Narada Muni commented, “O Hunter, your transformation is not surprising. Those in whose hearts devotion to Lord Hari has awakened can never cause harm to others. Non-violence is an incidental characteristic of a devotee’s intrinsic nature.”

The hunter offered his gurudeva and Parvata Muni a comfortable seat made of kusa grass and requested them to be seated. He washed their feet with water and, along with his wife, drank that sacred water (caranamrta). While chanting the Holy Name of Sri Krsna, ecstatic symptoms (sattvika-bhavas) manifested in his body. Raising his arms, he began to sing and dance in love for Sri Krsna. Seeing such divine love in the hunter, Parvata Muni glorified Narada Muni, “You are a touchstone (sparia-mani). Therefore, by your touch even iron has turned to gold.”

From the life of the devotee hunter, we learn that even a violent person can attain the supreme benefit by the influence of association with saintly persons. The great devotees of the Lord (maha-bhagavatas) are truly touchstones. If one commits no offence to them and follows their instructions sincerely, even a violent person like that extremely sinful hunter can attain the ultimate goal of life, devotion to Lord Hari, and become blessed.

We can also derive a lesson from the ideal of the hunter’s wife. Sometimes, it is observed that although a husband has a change of heart and becomes a devotee, the wife does not change because she fears that she will no longer be able to enjoy material sense gratification. Therefore, she is not prepared to follow her husband’s example and serve Sri Krsna. In fact, only a woman who adheres to her husband’s ideals of devotion to Bhagavan, who even accepts hundreds of difficulties, remaining bereft of material pleasures, and who helps her husband in his devotion with complete dedication has the right to be called a wife (sahadharmini) or sati (chaste woman). And a husband who does not engage his spouse in devotion to Sri Hari cannot be called a true husband. Such a person is cruel to his wife and more dangerous than a wild animal.

## Chapter 31 Morality, immorality and the transcedent morality of devotion (suniti, durniti and bhakti- niti)

Sri Ramanuja was an ancient preceptor (acarya) of the Sri Vaisnava tradition. He appeared in this world near Mahabhutapuri in Perambedur Nagara, close to Madras, in the year 1016. Before Sri Ramanuja, the preachers of this philosophy were referred to in the Dravidian language as ‘Alvaras’, denoting great liberated associates of Bhagavan. Among the Alvaras, there was a revered saint known as Tirumangai Alvara. Some say that he appeared in the eighth century.

In his youth, he travelled to pilgrimage sites and dedicated himself to the devotional service of Bhagavan.
