His sole principle was that all things in the world have been created for the service of Lord Narayana. Therefore, it is one’s duty to serve Lord Narayana with all these things in the most appropriate manner. He believed that there is no meaningful purpose for any object or living being if it is not utilized in the service of Lord Narayana. While travelling to various pilgrimage sites, four individuals endowed with extraordinary qualities accepted him as their spiritual master and became his disciples.

The first disciple was named ‘Todavadakkun’ or ‘Tarka Cudamani’, signifying that no one could defeat him in logical debate. The second disciple was called ‘Tadaduyan’ or ‘Dvaronmocaka’. He could open any security lock just by blowing on it. The third disciple was ‘Nedelai Merippan’ or ‘Chaya Graha’ Whenever he touched someone’s shadow with his feet, they would become motionless. The fourth disciple named ‘Nilmel Nadappan’ or ‘Jaloparicara’ had the ability to walk on water.

Tirumangai, along with his four disciples, travelled on a pilgrimage to visit various holy places. During this time, they arrived at an ancient and dilapidated temple where the four-armed form of Lord Ranganatha reclined upon the bed of Sesa. At that time, this famous temple was the residence of animals and birds, and it was surrounded by a jungle inhabited by ferocious animals. A servant would come for a brief moment every day. He would place fruit and water in front of the Lord and then quickly run away from there as soon as possible.

After witnessing this, Tirumangai was deeply inspired to build a magnificent temple for Sri Ranganatha. Along with his disciples, he travelled to different countries, seeking alms for the construction of the temple. However, all the wealthy individuals they approached called them scammers, bounders and thieves and chased them away without giving even a penny.

Tirumangai was not disheartened by this. Rather, he became even more determined to serve Sri Ranganatha. He called his four disciples and said, “My children! You have witnessed how the wealthy are blinded by their riches. Narayana, the husband of Laksmi, the Goddess of Fortune, has deposited a small portion of His own wealth with them. They should use that wealth to serve Narayana. However, they have misappropriated this wealth and assumed that they are the owners. How averse to the service of Sri Narayana they are, as they live a life of luxury. They rest on beds as soft as the foam of milk in their lofty towers while the deity of Sri Narayana is lying broken and neglected in the jungle. Alas! They do not even think about this injustice. It is the duty of wealthy householders to serve and worship Lord Visnu. Otherwise, their journey towards hell is inevitable. Therefore, auspiciousness must be bestowed upon these affluent people by any means possible.”

Then Tirumangai properly utilized the great talents of his disciples in Visnu’s service and manifested his desire to bring auspiciousness to those wealthy people. He summoned his first disciple, Tarka Cudamani, and instructed him to ensnare wealthy individuals in the net of logical debates. While the debate was going on, the second disciple, Dvaronmocaka, opened the doors of the treasury using his skills and freely collected together masses of treasure. Through his disciple Chaya Graha, Tirumangai made the wealthy householders motionless and looted all their wealth. Through the disciple capable of walking on water, he collected a significant amount of wealth from the royal towns surrounded by water. It seemed as if he were the leader of a formidable group of dacoits, amassing an immense amount of wealth for the service of Ranganatha.

Tirumangai gathered renowned sculptors from various countries to commence the construction of the temple. After the labour of a thousand sculptors for four years, the first outer portion was completed, and in two more years the second and third. In eight years the fourth, in twelve years the fifth, and in eighteen years the sixth outer portion was completed. The construction of the complete temple took sixty years, by which time Tirumangai was eighty years old.

After the completion of the inner sanctum, the nearby king was eager to assist Tirumangai. Some were inspired on seeing his prosperity, while others assisted him in his service out of fear. They all acquired some piety (sukrti) by assisting a great saint (maha-purusa). Although it seems that Tirumangai adopted the occupation of a robber from an external perspective, because he never endeavoured for his own personal enjoyment, he was exclusively engaged in the service of Bhagavan. He did not spend a single penny of that wealth on himself.

When the construction of the temple of Sri Ranganatha, with seven concentric townships, was successfully completed, Tirumangai paid the builders their due wages. In the end, there was no money left over. In such a situation, almost a thousand robbers surrounded him and demanded money. Tirumangai whispered some advice in the ear of his disciple Jaloparicara, who was skilled in walking on water. Jaloparicara told the robbers that he would take them to the location where the stolen wealth was stored. He called for the same ship that had been used to bring huge stones for the construction of the Sri Ranganatha Temple. The robbers boarded the ship with Jaloparicara and they set off. The Kaveri River was overflowing at that time, during the monsoon season. Jaloparicara took the ship to the middle of the Kaveri River where he intentionally sank it, along with all the robbers. Jaloparicara walked on water and returned to his guru. Those robbers had actually come there to kill Tirumangai.

On the return of Jaloparicara, the great sage Tirumangai said, "The Kaveri River is the destroyer of sins and the bestower of devotion to Visnu. By giving the robbers samadhi (last rites) in the purifying waters of the Kaveri, their souls will undoubtedly be accepted in the service of Sri Ranganatha. Do not worry about them. By not allowing the robbers to loot and commit violence against vaisnavas, you have given them the opportunity to go to Vaikuntha. Is this not highly beneficial for them? We took their assistance only for the service of God. If anyone follows this path for personal gain, it will certainly be considered the terrible sin of murder and the path to hell. There is no doubt about this.” To this day, the northern part of the Kaveri River where the robbers were eliminated is still called ‘Koliran, meaning the place of murder.

From Tirumangai’s example, we learn the lesson that bhakti-niti, or the transcendent morality of serving the Supreme Lord, is superior to and cannot be compared with any worldly morality (suniti) or immorality (durniti). The morality and immorality prevalent in society must be done for the sake of the pleasure of the Supreme Lord, Sri Hari. In this regard, there are two scriptural statements found in the Bhakti Sandarbha (Anuccheda 148) of Srila Jiva Gosvami Prabhu. In the Reva-khanda of the Skanda Purana, Sri Brahma says:

*sa karta sarva-dharmanam bhakto yas tava kesava*  
*sa karta sarva-papanam yo na bhaktas tavacyuta*  
*papam bhavati dharmo'pi tavabhaktaih krto hare nihsesa-dharma-karta vapy abhakto narake hare*  
*sada tisthati bhaktas te brahmahapi vimucyate*

**О Kesava! One who is Your devotee has performed all religious duties (dharma). О Acyuta! One who is not Your devotee has committed all sins. О Hari! The religious duty performed by one who is not Your devotee is only sin, and although he completely performs all religious duties, he still always resides in hell. However, even if Your devotee has killed a brahmana, he becomes liberated.**

In the Padma Purana, Bhagavan explains:

*man-nimittam krtam papam api ksemaya kalpate*  
*mam anadrtya dharmo’pi papam syan mat-prabhavatah*

**The sinful actions performed by My devotee for My sake are considered virtuous deeds, whereas the righteous actions performed without honour for Me turn into sinful acts by My influence.**

Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura would narrate this anecdote to illustrate that the character of a true devotee of Hari cannot be measured by the insignificant yard stick of sense perception (pratyaksa-jnana) because all their actions are solely for the satisfaction of Sri Hari. In devotion (bhakti), there is a beautiful harmony of all things. The great devotees, like snake charmers, can take along with them even cruel and wicked individuals, who are like poisonous snakes, and still accomplish their tasks. However, if someone else imitates this, his demise is inevitable.

From an external perspective, the immortal preceptor (acarya) shows the pastime of accepting many persons with ulterior, material motives (anyabhilasi) to assist in his devotional service and to generate spiritual merit (sukrti) for the living beings. However, when those individuals begin to adopt the attitude of a merchant (vanija-vrtti) in their dealings with the spiritual master or the acarya, then the spiritual master destroys them for their own auspicious benefit. This truth is established by the examples of Sri Krsna’s annihilation of the Yadu dynasty and by Tirumarigai drowning the robbers who had assisted in the construction of the Sri Ranganatha temple in the Kaveri River.

## Chapter 32 The deceitful brahmana

Once upon a time, when a snake charmer was performing a dance at the residence of a wealthy man, Thakura Haridasa unexpectedly arrived there. He saw that Vasuki, the king of the snakes, had entered into the snake charmer. Being thus possessed, the snake charmer was dancing in the mood of Vasuki, and his companions were singing about Sri Krsna’s pastime of chastising the serpent Kaliya (kaliya-damana-lila) in the melodic mode of compassion (karuna-raga). Thakura Haridasa, the best among devotees, fell unconscious to the ground in ecstasy upon hearing the song about this divine pastime. After some time, when he regained consciousness, he roared in ecstasy and began to dance in a state of bliss. The snake charmer, witnessing the extraordinary mood and expressive gestures of Thakura Haridasa, respectfully moved from the centre and stood to one side. Various ecstatic symptoms began to manifest in the body of Haridasa Thakura such as tears, horripilation and trembling. Sometimes Thakura Haridasa would cry out in a pitiful voice. Everyone gathered around Haridasa Thakura and began to sing the Holy Names of Sri Krsna.

After a while, when Haridasa returned to external consciousness, the snake charmer resumed his dance performance. On witnessing Haridasa Thakura’s unpretentious natural absorption in divine love, all members of the audience present there began to smear the foot dust of the great devotee upon their heads and their bodies.

In that gathering, there was a cunning and deceitful person who had been born in a brahmana lineage. He thought, “I am a prestigious brahmana and Haridasa is just a beggar born in a non-Hindu caste. Nowadays, people see the dancing of a low-class fool and become devoted to him. So if they were to see an exhibition of ecstatic sentiments by a prestigious brahmana like me, then they would definitely give me even greater respect.” The deceptive brahmana was overwhelmed with envy of Thakura Haridasa. Artificially showing ecstatic emotions, he became unconscious and fell to the ground. Understanding that this cunning person was trying to compete with Haridasa Thakura by making a display of false sentiments, the snake charmer began to beat him with his bamboo stick. As a result of the forceful blows, that imitator (prakrta-sahajiya) returned to his real character. Shouting, “Oh my God! Oh my God!”, he fled from the assembly.

Then the snake charmer, undeterred, resumed his dance performance. Later, the spectators folded their hands and humbly asked him, “Why did you respectfully stand aside when Thakura Haridasa was dancing, but when the brahmana began to dance you beat him with a bamboo stick?”

The snake charmer replied, “Vasuki, the king of snakes, is a pure devotee (vaisnava-naga). I was dancing because Vasuki had entered into me. The deceitful brahmin, impelled by envy of Thakura Haridasa, was trying to compete with him by making a show of artificial emotions just to gain respect from the public.      “Sri Krsna personally dances in the dance of Thakura Haridasa. By seeing his dancing, the entire brahmana community becomes purified and the soul’s bondage of illusion (Maya) is broken. Those deceitful pretenders (sahajiyas), who make a theatrical show of being absorbed in ecstasy just to woo the public, are greedy for worldly fame and respect. Such imitation of real devotees is nothing but hypocrisy.”

*haridasa-sange sparddha mithya karx kare*  
*ataeva sdsti bahu karilun uhare*

**Out of audacity, he tried to imitate Haridasa, and therefore I punished him accordingly.**

*“bada loka kari loka januka cimare”*  
*apanare prakatai dharma-karma kare*

**He presented himself as an important person by imitating some religious sentiments.**

*e-sakala dambhikera krsne priti nai*  
*akaitava haile se krsna-bhakti pai*

**Actually, that arrogant and deceitful brahmana has no love for Krsna. To achieve the devotional service of Lord Krsna, one must be free from duplicity.**

**Sri Caitanya Bhagavata, Adi-lila 16.227-229**

## Chapter 33 The result of enmity towards a devotee

This incident took place after the disappearance of Lord Sri Caitanya. The seat of the government of King Krsnananda Datta was situated in the village of Kheturl on the banks of the Padmavati River in the northern region of Narendrabhumi. Srila Narottama Thakura appeared as the son of King Krsnananda Datta. The king’s elder brother, Purusottama Datta, had a son named Santosa, who was expert in all scriptures and adept in governance. Sri Narottama Thakura renounced his vast kingdom and wealth and became deeply attached to the vaisnava tradition propounded by Lord Sri Caitanya. Seeing this, Santosa also became a disciple of Sri Narottama Thakura.

Thakura Narottama had a very close friend, Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja. They were both great realized devotees (maha-bhagavatas) of the Lord. While Sri Narottama resided in the village of Kheturi, absorbed in devotional service, Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja spent a considerable amount of time with him and they served the Lord together. People often remarked that they were two bodies of the same soul like Visnu and Siva (hari-haratma).

A few days before the Saradiya Durga-puja, Thakura Srila Narottama and Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja were going to take bath in the Padmavati River. On the way, they saw two beautiful young brahmana boys, accompanied by their followers, enthusiastically leading a group of goats, buffaloes and sheep towards the Padmavati River. Observing this scene, Srila Narottama Thakura remarked to Sri Ramacandra, “These two brahmana boys appear to be very intelligent. If only they were engaged in hari-bhajana, their aristocratic birth, scholarship, wealth and beauty would all become successful.”

Srila Narottama Thakura and Sri Ramacandra pondered, “How can we broach the subject with these unknown brahmana boys without hesitation? Given the way they are taking goats, buffalo and sheep, it seems most likely that they are descendants of fanatical worshippers of Sakti (Durga). Under the circumstances, if we speak about vaisnava-dharma, they will not have faith in our words. Still, we must adopt an approach to somehow make their lives auspicious.”

Ramacandra Kaviraja and Srila Narottama Thakura began to walk very slowly while discussing scriptural topics within earshot of the young brahmana boys, who were overhearing their conversation with great interest. As they overheard the discussion, which was full of elegant logic and scriptural evidence, whatever doubts they had regarding vaisnava-dharma were dispelled, and their consciousness (citta) became purified. The two brahmana boys began to express to each other, “We have heard about the greatness of Sri Narottama Thakura and Ramacandra Kaviraja from others. Witnessing their knowledge of scriptures, it seems that they are both indeed great souls (mahatmas). Today, we are very fortunate to have their audience. However, we now have with us goats, sheep, and other items for the worship of Sakti. How can we present ourselves before those great vaisnavas with all these things?”

Then the brahmana boys left the goats and sheep at some distance. Being somewhat fearful and hesitant, they approached Narottama Thakura and Ramacandra Kaviraja. Thakura Narottama looked at the two brahmanas and asked them to introduce themselves. The elder one introduced himself, “I am Harirama Bhattacarya,” and the young one said, “I am Sri Ramakrsna Bhattacarya and our father is Sri Sivananda Bhattacarya.”

Narottama Thakura then asked, “Where were you taking the goats and sheep?”

Harirama Bhattacarya explained, “Every year, my father spends a lot of money on performing Durga-puja, the worship of Sakti. He is not interested in violence towards living beings, but by sacrificing the goats, sheep and buffalo to goddess Durga, one obtains everlasting heaven (svarga). He is motivated by this religious greed. On his order, we have purchased these ingredients for the puja from the market. We know Sri Balarama Kaviraja. He is a great vaisnava. We used to hear about vaisnava-dharma from him, and we were aware that causing harm to living beings is a great sin. By listening to your scriptural discussions, whatever doubts we had about this have been dispelled. Kindly give us lowly persons the shelter of your lotus feet and thereby make your name 'patita pavana’, saviour of the fallen, successful. We will not engage in violence towards ourselves and others anymore. We are leaving these goats and sheep here. We will not take them to our father to be sacrificed. By your grace, our eyes of knowledge (jhana-caksu) have been opened.”

Having said this, Harirama Bhattacarya called his companions and subordinates together and instructed them, “Leave aside all those innocent creatures and immediately cross to the other side of the Padmavati River. I will stay here now.”

Hearing these words from the mouth of their master’s eldest son, the servants were astonished. They remarked, “All these animals were brought for sacrifice to goddess Durga, and these two were also supposed to take part in the celebration. How have they become so confused? Have they become mad under the spell of these deceitful vaisnavas? What type of magic do they know? It is a grave sin to give up the animals brought for Durga-puja. When the master learns about all this, he will surely punish his two sons and their followers. And what will people say? The priest and the master himself are both waiting for these ingredients for the puja. How will the worship take place if all these items do not arrive on time? Everything will be a complete mess.” As the servants were discussing the situation, Harirama Bhattacarya, not paying heed to their words, ordered them to release all the animals. Being obliged to obey, they left the animals behind and crossed the river.

The thirst of Harirama and Sri Ramakrsna began to intensify. They were anxious to attain the mercy of their guru. Overwhelmed with humility, with tears streaming from their eyes, they began to plead. Narottama Thakura and Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja lifted both brahmana boys from the ground, embraced and reassured them. After bathing in the Padmavati River, they took the brahmana boys to the temple of Thakura in Kheturi. In the temple, the deities established by Sri Narottama Thakura - Sri Gauranga, Sri Vallabhi-kanta, Sri Krsna, Sri Vraja-mohana, Sri Radha-kanta, and Sri Radha-ramana - were radiating divine splendour. As soon as one attains a vision (darsana) of an eternal spiritual master (sad-guru) who has realized the Absolute Truth (krsna-tattva), it is one’s duty to accept his grace without delay. Knowing this, on that very day, Harirama Bhattacarya received initiation (mantra-diksa) from Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja, and Sri Ramakrsna Bhattacarya received initiation from Srila Narottama Thakura. They considered Ramacandra and Sri Narottama to be one in principle (tattva), so both disciples considered both saints as their guru. They never distinguished between them. Through the transmission of the divine power of the two great devotees and by hearing the conclusions of devotion (bhaktisiddhanta) from them, Harirama and Sri Ramakrsna both became expert in scriptural reasoning and steadfast in their faith.

After Vijayadasami, on the day of Ekadasi, Harirama and Sri Ramakrsna took the dust from the lotus feet of their gurus upon their heads and left Kheturi. They went to the village of Goyasa Grama where they met with Balarama Kaviraja. They spent the night at Balarama Kaviraja’s house and they explained to him what had happened and all about Sivananda. The next day, Sivananda met with both sons. On seeing them dressed in vaisnava attire, Sivananda was furious. Losing all composure out of anger, he began to scold them in vulgar language in front of hundreds of people. “Fools! Disgraceful blemishes upon your dynasty! You two were born in my house just to tarnish my noble lineage. You have cut off the nose and ears of your own ancestors. Whoever sees you will have to take bath in the Ganges with all their clothes on as an atonement. If you had accepted a non-Hindu religion, that would be understandable. But you fools have forsaken the brahmana-dharma and adopted vaisnava-dharma. You idiots! Where have you heard that a vaisnava is greater than a brahmana? Brahmamayi Ma (Durga) has given you a suitable punishment after so many days. You used to cunningly introduce yourselves as brahmanas. By forsaking the worship of the goddess Bhagavati, your human life is wasted. As soon as you deprived Bhagavati, you got caught in the illusion of the charlatan vaisnavas and became deprived yourself. We have never seen such a vaisnava, who makes a brahmana his disciple. I will bring panditas and quickly break the pride of your guru. Let us see how much knowledge he has. By the grace of goddess BhavanI Devi, you will see with your own eyes how your guru will be humiliated. Bhagavan Visnu holds the mark of a brahmana's foot on His chest. How your revered deities, Sri Krsna and Sri Caitanya, have honoured the brahmanas. Krsna was a ksatriya. Therefore, He used to worship the brahmanas so much. Your Caitanya used to drink the caranamrta of the brahmanas! And your guru, on the pretext of following Krsna and Caitanya, who were devoted to the brahmanas, has made brahmanas his disciples. We scholars will unite and expose this blatant hypocrisy. Vaisnavas accuse the iaktas of killing living beings by offering animal sacrifices. But what about them? The vaisnavas celebrate festivals by cutting the bellies of pumpkins and gourds. Is that not killing a living entity? They are just sentimentalists. Their religion is a recent invention and it is against the Vedas. Brahmana-dharma is the ancient and eternal Vedic religion .”

Hearing these statements of Sivananda, his son Harirama replied, his words blazing with spiritual potency, “What to speak of calling all your great scholars to defeat our gurus; I say, let them debate with me first. If they can defeat me with their scriptural arguments, then I will take you seriously. Otherwise, all your statements are as meaningful as the croaking of a frog.”

Hearing the words of his son, Sivananda became even more incensed. “You imbecile! You are like black coal, sullying and burning your dynasty! You say my words are the croaking of a frog. How dare you! You do not even consider me your father. Previously, the vaisnavas used to teach that one should be more humble than a blade of grass (trnad api su-nicena), but your guru has taught you to be arrogant and betray your father!”

Enraged and without further delay, Sivananda summoned a contingent of elite scholars. Those panditas initiated a philosophical discussion on the conclusions of the scriptures with Harirama Bhattacarya. Despite being younger in age, Harirama roared like a lion, while refuting all their non-devotional doctrines and establishing the glory of guru-bhakti. Harirama Bhattacarya presented extensive scriptural evidence from the Sruti, Smrti and the Puranas to demonstrate that vaisnavas are superior in every way and that the brahmanas are their servants. Brahmanahood is protected by becoming a servant of the vaisnavas. Otherwise, brahmanas fall down from their position. Harirama Bhattacarya proved that the vaisnavas’ worship of Bhagavan is transcendental to the modes of material nature (nirguna) and causeless (ahaituka). However, the worship of Brahmanda Bhandodari, Mahamaya (the external material energy, Durga, who contains the entire universe), performed with material desires, is included in the gunas- that is, it is mixed with the material modes of goodness, passion and ignorance. The devotees of Bhagavan perform all their activities solely for the satisfaction of Sri Krsna’s senses. Therefore, there is no violence in any of their actions. Vaisnavas do not desire dharma (religiosity), artha (wealth), kama (enjoyment) and moksa (liberation). From the Bhagavad-gita verse ‘sarva dharman parityajya’, it is understood that Lord Krsna protects His surrendered devotees from all kinds of sins. Those who surrender to Sri Krsna are not contaminated by panca-suna-papa (panca: five + suna: place of slaughter + papa: sin - the five ways in household life by which creatures may be accidentally destroyed: the fire place, the slab on which condiments are ground, the broom, the pestle and mortar, and the water pot). The vaisnava is freed from all debts to the demigods, sages, human beings, forefathers and other living entities. The vaisnava does not incur sins like the worshippers of various other demigods.

On hearing Harirama Bhattacarya’s irrefutable scripturally based conclusions, the panditas were astonished and began to confer amongst themselves. “We used to consider him an ordinary boy. How did Sivananda’s son gain such profound knowledge of the scriptures at such a young age? These two sons of Sivananda have undoubtedly obtained a boon from the goddess of learning, Sarasvatl. Such marvellous scholarship has appeared in them by the power of Narottama Thakura and Ramacandra Kaviraja. We do not believe that there is any scholar who could defeat them.”

Sivananda wanted to defeat his sons through the scholars, but the reverse happened. The scholars became stunned and speechless. They humbly returned to their respective residences. This outcome was just like adding ghee to the fire of Sivananda’s anger. He vowed, “I will bring an even greater undefeated scholar (digvijayi-pandita) and definitely break their pride.” He sent his capable assistant to Mithila and summoned the unparalleled scholar, Digvijayi-pandita Murari, to his village.

Murari, along with many of his disciples, arrived in the village. He was so proud of his knowledge that he used to consider everyone else to be as insignificant as pieces of straw. He had defeated all the panditas throughout the whole of India. So, it is not surprising that he considered Sivananda’s two young sons to be even less than pieces of straw. When he was informed about the scholarship of Harirama and Sri Ramakrsna, he responded, “It would be embarrassing for me to discuss the meaning of scripture with these boys. If they have a guru or a senior member of their group who is willing to engage in a battle of logic with me, only then will I agree to a debate. Otherwise, I will not use a cannon to kill a mosquito.” Then the most expert Balarama Kaviraja stepped forward to engage in scriptural discussions with the Digvijayi-pandita Murari. However, it is astonishing that the revered Kaviraja did not need to elaborate any further on vaisnava philosophy because he defeated Digvijayi Murari simply by using his own statements against him.

Murari had one virtuous quality. When defeated, he would accept his faults and never unjustly claim that he had won. He admitted, “I do not possess the ability to describe the glory of a vaisnava. Nothing is superior to a vaisnava".

Murari gave away everything he had, and considering himself unworthy to show his face again in his own country, he immediately adopted the occupation of a mendicant (bhiksuka-dharma). He renounced his pride in scholarship, considering it useless. Because he did not adopt the occupation of a digvijayi-pandita or the exclusive occupation of a devotee (vaisnava-dharma), the path he chose became known as ‘the third path of Murari’ (murarestritiyah panthah).

Sivananda Bhattacarya, overwhelmed with sorrow and shame, although still breathing, became as if dead. The goddess Bhagavati, Durga, had punished him appropriately for his enmity towards vaisnavas.

Оm Visnupada Sri Srimad Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Gosvami Prabhupada has taught us through this incident that from the moment one attains the vision (darsana) of a pure devotee spiritual master (vaisnava-sad-guru), it is necessary to take shelter of his lotus feet and be under his guidance. The religion that the common populace resort to is all contaminated by selfish desire (sakama) and mixed with the material modes of nature (saguna) due to their thirst for worldly righteousness, wealth, enjoyment or liberation. But the ultimate purpose of the consciousness of all living entities is love of God. This religion of love (prema-dharma) is the genuine occupation for all living beings at all times and in all places. To be initiated into this prema-dharma, one must promptly forsake the shelter of all other religious conceptions that promote non-devotional motives. One must accept the shelter of a true spiritual teacher who has realized Sri Krsna (krsna-tattvavit). It is not our duty to cover the path of pure bhakti by the imposition of any kind of worldly, social or so-called moral restrictions. If one’s mother, father or worldly gurus obstruct one’s service to Sri Hari, one must first politely try to make them understand. However, even after politely explaining everything, if they deny and oppose the path of devotion, then it is necessary to completely renounce their association, considering it to be harmful, and serve the Lord of all lords, the eternally worshipful of all worshipful persons, Sri Bhagavan, and His devotees without duplicity. In this regard, Lord Caitanya has stated:

*sei se parama bandhu sei pita, mata*  
*sri krsna caranejei prema-bhakti data*  
*sakala janme pita mata sabe paya*  
*krsna guru nahi mile bhajaha hiyaya*

*Sri Caitanya Bhagavata, Madhya-khanda*

**Не who gives love (prema-bhakti) for the lotus feet of Sri Krsna is one’s dearest friend, father and mother. Everyone attains a father and mother in every birth and species. However, it is extremely rare to attain Sri Krsna and Sri Guru. Therefore, in the human birth one should serve Sri Krsna under the guidance of Sri Guru.**

*gurur na sa syat sva-jano na sa syat*  
*pita na sa syaj janani na sa syat*  
*daivam na tat syan na patis ca sa syan*  
*a mocayedyah samupeta-mrtyum*

*Srimad-Bhdgavatam 5.5.18*

One who cannot deliver his dependents from the path of repeated birth and death is not worthy to be a spiritual master, relative, father, mother, husband or worshipful demigod.

## Chapter 34 The arrogance demon (dambha daitya) and the goddes of humility (Dinata Devi)

After Thakura Narottama gave mantra initiation to Sivananda Bhattacarya’s sons, Sri Ramakrsna and Sri Harirama Bhattacarya, many brahmanas such as Sri Ganga Narayana Cakravarti and Sri Jagannathacarya came to take shelter at the feet of Thakura Narottama. At that time, there was a mighty king named Raja Narasimha in Bangladesh. Many priestly scholars, the brahmana panditas, used to sit in his assembly. They became angry and complained to King Narasimha, “Narottama Dasa, the son of Krsnananda Datta, has adopted the vaisnava creed and he is ruining many people. We do not know what kind of magic he uses that comes with such force and effortlessly makes brahmanas become his disciples. People call him sastravid, a scholar of scriptures, but this is just his false egotism. He is called a scriptural scholar only in front of fools. He cannot even open his mouth in front of us. О King, please take us there immediately and then you will see for yourself how he becomes afraid in our presence. He will pack up his fake emotions and run away from there at that very moment. Then your fame will spread throughout the country and you will establish the dignity of brahmanas. The duty of the king is to punish. If you do not intervene against such an atrocity and disrespect towards the brahmana dynasty, then it will be destroyed very soon.”

Encouraged by the words of the brahmana scholars, King Narasimha decided to launch a campaign against Thakura Narottama. In jubilation, the teachers set out proudly carrying many books. King Narasimha personally led the campaign in the company of Rupa Narayana, who was famous as digvijayi-pandita, a scholar who has conquered all directions. The king, with his group of scholars and his military commanders, first established a camp in the village of Kumarapura near Kheturi. This news reached Narottama Thakura by word of mouth. Thakura Mahasaya spoke to Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja, who was non-different from his own soul (abhinnatma), “We will have to argue with teachers who are bereft of devotion to Bhagavan. It seems that this will obstruct our inner contemplation (bhajana) because they will not agree to accept any conclusion (siddhanta) of the eternally perfect scriptures (sat-fastra). They will remain steadfast in their arrogance.”

Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja said, “Please continue your bhajana without anxiety. You will see that the pride of all those arrogant people will be shattered very easily, and in the end, they will surrender at your feet.”

Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja consulted with Mahasaya Ganga Narayana Cakravarti. Both of them set out towards Kumarapura village. On the way, Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja disguised himself as ‘Varujivi’, a betel seller, and Sri Ganga Narayana Cakravarti as a potter. They reached Kumarapura village carrying some betel leaves and earthen pots on their heads, set up their shop there and sat down. Just then, a student of one of the teachers who were accompanying King Narasimha came to buy betel for his teacher. Mistaking the disguised Sri Ramacandra Kaviraja to be an ordinary betel seller, the student asked the price of betel in the Bengali language of the village. He was astonished to see that the betel seller replied in pure and perfect Sanskrit. Then the student, out of pride, also began to converse only in Sanskrit, but he could not defeat the statements of the betel seller. He was quickly defeated after saying only a few words in Sanskrit. It seemed as if the student’s face had been blackened with soot. The disguised betel seller said to the student, “You are very foolish. You do not have any knowledge. Bring your teacher. We will see how much knowledge and wisdom he has.”

The student, overcome with extreme anger and shame, went to his teacher immediately and told him the whole story, “Alas! Alas! I had to be defeated by a common betel seller. How will I ever show my face to people again?”

When he began to express his remorse in this way, the king said, “If the betel seller and the potter of the Kheturi village where Srila Narottama Thakura lives are also such great scholars, then how will the extent of Srila Narottama’s erudition be measured? If you scholars can defeat that betel seller, then you can enter Kheturi, otherwise you should return from here.”

Hearing all this, the teacher turned red with anger and said, “Show me! Where is that betel seller? I will now teach him a good lesson.” That teacher, along with his student, went back to the betel seller and began to debate in Sanskrit and gradually the discussion turned to scriptural conclusions. The other teachers also arrived there. Then, King Narasimha also arrived along with the famous Pandita Rupa Narayana. A crowd of people gathered all around. A fierce scriptural battle started between the two sides in the marketplace. The betel seller began to refute the opinion of the scholars with scriptural statements presented with sweetness and well-reasoned argument. In the end, all the teachers were completely defeated and extremely embarrassed. Their whole bodies were trembling with anger. Taking the teachers along with him, the king returned to his camp.

The crowd of onlookers expressed their astonishment, “All the teachers had come here roaring like lions, but after being defeated in the scriptural battle, they returned like dogs with their tails between their legs. These people were fools. What do they know of the glory of Thakura Mahasaya? Parvatl Devi herself ordered the brahmanas to become the disciples of Thakura Mahasaya. There will be no salvation after committing offences at his feet.”

All this news reached the king by word of mouth. He called Pandita Rupa Narayana and said to him in private, “Brother, what will be the solution now? I had thought that all the teachers would prove Srila Narottama Thakura to be a fool and make a laughing stock of him in front of everyone. Instead, they had to accept defeat from uneducated people, just an ordinary betel seller and potter from Thakura Mahasaya’s village. Not only have the panditas been insulted, but I have also lowered my head in shame.”

Then Pandita Rupa Narayana said to the king, “The truth is that there is no dharma above vaisnava-dharma. There is no offence equal to criticizing a saint (vaisnava-ninda). Now, to become free from the offence we have committed, there is no solution for us other than going to Kheturi, seeking forgiveness from Thakura Mahasaya and taking shelter at his feet. We should go to Kheturi tomorrow with everyone.”

The teachers had fallen into even greater calamity. They were neither able to show their faces to the king nor return to their village. Their condition was like a person on the verge of death waiting for another day.

In contrast, Ramacandra Kaviraja and Ganga Narayana Cakravarti, after distributing their betel leaves and earthen utensils to the poor, returned to Kheturl with great joy and related everything to Srila Thakura Mahasaya.

King Narasimha became extremely anxious to seek the blessings of Thakura Mahasaya. He wondered, “Will Thakura Mahasaya show mercy to such a wicked offender like me?”

In the final portion of the night, the most arrogant of all the teachers saw the goddess Bhagavati Devi (Durga) in his dream. Raising a sword in her hand, she expressed her anger to that arrogant brahmana, “O wicked one! All your study and teaching are in vain. You have criticized a vaisnava. The pain in my heart will only be relieved by cutting your head into pieces. О evil demon! What else should I teach you? If you want protection, then pray to Srila Thakura Mahasaya for forgiveness and take shelter at his feet.”

On awakening from the dream, he narrated this to everyone, while weeping and trembling with fear. As soon as the sun rose, the brahmana told everything to the king. The king asked them all to bathe and get ready. King Narasimha along with all the teachers, on foot, in modest attire and in a humble mood arrived in the courtyard of Thakura Mahasaya’s Sri Gauranga Deva temple and prostrated upon the ground. Srila Thakura Mahasaya was alone at that time, chanting the Holy Names on his rosary and remembering Bhagavan. Ramacandra Kaviraja respectfully gave seats to the king and his retinue. At an opportune moment, he took the king and Pandita Rupa Narayana to Thakura Mahasaya and introduced them. Calling themselves material sense enjoyers and confessing their offences, they repeatedly fell at the feet of Thakura Mahasaya. They begged for forgiveness and for mantra-initiation. The most arrogant teacher approached Thakura Mahasaya and told him about the order of Bhagavati Devi. He prayed piteously again and again for forgiveness. Srila Thakura Mahasaya, who was adosa-darsi, one who never sees the faults of others, graciously embraced that brahmana teacher. Then, the other brahmanas prostrated themselves and rolled in the dust at the feet of Srila Thakura Mahasaya.

Then, everyone participated in the loud congregational singing of the Holy Names of Sri Krsna (maha-sankirtana) in the courtyard of that Gauranga Deva and food (raja-bhoga) was offered to the deity. Sri Santosa Raya (Thakura Mahasaya’s uncle’s son and disciple) gave a befitting welcome to King Narasimha and his retinue. Everyone sat in one long row and partook of the sacred food (prasadam). The next day, Srila Thakura Mahasaya gave mantra-diksa and offered everyone at the feet of Sri Gaurariga. They listened to the recitation of Srimad-Bhagavatam from the holy lotus mouth of Sri Ganga Narayana Cakravarti and music from Govinda Kaviraja. King Narasimha, Pandita Rupa Narayana and the rest of the group returned to their village, but after some time, the king returned to meet with Thakura Mahasaya. This time, his queen Sri Rupamala Devi also came along with him and took mantra-diksa from Srila Thakura Mahasaya. Sri Rupamala used to recite the Hare Krsna maha-mantra one hundred thousand times every day with great determination. In this way, by the grace of Srila Thakura Mahasaya, everyone became vaisnavas.

## The Sri Sarasvati-tosani Commentary on the Upakhyane Upadesa of Prabhupada Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura By Sri Prema Prayojana dasa

Who am I? Why am I suffering? What is the goal of life? How can I attain that goal? These are the pressing questions for which the spiritual aspirant must seek answers. However, in our state of ignorance, we are ill-equipped to understand or recognize the answers even if we were to find them. Our present state of illusion is so profound that we cannot comprehend the depth of our ineligibility. Why is it so? The Vedas describe in scientific detail that the intrinsic nature of every soul is to be a servant of the Absolute Truth, the Supreme Soul, Sri Krsna. As long as we remain averse to the loving service of the Absolute, the spiritual identity of the soul is eclipsed by ignorance \[avidya: a (negation) + vidya (knowledge)\].

In common parlance, ignorance is associated with a state of being uninformed, uneducated or unaware of a subject. Alternatively, ignorance might be associated with knowing about something and yet wilfully neglecting the same. However, in the context of Vedic metaphysics, ignorance (avidya) is a specific function of Sri Krsna’s external material energy. This avidya has two aspects. First, the covering aspect (avaranatmika) conceals all spiritual self-awareness except for the bare sense of subjecthood, “I am.” Secondly, the distracting aspect of avidya (viksepatmika) projects this contextless sense of subjecthood onto the external material elements that comprise the mind-body complex. In this way, the soul, who is an eternal spiritual servant of God, enters a state of identification with the temporary material body. Though the soul is factually beyond worldly dualities such as hunger, thirst, fatigue, distress, and even death, due to misidentification, all his thoughts and activities feverishly revolve around the mitigation of discomfort and the existential struggle for ephemeral security. Thus, a person may be well-informed and highly educated by conventional standards, yet he remains in a state of complete ignorance if he is unaware of his spiritual identity. The engrossment of consciousness in the material identity is so complete that a soul rarely questions its legitimacy, and even if he does, he cannot in any way extricate himself from ignorance by his own effort.

In the Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad (1.3.28) it is stated:

*tamaso mа jyotir gamaya*

**From darkness, lead me to light.**

The Absolute Truth, Sri Krsna, is compared to the sun, and illusion is compared to darkness. Darkness is not a positive entity. It is simply the absence of light. Similarly, the covering aspect (avaranatmika) of avidya is not a positive entity. Rather, it is an absence (abhava). Specifically, it is an absence of Krsna consciousness and of self-awareness. Since the intrinsic nature of the self is to be the loving servant of Sri Krsna, the soul’s telos remains undiscovered until divine love is attained. As long as the attention of the soul remains turned away from the shining sunlight of Sri Krsna, the darkness of avidya covers his identity. And, as long as the soul is afflicted by avidya, this absence lies at the subconscious and pre-intellectual root of all his ruminations. Thus, when the conditioned soul’s capacity to reflect (mantrtva) acts through the subtle external material elements of mind and intelligence, he is predisposed to project the characteristics of his own avidya upon his mind-body complex and the world. Thereby, the notions of changelessness, non-impurity, insentience and independence arise naturally by the action of avidya. According to the logic of atmavan manyate jagat, everyone views the world through the prism of his subjective experience. The nature of avidya provides the conditioned living entity with a particular set of psychological default settings that he finds to be at once normal, obvious, universal and unquestionably certain:

1\)    The conditioned living entity projects a sense of permanence (changelessness) onto his temporary situation, despite the fact that experience shows that all material things come to pass.

2\)    The conditioned living entity feels that his own consciousness is normal and blameless rather than being in a state of utter corruption. He thereby eschews the necessity for reformation, “I am fine. The world is faulty and in need of reform.” This externalization of inner disorder compounds our state of extroversion, thereby depriving us of our inner wealth, the Lord in the heart.

3\)    The notion that the base level of reality is unconscious leads to various shades of atheism and also atheism in the guise of theism.

4\)    The notion that a multiplicity of physical objects exist as separate and independent self-existent entities, not requiring the active will and presence of God as the support for their existence, confines the consciousness to an implacable state of duality.

*anityaiuci-duhkhanatmasu nitya-suci-sukhatma-khyatir avidya*

*Yoga Sutra 2.5*

**Ignorance entails accepting the impermanent as permanent, the impure as pure, the causes of suffering as the causes of happiness and the non-self as the self.**

There is no way to philosophize oneself out of avidya. Spiritual knowledge does not lie somewhere further along the continuum of our present understanding. It is of an entirely different order. Since all the thoughts of a conditioned soul are in fact second-tier intellectual functions proceeding from the basis of the subconscious first-tier platform of avidya, even his sincerest efforts to be impartial remain unconsciously theory-laden and permeated with bias towards mechanistic, impersonal, voidist, or monistic conceptions. In conclusion, without the help of an enlightened spiritual master, no one can deliver himself from ignorance.

*anady-avidya-yuktasya*  
*purusasyatma-vedanam*  
*svato na sambhavad anyas*  
*tattva-jno jnana-do bhavet*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 11.22.10*

**Because a person who is united with beginningless ignorance is not capable of effecting his own self-realization, there must be some other personality who is in factual knowledge of the Absolute Truth and can impart this knowledge to him.**

When the Supreme Lord Sri Krsna is pleased by a disciple’s dedicated service to his spiritual master, He personally dispels the darkness of avidya from the heart of the disciple and takes His seat there. Those who are fortunate enough to meet with a great saint situated in pure love for Sri Krsna have the opportunity to attain the perfection of life. Still, it is seen that among those who meet with a qualified guru, many fail to understand his teaching because the divine words of sri guru are often intercepted, distorted and obstructed by the functions of the intellect based on avidya. Under the circumstances, what are we to do?

*dharmasya tattvam nihitam guhayam*  
*mahajano yena gatah sa panthah*

*Mahabharata, Vana-parva 313.117*

**The truth of religious principles is hidden in the heart of the self-realized saints. Consequently, the sastras confirm, one should follow the path of the great saints (mahajanas).**

**The Ingredient Components of Success**

To receive the divine message in its pristine purity and attain perfection, one must adopt the path of the mahajanas. The disposition and conduct of the saints is completely foreign and incomprehensible to the conditioned souls. Yet the saints prescribe specific components of disposition and conduct that are suitable for conditioned souls to adopt by which they may gradually evolve beyond their present limitations. In this Upakhyane Upadesa, Prabhupada Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura has extracted the essential significance of thirty-four episodes to provide all the ingredient components (upadana) necessary for complete success in spiritual life. In this commentary, sixteen upadanas related to disposition (svabhava), conceptions (vicara) and conduct (acara) have been compiled with reference to their origin within the upakhyanas. The purpose of the commentary is to provide an integrated vision of the entire text and to facilitate the readers’ contemplation and conscious assimilation of the upadanas into their own lives. It is worth noting that all the upadanas are essential. Apathy towards any component will result in a lack of maturity or ripeness in attaining the exalted position of servant of the servant of the Gaudiya saints.

l)    Guru-karana - Subordination to Sri Guru      So-called spiritual activities performed independently without the guidance of the spiritual master will remain on the material platform. It has become fashionable to formally accept a guru but continue to act independently. The person who, in accordance with his own whims, wanders aimlessly here and there, worshipping any deity and reading any literature, cannot become free from manodharma (mental conditioning). By acting independently, one will not become sufficiently humble to overcome the illusory sense of material agency (kartrtva-abhimana) and chant the pure Holy Name. One must hear instructions and receive guidance directly from the lotus mouth of mahanta-guru, a physically present acarya. Even if one receives knowledge from other sources, according to the principle of guru-karana, one must return to Sri Guru and hear it again from him for the knowledge to bear fruit. Books, audio recordings, video and other media, though useful in a supporting role, can never be a substitute for the direct guidance of Sri Guru.

*mahat-krpa vina kona karme ‘bhakti’ naya*  
*krsna-bhakti dure rahu, samsara nahe ksaya*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya-lila 22.51*

**Without the mercy of a pure devotee, transcendental devotion is not present in one’s activities. To say nothing of krsna-bhakti, one cannot even be relieved from the bondage of material existence.**

The importance of guru-karana is powerfully conveyed in the story of Satyakama (Chapter 5) and Arjuna and Ekalavya (Chapter 7).

2\)    Anutapa - Repentance

Repentance is the first samskara of spiritual initiation (diksa). Unless one acknowledges ones errors, recognizes one’s fallen state and adopts the permanent penitent disposition, there is no question of entering the process of diksa. “Alas! I have wasted my life and brought calamity upon myself. I have committed countless transgressions and offences.” Without such genuine contrition, the disciple is bound to fall back into his previous sinful habits. Furthermore, lamentation itself is not sufficient. The penitent must also make a vow to observe the appropriate standards of vaisnava conduct and strictly adhere to them at all cost.      Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu has set the example of expressing remorse:

*durdaivam idrsam ihajani nanuragah*

*Sri Siksastakam, verse 2*

**Alas! Due to offences, my misfortune is such that I have no eagerness for chanting the Holy Name.**

Vivid illustrations of anutapa are found in the story of Bharata (Chapter 18), Ajamila (Chapter 19) and the Avanti brahmana (Chapter 29).

3\)    The power of sadhu-sanga      A miraculous personal transformation is only possible by the association of pure saints. The mercy of the Supreme Lord descends through His devotees. Sadhu-sanga is the root cause of the appearance of bhakti and it remains essential at every stage of our development. The association of advanced saints, being extremely rare and precious, must be eagerly sought out at all times without sectarian considerations.

*krsna-bhakti-janma-mula haya 'sadhu-sanga’*  
*krsna-prema janme, tenho punah mukhya anga*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya-lila 22.83*

**The root cause of devotional service to Sri Krsna is association with advanced devotees. Even when love for Sri Krsna awakens, such association is still the chief component and most essential.**

Sometimes a devotee who has not yet attained the direct vision of the Supreme Lord thinks, “I have had the association of a sadhu many years ago. That is sufficient. I do not need any more.” This notion is found to be quite absurd when we consider that personalities such as the Pracetas (Chapter 17), Prthu Maharaja (Chapter 15), Manigrlva and Nalakuvera, even upon attaining the direct darsana of the Supreme Lord, prayed to Him for the benediction of sadhu-sanga.

The ability of vaisnavas to infuse spiritual potency (sakti-sancara) depends on their level of advancement. The spiritual potency of premi-bhaktas is unlimited. Bestowing their mercy, they can infuse anyone with their own potency, including extremely sinful persons. The bhava-bhaktas can bestow mercy upon the sadhana-bhaktas and infuse their potency in accordance with the individual sadhaka’s eligibility. This implies that bhava-bhaktas, by their own potency, can awaken ruci for Bhagavan in persons whose consciousness is averted from the Lord (bahirmukha) in accordance with their praktana-yogyata (previous eligibility arising from samskaras from the previous life). By their instructions and example, the sadhakas of vaidhi and raganuga-bhakti can cause the appearance of sraddha in persons who are oblivious (bahirmukha) to the Supreme Lord in accordance with their past lives’ impressions.  
According to Srila Jiva Gosvami, the practising devotee develops an attraction to Sri Krsna corresponding to the mood of the vaisnavas with whom he associates:

*tatra prathamam tavat tat-tat-sangaj jdtena tat-tac-*  
*chraddha-tat-tat-parampara-katha-rucy-adinajata-*  
*bhagavat-sammukhyasya tat-tad-anusangenaiva tat-*  
*tad-bhajaniye bhagavad-avirbhava-visese tad-bhajana-marga-visese ca rucir jayate*

*Bhakti Sandarbha, Anuccheda 202*

First of all, by association with pure devotees, one develops a corresponding type of faith in Bhagavan and taste in hearing about the Lord as He is understood in that particular tradition. By this association, ones attention is turned towards Bhagavan. By further association, one develops taste in the particular form of Bhagavan served by those devotees, and also towards their particular path of bhajana.

*tad evam tesam bahu-bhedesu satsu tesam eva*  
*prabhava-taratamyena krpa-taratamyena bhakti-*  
*vasana-taratamyena sat-sangat kala-saighrya-svarupa-*  
*vaisistyabhyam bhaktir udayate*

*Bhakti Sandarbha, Anuccheda 202*

**There are many divisions of sat (pure devotees). By their association, bhakti in a specific mood may manifest quickly or gradually corresponding to the gradations of their proficiency, mercy and devotional mood.**

The powerful effect of sadhu-sanga is beautifully illustrated in the stories of Raikva Muni (Chapter 4), Ajamila (Chapter 19), King Rahugana (Chapter 18), Pracinabarhi (Chapter 16), the hunter Mrgari (Chapter 30), and Harirama and Sri Ramakrsna (Chapter 33).

The danger of not availing oneself of sadhu-sanga is illustrated in the story of King Bharata (Chapter 18) and Saubhari Rsi (Chapter 25).

4\)    The absolute necessity of avoiding offences to vaisnavas

The vaisnavas are extremely dear to Sri Krsna. One must not disrespect them under any circumstances. The material mind, being inherently averse to the service of Sri Krsna, is expert at fabricating justifications for neglecting His devotees. The instructions of pure devotees are always for our benefit. However, due to material attachments, their kindness can be perceived as a threat to our worldly comfort, security and ambitions. Then the mind will create ostensibly plausible reasons to neglect or oppose the Lord’s dear servants. The slightest antagonism towards a saint, whether physical, verbal or even mental, produces a backlash of catastrophic reactions such as a loss of taste for spiritual life, inadvertent philosophical deviations and the awakening of uncontrollable sinful desires.

Those who deliberately avoid sadhu-sanga, inevitably become proud and prone to committing offences. The association of pure saints acts like a protective fence around our tender creeper of devotion, imparting maturity and protecting it from possible attacks by the destructive mad elephant of offences.

Firm impressions for guarding against offences to vaisnavas are received from the stories of Virocana (Chapter 2), Ekalavya (Chapter 7). Duryodhana (Chapter 8), Dhrtarastra (Chapter 9). Daksa (Chapter 13). Ambarisa Maharaja (Chapter 24), the deceitful brahmana (Chapter 32), and Sivananda Bhattacarya (Chapter 33)

5\)    Anugatya - Remaining under guidance

In the spiritual world, the liberated devotees serve Sri Krsna under the guidance of His eternal associates. That state of subordination to one’s spiritual authorities must begin here in this world. It has become fashionable to associate with a teacher for a short time and then, after gaining a little knowledge, set off independently to impart knowledge to others. The problem is that there are levels of eligibility (adhikara) and different instructions are necessary for persons on different levels of eligibility. A person in a lower level of adhikara cannot understand the siddhanta and practices suitable for a higher level. Therefore, he must practice appropriately in accordance with his level until his consciousness is sufficiently purified and expanded to receive the next level of instruction. This process repeats itself forever. Therefore, anugatya, being under guidance, is an eternal intrinsic aspect of pure devotion. Without remaining under the guidance of advanced devotees it will not be possible to receive the next level of inspiration.      Srila A.C. Bhaktivedanta Svami Prabhupada has commented that without advanced association one cannot progress simply by reading books:

**The second-class devotee, even though he cannot support his position with sastric reference, can gradually become a first-class devotee by studying the sastras and associating with a first-class devotee. However, if the second-class devotee does not advance himself by associating with a first-class devotee, he makes no progress.**

**Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya-lila 22.71 purport**

Srila Raghunatha dasa Gosvami has perfectly exemplified the principle of anugatya throughout his life. From childhood, he served his diksa-guru, Sri Yadunandana Acarya. Later, in Jagannatha Puri Dhama, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu placed him under the guidance of Sri Svarupa Damodara Gosvami. After the disappearance of Mahaprabhu and Svarupa Damodara Gosvami, he took shelter of Srila Sanatana Gosvami. Finally, in his advanced stage, he remained externally and internally under the guidance of Srila Rupa Gosvami.

**The process of hearing from higher spiritual authorities must go on for a long time until one becomes mature in realization. Then, in the advanced stage, the result of such hearing is more hearing.**

*srutasya puthsam sucira-sramasya*  
*nanv anjasa suribhir idito ’rthah*  
*tat-tad-gunanusravanam mukunda-*  
*padaravindam hrdayesu yesam*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 3.13.4*

**The goal of a person who makes a great effort for a long time to hear the scriptures from the lips of Sri Guru is to continuously hear about the glories of those in whose hearts Mukunda resides. This goal is praised by the great devotees.**

The principle of receiving progressively higher instruction in accordance with the development of one’s adhikara over a long time is vividly illustrated in the stories of Brahma and Indra (Chapter 2), Prthu (Chapter 15), and the Pracetas (Chapter 17). The consequences of not remaining in higher association are illustrated in the stories of Saubhari Muni (Chapter 25) and Bharata Maharaja (Chapter 18), whose strong sadhana in solitude was not sufficient to protect them from the appearance of material attachments. The principle of siddhanta being presented differently with respect to adhikara is found in the analysis of nama-tattva in the episode of Ajamila (Chapter 19).  
6\)    Vaisnava-seva - Serving the saints

*prabhu kahe, — “vaisnava-seva, nama-sankirtana*  
*dui kara, iighra pabe srt-krsna-carana"*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya-lila 16.7*

**Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu said,“Serve the vaisnavas and always chant the Holy Name. By these two activities, you will very soon attain shelter at Sri Krsna’s lotus feet.”**

Praying for mercy has no value if it comes from the lips but not from the heart. When the soul employs all his abilities and energy sincerely in the service of vaisnavas, only then does he become krpa-prarthi, truly desirous of the grace of Hari, guru and vaisnavas. The perfect example of complete dedication of body, mind, words and all possessions in the service of saints is found in King Prthu (Chapter 15).

7\)    A sadhu is not understood by material vision       In accordance with drg-drsya-vicara, deliberation on the seer and the seen, transcendental entities are not the objects of our material vision. We cannot perceive Sri Krsna, His Holy Name, maha-prasada, or the vaisnavas. But we pray to become the objects of their vision. Until the transcendental entities bestow their mercy and reveal themselves to us, they remain entirely unamenable to any means of investigation. Thus, without the mercy of a sadhu, it is difficult to recognize his exalted position, what to speak of understand his mind or behaviour.

*vajrad api kathorani*  
*mrduni kusumad api*  
*lokottaranam cetamsi*  
*ko nu vijnatum isvarah*

*Sri Caitanya-aritamrta, Madhya-lila 7.73*

**The hearts of those who are situated beyond this mundane plane are sometimes harder than a thunderbolt and sometimes softer than a flower. Who can understand such contradictions in great personalities?**

One should refrain from trying to evaluate the appearance or behaviour of a saint by the standards of worldly experience. This principle is exemplified in the story of Raikva Muni (Chapter 4) and Lord Siva (Chapter 20).

8\)    Insubstantiality of the material world

Faith implies nirbharata, dependence. One who has faith in Adhoksaja, the Supreme Lord, who is beyond material sense perception, depends solely upon Him. Conversely, the faithless invest all their energy into the physical plane, trusting the world to afford them shelter. The materialist walks supported by the ground beneath his feet, whereas the devotee always considers that he is suspended from above by the Lord’s mercy. The flow of time will wash away all material things leaving no trace behind. As such, the world is as insubstantial as a dream and not worthy of our dependence. One’s relationship with Sri Krsna is the only enduring reality. This principle is powerfully expressed through the stories of Naciketah (Chapter 3), Puranjana (Chapter 16), Bharata’s teaching to Rahugana (Chapter 18), Ambarlsa (24), and the spider (Chapter 28).

9\)    Unconditional surrender now

Bhakti is not a business contract. Surrender to Sri Krsna must not be subject to bargaining with terms and conditions. The reward for Sri Krsna’s service, that is, Sri Krsna’s pleasure, is more than sufficient. The difficulties of life are not obstacles on the path of bhakti. Rather, they are golden opportunities to purchase the heart of Sri Krsna by humble service in the face of all adversity. This principle of joyfully accepting all difficulties on the path of loving service is exemplified in the life of Prahlada Maharaja, who was tortured by his father (Chapter 22), and Bali Maharaja, who was apparently mistreated after surrendering to Lord Vamanadeva (Chapter 23). The principle of accepting everything as the mercy of the Lord is exemplified by Citraketu, who happily accepted the curse of Parvatl Devi as the Lord’s mercy (Chapter 20). The best time for unconditional surrender is right now, as shown by Khatvanga Maharaja (Chapter 26).

10\)    Freedom from extraneous desires (anyabhilasa)

Transcendental bhakti is completely devoid of desires other than the desire to serve Sri Krsna. Only transcendental bhakti takes one beyond the darkness of material existence. One becomes free from worldly motives by hearing about the remorse expressed by Dhruva Maharaja, who had initially performed sadhana for the sake of worldly gain (Chapter 14).

11\)    The power of the Holy Name

The Holy Name is non-different from the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Sri Krsna Himself. Playing eternally beyond the jurisdiction of the mind and senses, the Holy Name is omnipotent, independent, self-existent and self-revealing to those who are attentive to service.      As such, for those who have heard the glories of the Holy Name from sri guru, it is an offence to maintain the mundane conception that the Holy Name is a material sound subject to the control of the tongue. The Holy Name is the prime agent (karta) in nama-bhajana. The chanter is just an instrument (yantra) and the Holy Name is the player (yantri). An offender to the Holy Name cannot continuously experience the vision (sphurti) of Sri Krsna’s beauty within his heart.

*varam huta-vaha-jvala-*  
*panjarantar-vyavasthitih*  
*na sauri-cinta-vimukha-*  
*jana-samvasa-vaisasam*

*Katyayana Samhita*

**It is preferable to live in an iron cage in the midst of a blazing fire than to associate with those who are averse to the internal appearance of Sri Krsna, for such association is a great calamity.**

The purport of this verse is that one should strictly avoid the association of those offenders who pick up their chanting beads with the same agent mentality (kartrtva-abhimana) as one who takes up the reins of a horse, and those who make a noise yet do not experience the sweetness of Sri Krsna directly in every syllable of the Holy Name.

*nama cintamanih krsnas*  
*caitanya-rasa-vigrahah*  
*purnah suddho nitya-mukto*  
*’bhinnatvan nama-naminoh*

*Padma Purana*

**The name of Sri Krsna is a wish-fulfilling jewel and the embodiment of all rasas. It is complete, pure, and eternally liberated, since the name and the named are non-different.**

Therefore, one who does not remember the name of Sri Krsna is guilty of the faults of incompleteness (apurnata), impurity (asuddhata), impermanence (anityata), and confinement in the shackles of illusion (srnkhala-baddhata).

Just as fire burns one who is unaware of its burning capacity, the intrinsic power (vastu-sakti) of the Holy Name is not dependent upon the foreknowledge of the chanter. This principle is vividly expressed in the teaching of Yamaraja to Naciketah (Chapter 3) and the history of Ajamila (Chapter 19).

12\)    The necessity of preaching the glories of the Holy Name      One should not think that preaching the glories of the Holy Name is an unnecessary and worthless distraction from one’s own spiritual practice. Srila Sanatana Gosvami has described two duties in relation to the Holy Name - practice and preaching.

*apane acare keha, na kare pracara*  
*pracara karena keha, na karena acara*

*'acara', 'pracara’, — namera karaha 'dui’karya*  
*tumi — sarva-guru, tumi jagatera arya*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Antya-lila 4.102-103*

**Some practice but do not preach the glories of the Holy Name, whereas others preach but do not practice properly. By practicing and preaching, you perform both duties in relation to the Holy Name. Therefore, you are the spiritual master of everyone and the most respected noble saint in the world.**

For the practicing devotee, engagement in preaching the sublime mission of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu is not only beneficial for others, the preacher himself is also greatly benefited. Firstly, he is protected from the powerful waves of sense gratification; and secondly, his devotion becomes mature.

*yare dekha, tare kaha 'krsna’-upadesa*  
*amara ajnaya guru hand tara’ ei desa*

**Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu advised the brahmana Kurma, “Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Sri Krsna as they are given in the Bhagavad-gita and Snmad-Bhagavatam. On My order, become a spiritual master and liberate everyone in this land.”**

*kabhu na badhibe tomara visaya-taranga*  
*punarapi ei thani pabe mora sanga*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya-lila 7.128-129*

**If you follow this instruction, your consciousness will never be obstructed by the waves of sense gratification. Moreover, you will again have my association in this place.**

One may raise the objection, “How can one meditate on the confidential moods of devotion without remaining in solitude?”

In reply, it is stated:

*atra ca bahubhir militva kirtanam sankirtanam ity ucyate*  
*tat tu camatkara-visesa-posatpurvato 'py adhikam iti jneyam*

*Bhakti Sandarbha, Anuccheda 269*

**When many people gather together to sing the glories of Sri Kr$na, it is called sankirtana, congregational chanting. It should be understood that this congregational chanting is even superior to chanting japa or singing alone, because it nourishes the intense astonishment of divine sentiments. One who is still not convinced might raise the objection that when we perform nama-sankirtana in public, we may have to face numerous obstacles such as opposition from sceptics, worship from admirers, and physical exhaustion. There are no such disadvantages in effortlessly performing silent meditation in solitude. So what is the use of performing loud kirtana in public?**

In response it may be said that the joy one feels and the depth of realization from chanting the Holy Name is not dependent upon one’s own endeavour or any material circumstances. Rather, the Holy Name Himself freely bestows mercy upon those with whom He is pleased.

*vicitra-lila-rasa-sagarasya*  
*prabhor vicitrat sphuritat prasadat*  
*vicitra-sankirtana-madhuri sa*  
*na tu sva-yatnad iti sadhu sidhyet*

*Sri Brhad Bhagavatamrta 2.3.168*

**The wonderful and manifold sweetness of sankirtana manifests through the amazing mercy of Sri Krsna, who is an ocean of the astonishing flavours of loving pastimes. This sweetness cannot be achieved by one’s own efforts or abilities. It is impossible for faults or any other obstacles to impede that which is obtained by Bhagavan’s mercy.**

Srila Jiva Gosvami has confirmed that inspiring others to chant the Holy Name leads to one’s own perfection in devotion:

*tad evam kalau nama-kirtana-pracara-prabhavenaiva parama-*  
*bhagavat-parayanatva-siddhir darsita*

*Bhakti Sandarbha, Anuccheda 274*

**Thus, we have shown that in the present age of Kali-yuga the perfection of absorption in loving service to the Supreme Lord is possible only by the influence of preaching the glories of the Holy Name.**

Bharata Maharaja (Chapter 18) and Saubhari Muni (Chapter 25) engaged in their sadhana in solitude. Although both had achieved an advanced stage of practice, they met with obstacles and became entangled in the waves of Maya. This is contrasted with the life history of Maharaja Rantideva, who was moved by the distress of all the fallen souls (paraduhkha-duhkhi). Maya did not disturb Maharaja Rantideva because, without personal consideration, he was ready to sacrifice everything for the spiritual upliftment of others (Chapter 18).

A genuine preacher of the glories of the Holy Name is devoid of the arrogance of thinking that he is the saviour of the world or the reformer of others. Prabhupada Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura clarifies:

**The world stands in no need of any reformer. The world has a very competent person for guiding its minutest happenings. The person who finds that there is scope for reform of the world, himself stands in need of reform. The world goes on in its own perfect way. No person can deflect it by the breadth of a hair from the course chalked out for it by Providence. When we perceive any change being actually effected in the course of events of this world by the agency of any particular individual we also know very well that the agent possesses no real power at any stage. The agent finds himself driven forward by a force belonging to a different category from himself. What is necessary is to change our outlook on this very world. The Scriptures declare that it is only necessary for one to listen with an open mind to the Name of Krsna from the lips of the bona fide devotee. As soon as Krsna enters the listening ear He clears up the vision of the listener so that he has no longer any ambition of even acting the part of a reformer of any other person because he finds that nobody is left without the very Highest Guidance. He is, in fact, only anxious to realise this fact in all its fullness. It is, therefore, his own reform by the Grace of Godhead whose supreme necessity and nature he is increasingly enabled to realise by the eternally continuing mercy of the Supreme Lord. (The Harmonist, May 1932, Vol. XXIX, No. 11)**

13\)    Preaching the Truth is unpopular and inconvenient     Transcendental truth cannot be exploited or monetized. Due to attachment to popularity, prestige, wealth and followers, it is seen that fraudulent persons make a theatrical performance of devotion and present watered-down conclusions meant to cater to the taste and flatter the egos of the ignorant public. Those who attempt to make a business out of spirituality can never touch the eternal reality. Instead, they are cheated blind by Maya just as Ravana was cheated in his attempt to exploit Sita Devi (Chapter ll). Rather than seeking to please the whole world, it is better to seek the favour of our spiritual superiors. This honest sentiment has been expressed by Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami:

*yadi haya ragoddesa, tahan haye avesa,*  
*sahaja vastu na yaya likhana*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya-lila 2.86*

**If I become concerned about the likes and dislikes of others, I cannot possibly write the simple truth.**

Throughout history, all the pure saints who fearlessly spoke the Absolute Truth were attacked and reviled. Therefore, persons who yearn for a life of comfort and convenience have no stomach for the path of devotion to the Supreme Lord. One who is preaching the message of Godhead must face severe opposition. If one’s message is accepted everywhere unopposed, it is certain that no real preaching is taking place. A sycophant is neither a guru nor a preacher. In this regard, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura has commented:

In a world full of duplicity, only duplicitous persons are honoured. Genuine saints, who do not lead the masses along the wrong path, are not respected in this world. In modern times, simply getting cheated by those who mislead the masses through a pretentious form of spiritual discourse has somehow emerged as the religion of the age (yuga-dharma). And because the true saints wish to expose wicked and unholy persons (asadhus), those same unholy cheaters bewilder the masses by condemning the true saints as unholy, duplicitous and thieves themselves in a ploy to secure their escape. The illusory potency of the Lord, Maya, never allows the jivas to be sincere (niskapata) and to that end she has even devised various expert strategies for depriving the jivas of the association of genuine sadhus.

Srila Prabhupadera Upadesamrta

Unless the general populace is liberated from worldly desires and attachments, any spiritual instructions offered will merely bounce off their ears and fail to penetrate their hearts. Hence, it is imperative to engage in loud kirtana everywhere, under the guidance of liberated saints. Through the resounding vibration of the Holy Name, the general public will gradually accumulate the piety (sukrti) to develop confidence in the potency of the Holy Name and faith in the path of pure devotion (suddha-bhakti).

The principle that genuine saints face severe opposition is vividly illustrated by the life histories of Prahlada Maharaja (Chapter 22), the Avanti brahmana (Chapter 29), and Harirama and Sri Ramakrsna Bhattacarya (Chapter 33). Although it is true that those favoured by the Lord find their paths beset by thorns, the inconceivable mercy of the Lord turns all their tribulations into ineffable joy.

14\)    Non-cooperation with offenders      The Supreme Lord bestows His mercy upon those who cannot tolerate disrespect towards His dear devotees. By defending the honour of those who are dear to Sri Krsna, one becomes blessed with great fortune. It is not a sign of humility and tolerance to remain peaceful when the Lord and His devotees are blasphemed. Rather, one becomes implicated simply by hearing such inauspicious statements.

*nindath bhagavatah srnvams*  
*tat-parasya janasya va*  
*tato napaiti yah so ’pi*  
*yaty adhah sukrtac cyutah*

*Srimad-Bhagavatam 10.74.40*

**Anyone who fails to immediately leave the place where he hears criticism of the Supreme Lord or His faithful devotee will certainly fall down, bereft of his pious credit.**

Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja rebuked his own brother when he disrespected Sri Mlnaketana Ramadasa. By refusing to appease an offender, he received the mercy of Sri Nityananda Prabhu and left home at once for Vrndavana.

*bhaike bhartsinu muni, land ei guna*  
*sei ratre prabhu more dila darasana*

*Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Adi-lila 5.180*

**That night, Lord Nityananda appeared to me in a dream simply because of my virtue in rebuking my brother.**

One of the most striking examples of non-cooperation with an offensive person is found in the life of Sati. She relinquished her body due to its association with Daksa, who had criticized Lord Siva (Chapter 13).

15\)    Simplicity

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu has distributed the treasure of pure love for Sri Krsna without considering who is qualified or unqualified. There are only three types of duplicitous persons or cheaters who are excluded from this spiritual path of devotion to God (bhagavata-dharma): (l) dhana-kapati, (2) bala-kapati, and (3) prema-kapati.

After hearing about bhagavata-dharma, some persons do not utilize their wealth with affection in the service of Sri Guru, Sri Krsna, and vaisnavas. They are in the habit of investing huge amounts of wealth in worldly projects hoping to make more profit, yet they fear to expend relatively small amounts in the service of Sri Krsna, sri guru, and vaisnavas. Such persons are called dhana-kapati, financial cheaters.
