Although they have strength, those who remain in a state of inertia and do not utilize their energy in activities related to Sri Krsna and the vaisnavas, who are lazy and reluctant to participate by dancing enthusiastically in the sankirtana of Sri Krsna Caitanya, are bala-kapati, cheaters in the matter of personal vigour.

Finally, those who do not have prema, but are devoid of the slightest anxiety to attain it, or those who proudly think, “I am a devotee!” are called prema-kapatis, devotional frauds. Prema is attained by unequivocally abandoning all duplicity and repeatedly bathing in the mercy of great devotees.

Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura has commented:

Simple-heartedness is synonymous with vaisnavism. Servants of paramahamsa vaisnavas should be simple hearted, a quality which makes them the topmost brahmanas. Life as an animal, bird, insect, or any other of the countless thousands of species is acceptable, but taking shelter of deceit is thoroughly improper. Only an honest person possesses real auspiciousness.

Fine examples of simplicity are found in the lives of Satyakama (Chapter 5), Upamanyu (Chapter 6), Dhruva (Chapter 14), and Harirama and Sri Ramakrsna Bhattacarya (Chapter 33).

16\) Humility      Bhakti is not a material sentiment. It is a special manifestation of Sr! Krsna’s internal potency. The wonderful speciality of bhakti is that when it appears in the heart of a devotee, it has the power to melt the heart of the Supreme Lord, Sri Krsna. Furthermore, when this potency comes in contact with the devotee’s attitude of humility, bhakti begins to surge like a powerful tidal wave and becomes all the more beautiful.

*dainya-sambandhena ca sadhikam ucchalita bhavattti*  
*tatra tad-adhikyam*

*Bhakti Sandarbha, Anuccheda 180*

As long as we try to approach Sri Krsna with pride in our material knowledge, wealth and status, Sri Krsna will remain far away. But if we call out to Sri Krsna with a humble heart, Sri Krsna will surely give us His darsana and immerse us in the ocean of bliss.

*ahankara nivrttanam*  
*kesavo na hi duragah*  
*ahankara-yutanam hi*  
*madhye parvata-rasayah*

*Brahma-vaivarta Purana 3.9.9*

**For those who are devoid of ego, Sri Kesava is not at all distant. But, for those who are full of ego, mountain ranges stand between them and Bhagavan.**

Sri Vallabhacarya has stated:

*na hi sadhana-sampattya*  
*harih tusyati karmavat*  
*bhaktanam dainyam evaikam*  
*hari tosana sadhanam*

*Sri Vallabhacarya, Subodhim*

**Lord Hari is not satisfied with a mere abundance of spiritual practice (sadhana), as if quantity were relevant, as it is in the performance of reward-seeking activities (karma). Lord Hari is only pleased with the humility of the devotees.**

The saying ‘birds of a feather flock together’ holds true, as everyone naturally gravitates towards the company of like-minded individuals. Despite His unlimited glories, the Supreme Lord Himself is a paragon of humility. Therefore, He only relishes the company of His humble devotees.

An example of perfect tolerance and humility is found in the life of the mendicant of Avanti City (Chapter 29). The Supreme Lord’s own humility is evident from His sweet dealings with Bhrgu Muni (Chapter 27).

In conclusion, the devotee should live in a constant state of prostration before his divine transcendental authorities, sri guru and the vaisnavas, while engaging at every moment in their service. One who is humble will realize the secret that absorption in service and the mercy of the Lord are not two things. They are one and the same.

May our hearts be decorated with the fragrant garland of these sixteen upadanas by which all spiritual desires are fulfilled.

1\)    Guru-karana - Subordination to Sri Guru

2\)    Anutapa - Repentance

3\)    The power of sadhu-sanga

4\)    The absolute necessity of avoiding offences to vaisnavas

5\)    Anugatya - Remaining under guidance

6\)    Vaisnava-seva - Serving the saints

7\)    A sadhu is not understood by material vision

8\)    Insubstantiality of the material world

9\)    Unconditional surrender now

10\)    Freedom from extraneous desires (anyabhilasa)

11\)    The power of the Holy Name

12\)    The necessity of preaching the glories of the Holy Name

13\)    Preaching the Truth is unpopular and inconvenient

14\)    Non-cooperation with offenders

15\)    Simplicity

16\)    Humility

Thus ends the Sri Sarasvati-tosani Commentary on the Upakhyane Upadesa of Prabhupada Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura.
